===== arc --help =====
Persistent context and guarded workflow state over plain Git for agentic coding arcs.

Reconstruct project and workflow context across sessions and harnesses: work in progress, decisions, next steps, and what is safe to integrate. Git owns content and history; arc owns the journal, change ledger, review findings, verification evidence, and guarded integration.

`--version` prints the release on its first line and the compiled build as `build: <commit> (clean|dirty)`, or `build: unknown`, on a second; `-V` prints the release line alone.

Usage: arc [OPTIONS] [COMMAND]

Commands:
  guide           Read indexed workflow topics or search instruction text without project state
  begin           Open a change: create (or adopt) its branch and record its checkout
  roles           Record explicit planning and orchestration assertions on an open change
  trailers        Print a change's portable trailer block in convention order
  list            List changes
  query           Filter changes and print matching IDs (or JSON)
  show            Render one change with offline forge reconstruction, freshness, custody availability and review drift (Markdown, or full state with --json)
  explain         What one change knew and what it was accepted on, from the ledger and the journal
  log             Print recorded facts and offline forge reconstruction with freshness, custody availability and review drift
  gates           Declared gates and their measured cost across changes
  stats           Derived ledger analytics: stage, review, and gate durations, each landing's round trip, and how often provenance is recorded
  diff            Render a recorded patchset using Git's native diff output
  findings        List findings in text, JSON, or SARIF 2.1.0 form
  brief           Record or read a change-scoped implementation contract
  changelog       Record, read, or project changelog entries
  message         Append a structured cross-change announcement (never policy input)
  messages        Scan messages across open and closed changes (newest first)
  inbox           Lead-facing queue rollup: open changes, active claims, and outstanding debt (arc-inbox/11 schema)
  chain           Show a tagged program in dependency order (arc-chain/5 schema)
  take            Atomically claim the highest-priority ready change
  metadata        Append dependency, tag, or assignment metadata to an open change
  iterating       Declare that this change is being iterated on, or clear the declaration
  status          Machine-readable status report (the versioned arc-status/27 schema)
  blocker-status  Report whether declared prerequisite changes have integrated
  is-blocked      Dependency probe: exit 0 ready, 1 blocked, 2 on lookup/ledger errors
  events          Replay raw ledger events as NDJSON, optionally following new events
  watch           Wait for a change, or a tagged series, to reach a ledger-derived condition
  export          Export one change as a deterministic, versioned JSON bundle
  import          Import arc-bundle/6 or /5 into this repository's local store
  accept          Prepare a delivered change on the paired integration authority (lead only)
  replica         Pair independent stores and move integration authority with files
  check           Integration preflight under the resolved target shape or --shape; exit code identifies the first blocker
  claim           Acquire or renew an advisory executor claim on a change or a journal artifact
  release-claim   Release the advisory executor claim on a change or a journal artifact
  stage           Record typed executor progress (requires an owned live claim)
  squash          Rewrite a change as one commit on its base, with the same tree, and record it as a new patchset
  snapshot        Record the current branch head as a patchset with explicit base and brief binding
  keep            Keep a fact this work discovered, so `arc resume` hands it back to a compacted or cold session instead of it being re-derived
  comment         Add a discussion comment
  finding         Record a standalone review finding
  reply           Reply to a comment or finding event
  resolve         Record a finding disposition (supersedes current tips automatically)
  review          Read review state, or record a verdict with an optional findings batch
  receipt         Review receipts: what a delegated reader read and concluded, in one file arc records
  external        Record a review decision made outside the local ledger
  publication     Require publication gates at a local target branch's current head
  verify          Run a declared gate (or ad hoc command) and record the evidence
  done            Finish implementation: snapshot, verify all gates, then print check state
  rebase          Replay a change's branch onto its target, then snapshot the new head
  env             Print shell exports for a detected harness session, for `eval`: `eval "$(arc env)"`
  completions     Print a shell completion script to stdout
  mangen          Render the man page (arc.1) into a directory
  resume          Resume one change with its brief, live state, and journal context
  rescue          Recover work abandoned by another session
  prompt          Print one stable statusline summary for the current change
  slots           Leased fixed-path checkouts kept across changes, declared by [slots] in .arc/worktrees.toml (see arc guide slots)
  fork            Fork this repository: a worktree on a fork/<slug> branch, outside the change lifecycle
  hold            Set an integration hold
  release-hold    Release one hold by the event that set it (a unique prefix is enough)
  integrate       Explicit --provisional prepares one unsigned no-ff merge into an unchecked staging ref under refs/heads/arc/provisional/<local-replica>/<recorded-target>
  land            Land several changes on their target in one guarded transition
  debt            Record a review obligation this change carries but has not discharged
  audit           Record a review performed after integration (never a late verdict)
  close           Close a change without arc performing the merge
  history         Record or withdraw rewrite maps, and resolve recorded revisions
  rewrite         Rewrite this repository's history, carrying every recorded revision forward
  candidate       Register, show, list, judge, and retire candidates: alternative answers to a brief, recorded without opening a change
  context         Record what a change or a candidate read, declared, and captured: read records from a tool's record, attributed declarations, and capture reports
  pass            Record and list caller-declared review passes
  checkpoint      Name a revision somebody is testing, with the changes and todos open at it, and read what landed, closed or was filed since
  run             Record delegated run dispatches, the backend sessions that executed them, and their terminal outcomes
  cost            Report what the executor sessions bound to a change's or a journal artifact's runs consumed, retain the facts the report rests on, and export the evidence its sessions are divided by
  review-ladder   Import or inspect the repository's dated review capability registry
  policy          Read or write policy kept with this repository's local Arc ledger
  forge           Record local forge facts without contacting providers
  config          Show resolved paths and style values with sources as arc-config/1 JSON
  sandbox         Make, compare, and remove a disposable copy of this project under a sandbox prefix
  doctor          Check the append-only ledger for malformed or stale state (read-only)
  hooks           Manage the opt-in Git hook pack (never installed automatically)
  instructions    Print a portable convention specification, independent of arc state
  workspace       Aggregate changes, inboxes, or backlog across every known project, and retire or restore a project's registration
  restack         Advise (never execute) rebases for open dependents of a change
  catchup         Orient a session: ledger queue, journal backlog, live lanes, and recent sessions across harnesses
  fr              File a feature request in the project journal: the discoverable alias for `arc journal note --kind feature-request`
  journal         Cross-harness project and operator journals over plain Markdown
  help            Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

  -V, --version
          Print version

Run `arc` with no arguments for the capability map; `arc guide` for indexed depth, or `arc catchup` for live project state.

ARC_TRACE=1 writes one arc-perf/1 record of the command's work to stderr at exit; see `arc guide identity-options`.

===== arc guide --help =====
Read indexed workflow topics or search instruction text without project state

With no topic, list topic and command-help routes. A topic exceeding 16,384 bytes lists its exact child routes; --all prints the complete guide. --search performs literal, case-insensitive search over topics and command help, showing each matching route once with compact context. Unknown topics refuse naming `arc guide`. Reads work outside a repository, under every role and with broken configuration, recording nothing.

Usage: arc guide [OPTIONS] [TOPIC]

Arguments:
  [TOPIC]
          Topic from `arc guide`, including a listed child route

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --all
          Print the complete workflow guide

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --search <TEXT>
          Search topics and command help literally, ignoring case

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc begin --help =====
Open a change: create (or adopt) its branch and record its checkout

The opening event records session-store resolution. An unreadable Claude subagent directory or child ending records an unresolved session with the read failure and names no resolved model. Recordings and child metadata must be regular files; FIFOs are refused without waiting for writers. A plugin child's ending must name the parent's native session; a foreign or absent identity records an unresolved session with the identity failure and no resolved model.

New branches use [change] checkout (built-in: in-place), overridden by --worktree or --no-worktree. --adopt records the branch's existing worktree; it switches this checkout only with explicit --no-worktree and never creates a worktree.

Worktree ownership binds to the allocated Git administrative directory before checkout hooks run. If the registered path changes identity, the change opens without creation authority and all worktrees are kept. Worktree post-checkout receives Git's creation arguments (null old revision, HEAD, 1). Population or hook failure retains the branch and worktree, removes the unrecorded owner token when its identity matches, and reports adoption and discard commands without opening a change. Adoption recovery preserves the opening options and resolved target and base, attaching option values as --flag=<shell-quoted value> to preserve leading hyphens. Checkout selection follows the retained branch. Optional .arc/worktrees.toml preparation runs after population. A project can combine a fixed --worktree path with path-stable build storage; compiler freshness remains the project's responsibility. --slot leases a checkout of the declared [slots] pool instead: the best-ranked free slot is switched to a new branch at the target, its retained build kept unless the toolchain identity changed. A slot that is not owned, clean, detached and free of unlisted ignored paths refuses and keeps its lease; an exhausted pool refuses with its table (arc guide slots).

Without --base, the change derives its base from the local target: its head, or under --adopt the adopted branch's merge base with it. When that target lacks commits its configured upstream holds, as the last fetch left the remote-tracking ref (arc reads local refs only, never the network), the change still opens and stderr warns, naming both revisions, the base the change took, and the fix: for a target strictly behind, the fast-forward (`git merge --ff-only` in the checkout holding it, or `git fetch . <upstream>:<target>` when none does); for a diverged one, that no fast-forward exists; then `arc rebase` onto the moved target. A target level with or ahead of its upstream, or tracking none, gets no warning.

Usage: arc begin [OPTIONS] <SLUG>

Arguments:
  <SLUG>
          Kebab-case slug naming the outcome (also the ID prefix)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --planned-by [<PLANNED_BY>]
          Assert a planner: harness:model[#effort] or Name <email>; repeatable. Without a value, the invoking identity: a declared Name <email> actor's own form, else the resolved harness:model under a declared or session-derived actor. Empty segments, unresolved coordinates, placeholders and session ids refuse. Put the change first when omitting the value; values naming a repository change refuse. Use --planned-by=<value> for an explicit coordinate

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --orchestrated-by [<ORCHESTRATED_BY>]
          Assert an orchestrator in the same form; repeatable. Without a value, the invoking identity, as for --planned-by. Put the change first when omitting the value; values naming a repository change refuse. Use --orchestrated-by=<value> for an explicit coordinate

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --title <TITLE>
          Human title (defaults to the slug with spaces)

      --profile <PROFILE>
          Workflow profile: direct | local | forge | release
          
          [default: local]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --target <TARGET>
          Integration target branch (defaults to the current branch)

      --base <BASE>
          Base revision (defaults to the target head, or under --adopt to the adopted branch's merge base with the target). An explicit base skips the comparison of the target with its upstream

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --branch <BRANCH>
          Branch name (defaults to [change] branch_prefix followed by the slug; built-in arc/)

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --worktree [<WORKTREE>]
          Force a worktree for a new branch, optionally at this path. Without a path, use worktrees_dir/<repo>-<slug>

      --no-worktree
          Switch this checkout to the change branch, carrying local work. Create the branch unless --adopt is supplied. Refuse overwrite conflicts, including ignored files, or a branch checked out elsewhere without opening a change

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

      --slot
          Lease a slot of the [slots] pool in .arc/worktrees.toml and open the change's branch in its fixed checkout (see arc guide slots). [slots] default = true makes this the default for worktree checkouts

      --adopt <ADOPT>
          Track an existing branch instead of creating one

      --blocked-by <BLOCKED_BY>
          Change that must integrate before this one is ready (repeatable)

      --tag <TAG>
          Batch/query tag (repeatable)

      --from-journal <FROM_JOURNAL>
          Open from an actionable journal artifact, consuming it. An incomplete transition successor is refused before opening a change. A bare filename resolves in this project's journal; `<journal-dir>::<file>` resolves `<file>` in the journal at the absolute `<journal-dir>` that project's `arc journal dir` prints, and records the promotion in that journal, naming this repository and change. A journal that is not one, or a file it does not hold, is refused naming both; a filename this project's journal does not hold, naming each other known journal that holds it; a path, naming the reference that resolves it. The change records the reference as given and the digest of its body read now, and each later snapshot links it `via: begin` unless given --journal-ref

      --from-fork <FROM_FORK>
          Open by promoting a fork's work onto a new branch, recording the fork slug and the source base, head, and tree. Recovery adoption requires an explicit --base. The fork keeps its branch, worktree, and marker; no review credit crosses the link

      --dangerous
          Require an independent verdict whatever this change turns out to touch. One-way: nothing lowers it afterwards

      --iterating
          Open the change declaring that integration is not yet the goal

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc roles --help =====
Record explicit planning and orchestration assertions on an open change.

Values follow arc-contribution-trailers/2; ancestry grants no credit.

Usage: arc roles [OPTIONS] <CHANGE>

Arguments:

          Open change whose contribution assertions to record

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

Without a value, the invoking identity: a declared Name <email> actor's own form, else the resolved harness:model under a declared or session-derived actor. Empty segments, unresolved coordinates, placeholders and session ids refuse. Put the change first when omitting the value; values naming a repository change refuse. Use --planned-by=<value> for an explicit coordinate

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --orchestrated-by [<ORCHESTRATED_BY>]
          Assert an orchestrator (repeatable harness:model[#effort] or Name <email>). Without a value, the invoking identity, as for --planned-by. Put the change first when omitting the value; values naming a repository change refuse. Use --orchestrated-by=<value> for an explicit coordinate

===== arc trailers --help =====
Print a change's portable trailer block in convention order.

Planning and orchestration come from assertions, implementation from recorded patchset contributors, or the snapshot subject when none are listed. A subject that is a fresh harness:model[#effort] or Name <email> supplies itself. Retained replay or bound-run author coordinates take precedence over session names. The recorder's harness and model apply only to the recorder's own work on a snapshot that declared its attribution; a recorder inferred from its harness session or Git configuration is not credited. No line carries a placeholder or a session id: what cannot be attributed is omitted and named in a note with its reason and remedy. Review credit stays in the ledger; Reviewed-by is never projected.

Usage: arc trailers [OPTIONS] <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>
          Change whose attributed contribution trailers to print

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --json
          Emit arc-trailers/1 JSON with trailers and attribution notes

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

Unreadable changes are named with their errors on stderr; readable changes remain in the output

===== arc query --help =====
Filter changes and print matching IDs (or JSON)

Unreadable changes are named with their errors on stderr; readable changes remain in the output. With --assurance the output is the dated review assurance of each integrated change instead

Usage: arc query [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --status <STATUS>
          open | closed | integrated | abandoned | superseded

      --target <TARGET>
          Only changes whose integration target is this branch

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --tag <TAG>
          Only changes carrying every tag given (repeatable)

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --verdict <VERDICT>
          Only changes whose latest verdict is this
          
          [possible values: approved, changes-requested, comment-only]

      --actor <ACTOR>
          Only changes opened by this actor; filters only when explicitly typed

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Only changes opened from this harness; filters only when explicitly typed

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --commit <COMMIT>
          Report changes whose patchset, integration, or closure commit matches this revision (unique prefix accepted). Each change whose state cannot be read is named with its error on stderr, and a closing line names them all: a match beside one may not be unique, and no match beside one is not a no-match. With --json it prints the rows `query --json` prints, `[]` when nothing matches

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --forge <FORGE>
          Recorded identities and explicitly related acceptance/source changes, including superseded links: PROVIDER:HOST/OWNER/REPO#NUMBER

      --debt
          Only changes that integrated owing a review nobody has recorded yet

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

      --provisional
          Only changes whose gating approval was recorded as owed corroboration and has not received it — from an independent approval of the same patchset, or from an audit

      --assurance
          One row per integrated change: the reads it received (model, effort, verdict or audit, time, route version) and where its debt stands. Reads are derived from recorded verdict and audit events; nothing is written and no tier, price or score is ever stored. A replayed landing's verdicts cover only the original edit and never count, regardless of later debt declarations; audits read the landed range. Narrow with --target, --tag, --actor or --harness

      --stale
          With --assurance: ask agent-routing now whether a stronger eligible reviewer than every read a change holds can be seated, and list each change that is not known fresh, stale work first. A read counts only when routing assesses it eligible; a change with no eligible read is owed, and one routing cannot place is unknown with a reason, never fresh. Each row shows the frontier gap, the share of its added lines still live at HEAD (git blame, cached once per change and reused until a commit touches a path it read), exposure to the policy's dangerous paths, and the age of its newest read; stale rows order by those, in that order. A change with no live lines is listed as no-live-lines, never stale. Staleness gates nothing

      --json
          Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text (`arc-assurance/1` with --assurance)

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc show --help =====
Render one change with offline forge reconstruction, freshness, custody availability and review drift (Markdown, or full state with --json)

Forge views preserve provider-observed SHAs beside nullable local rewrite successors; observed and resolved head matches compare with the patchset's approval anchor, not a content or trailer rewrite successor. Forge diagnostics retain conflicting or invalid observations and their unresolved projections without blocking the change's state read. Closed head drift compares with the closure's source patchset when recorded, otherwise with the newest snapshot Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails A declared review debt reads `Declared, not yet integrated` until the change integrates, then `Owed`, then `Owed (discharged)` with its discharger; JSON carries the same as `debt.standing` Recorded producer models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded. JSON production identities and review coverage retain raw values with model_recorded: false; valid models omit the flag. Audit and verdict detail text validates models against their recorded sessions. A read recorded from a receipt names the receipt item's key and the file's digest, and each finding disposition it published inside its own event. With a registry file, bindings and author facts appear as `review_context`; see `arc guide debt-clearance`.

Usage: arc show [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --tag <TAG>
          Render every change carrying this tag instead of one change

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --json
          Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text

      --at <AT>
          Replay state as of this event ID ("what did the actor see?")

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>

===== arc explain --help =====
What one change knew and what it was accepted on, from the ledger and the journal.

Writes nothing: no event, no ref, no journal entry, no lock.

Eight slots, always all printed: contract (the brief in force, its planner credit, plan, base revision, and causes, and the selection and promotion behind each patchset a candidate promotion recorded), supplied context (the journal artifact the change was opened from, and every patchset's journal references with the operation that supplied each, `begin`, `brief`, `flag`, or `unrecorded`, and its thread), declared facts (every kept fact, with the events it cites), observed reads, rejected alternatives (kept facts of kind `rejected`, and each registration answering the same brief version as a promoted candidate, `declared` with its judgements or `inferred` while unjudged; judgements are read as they stand now, whatever `--at` names), evaluation (each counted gate's evidence event, tree, environment digest, timeout, reuse, and falsification), coverage at acceptance (the verdicts, waiver, and debt the integration event's authorization recorded), and later knowledge (audits, audit findings and dispositions, review findings' post-integration dispositions, debts, debt discharges, and verification receipts recorded after the integration).

Every row carries a standing: `recorded` (an event records it), `declared` (somebody stated it and arc did not check it), `inferred` (arc derived it from other records; no event states it), `absent` (nothing records it), or `unavailable` (its source cannot be read now). Every standing but `recorded` gives its reason, and an empty slot prints its absence and why.

A journal reference is resolved against the journal now, hot or cold: `same` when the body digest equals the recorded one, `amended` with both digests when not, `missing` when the name resolves to nothing. The artifact a change was opened from resolves the same way against the digest its opening recorded, and is `recorded`; a change opened before arc recorded that digest keeps it `declared`, with its digest shown as `current`. A kept fact's citations name records; the fact stays `declared`.

A counted pass's falsification lists a declared one (with its predicted reason) and the failure arc inferred it follows (standing `inferred`, with the failing event, its revision, and the rule that derived it) as separate entries, or `none` when neither was recorded.

A debt declared on a change that has not integrated appears as a `debt` row with `debt_standing` `declared`; after integration its standing is `owed` or `discharged`, and a discharge after the integration also names `discharged_later_by`.

A discharge by a later review that requests changes reads `fulfilled, not approved`. Coverage at acceptance never takes later knowledge into its values; it only points at the discharge. Recorded producer models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded. JSON production identities, review coverage, and audit and verdict details retain raw model values with model_recorded: false when missing, placeholders or session ids; valid models omit the flag. Audit and verdict details validate models against their recorded sessions. Forge content follows change knowledge; repository and rewrite frontiers and current custody availability remain separately named reads

Usage: arc explain [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --at <AT>
          Bound the view by this event ID on the change. For an integrated change, supplied context, declared facts, rejected alternatives, and later knowledge show only what was recorded by the event, while contract, evaluation, and coverage at acceptance keep their integration basis; the integration event itself, or any earlier one, leaves later knowledge empty. For a change not integrated it replays the change as of the event, as `arc show --at` does. An event not on the change is refused

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --json
          Emit `arc-explain/2` JSON instead of text

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc stats --help =====
Derived ledger analytics: stage, review, and gate durations, each landing's round trip, and how often provenance is recorded

Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Gate costs use command `run_ms`, falling back to `duration_ms` for older events.

Usage: arc stats [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --change <change_flag>
          Report a single change

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --tag <TAG>
          Report every change carrying this tag

      --all
          Report all changes (the default)

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --by-model
          One row per delegated identity instead of per change: patchsets contributed, rework rounds they opened, verdicts issued

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --provenance
          How often each provenance record was written, one line per class, each count beside the population it was counted in. With `--json`, `arc-stats-provenance/1`.
          
          Falsification: passing non-probe `verification-recorded` events carrying a declared `falsification`, only `falsification_inferred`, or neither; and, of neither, those an earlier failure of the same gate (or command, when unnamed) on the same change precedes, which were recorded before arc derived the inference.
          
          Journal refs: patchsets carrying `journal_refs`, of all patchsets, of those on changes opened from a journal artifact, and of those on changes opened without one; and every reference by its `via` (begin, brief, flag, or unrecorded).
          
          Rejected alternatives: integrated changes with at least one kept fact of kind `rejected`, of integrated changes.
          
          Plan-linked briefs: brief versions carrying a `plan_ref`, of all versions; and changes whose in-force brief carries one, of the selected changes.
          
          Cited kept facts: kept facts citing at least one event, of all kept facts.
          
          Read records: changes with at least one `context-read` on their ledger, of the selected changes.

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --round-trip
          Each integrated change's final landing, split into phases, with the median, the worst, and the five slowest. With `--json`, `arc-stats-round-trip/1`. A change nothing integrated has no row.
          
          The round trip runs from the `patchset-added` event of the patchset the last integration event names (or, on a legacy close, the last one before it) to that integration event. A patchset `arc rebase` recorded carries `rebase_started_at`, and the window then opens there instead. Rebase: from `rebase_started_at` to the patchset; null when the patchset records none. Gates: the union of every arc-observed `verification-recorded` run after the patchset, each spanning its `run_ms` (or legacy `duration_ms`) back from its event and clamped to the time before integration, so concurrent runs count once; per gate, each gate's own run time. Integration: from the `started_at` the integration event records (`integration_start: recorded`), or without one from the last event before it (`last-event`), to the integration event. Waiting: the remainder, so the phases sum to the round trip. Reuse: `verification-reused` events inside the window, counted and never timed. Rebased: the patchset's base differs from the base recorded before it. Slot: the pool slot the change leased before its integration (`slot`, null for an ordinary checkout), so a landing in a kept, warm checkout reads apart from a fresh one.
          
          Not recorded, so not reported: how long a rebase that stopped on a conflict took, and build versus test inside one gate command. Attested evidence carries no observed duration and counts as waiting.

      --json
          Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc brief --help =====
Record or read a change-scoped implementation contract

Probe edits without --body-file or --scaffold require a preceding brief and preserve its body and omitted title, plan provenance, and mandatory reads. Explicit metadata replaces the corresponding fields. A body or scaffold records a replacement contract. Forbidden planner coordinates grant no credit in text or JSON reads.

Usage: arc brief [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --planned-by [<PLANNED_BY>]
          Assert a planner on the change when writing a brief (repeatable). Value: harness:model[#effort] or Name <email>. Without a value, the invoking identity, as for `arc roles --planned-by`. Empty segments, unresolved coordinates, placeholders and session ids refuse. Put the change first when omitting the value; values naming a repository change refuse. Use --planned-by=<value> for an explicit coordinate

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Read a new brief body from a file ('-' for stdin). A write records the next version: v1 on a change with no brief, otherwise one past the latest

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --title <TITLE>
          Optional title for a newly recorded brief

      --base <BASE>
          Revision whose source and premises this brief was checked against. Defaults to the previous brief's base, or HEAD for the first brief

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --version <VERSION>
          Read one derived brief version instead of the latest

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --scaffold <SCAFFOLD>
          Scaffold template around the body: .arc/templates/<name>.md, or a built-in (sol-low, sol-high, reviewer, discussion). The body takes the place of the template's `{{body}}` line, or follows a template without one

      --plan-ref <PLAN_REF>
          Journal plan artifact implemented by this brief: a filename in this project's journal, or `<journal-dir>::<file>` for a plan in the journal at the absolute `<journal-dir>` another project's `arc journal dir` prints. The brief records the reference as given; a plan in another journal is read there, and that journal records the brief as a promotion naming this repository and change. A filename this project's journal does not hold is refused naming each other known journal that holds it; a path, naming the reference that resolves it

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --plan-slice <PLAN_SLICE>
          Opaque plan slice slug implemented by this brief

      --probes-json <PROBES_JSON>
          Named acceptance probes bound to this brief: a JSON array inline, a path to one, or '-' for stdin. Replaces the complete probe array; without a body or scaffold, preserves the preceding contract

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

      --probe <PROBE>
          Add or update a named acceptance probe (repeatable NAME=COMMAND). Other probes carry forward from the previous brief. Mutually exclusive with --probes-json. Without a body or scaffold, preserves the preceding contract

      --withdraw-probe <WITHDRAW_PROBE>
          Withdraw a named probe (repeatable); other probes carry forward. Unknown names are refused. Mutually exclusive with --probes-json. Without a body or scaffold, preserves the preceding contract

      --caused-by <CAUSED_BY>
          Earlier ledger fact that caused this version (repeatable): finding:<id>, verdict:<event> naming a changes-requested verdict, blocked-on:<event>, or forge:<observation>:<generation>, each on this change. v1 refuses a cause; every later version requires at least one, from this flag or --cause-note

      --cause-note <CAUSE_NOTE>
          External cause, when no earlier ledger object represents the reason. It is a cause like --caused-by: refused on v1, and enough alone for any later version

      --must-read <MUST_READ>
          A read the contract requires of whoever answers it (repeatable): a journal artifact, `<file>` or `<journal-dir>::<file>`, at `@sha256:<hex>` or at the body digest read now; or a file at a revision, `<revision>:<path>[:<from>-<to>]`, recorded as its blob and the one-based inclusive line range, whole when none is given. A locator that resolves to nothing is refused, an artifact filename this project's journal does not hold naming each other known journal that holds it, and a path to an artifact naming the reference that resolves it. `arc candidate select` meets a requirement only with a tool read at that version covering that extent. An artifact requirement is met by digest equality with the text a tool returned, so a tool that decorates what it returns, like a line-numbered `Read`, never meets one

      --json
          Emit the versioned structured brief projection

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc changelog --help =====
Record, read, or project changelog entries

While an open change has no changelog record, `arc status` and `arc check` advise one under the code `no-changelog-entry`, and `arc integrate` prints that advice once it has merged such a change; the advice never blocks. Recording an entry, or recording with --none that the change needs none, answers it. The latest record wins, and a --none record projects nothing.

A repository declares that it keeps no changelog with `none = "<reason>"` in `.arc/changelog.toml`. The reason must be non-empty and not a placeholder. `none` conflicts with explicit target, renderer, renderer_command, and renderer_timeout keys. The invoking checkout's declaration silences the advisory, including integration advice, and refuses entry recording and --write. Per-change --none --reason remains valid and is unnecessary; reads remain available. Without the file, the target is CHANGELOG.md.

The projection is rendered by the renderer `.arc/changelog.toml` selects: the built-in `keep-a-changelog`, or `command`, whose `renderer_command` argv runs from the repository root without a shell, with the authority of whoever runs arc changelog. It reads an arc-changelog-render-request/1 document on stdin and answers on stdout; `renderer_timeout` bounds it (default 60s), and its process group is killed at the deadline. `--json` never runs it. The request's projection is the text-side view: each entry's recorded session is null and its id is masked as [session] in the other recorded values, with or without --provenance.

Read-only projections name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable entries. --write requires every change to be readable; an unreadable selected change fails

Usage: arc changelog [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change whose entry is recorded or read. Omitted, recording infers it from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in, while a read projects every change's entry instead

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --category <CATEGORY>
          Free-form category for a newly recorded changelog entry

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Read a new entry body from a file ('-' for stdin)

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --none
          Record that the change needs no changelog entry, replacing any entry recorded before. Requires --reason

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --reason <REASON>
          Why the change needs no changelog entry; only with --none

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --json
          Emit a read result as JSON

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --provenance
          Include recording identity in human-readable output, excluding the session field and masking its id in metadata values as [session]. The provenance prefix and field names retain their literal text. JSON retains full session provenance

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --since <SINCE>
          Override the latest-tag release boundary

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --write
          Write the configured target. The built-in renderer replaces the generated [Unreleased] block in CHANGELOG.md; each paragraph and list item, bulleted or numbered, of an entry is refilled to 75 columns, continuations indented under their marker and never begun with a word Markdown would read as opening a block, and a fenced or indented code block keeps its lines, and empty list items keep their markers. Every line keeps its column within the list item holding it; where the entry's own bullet indents a line, a tab in front of its text is written as the spaces it stood for. The block is judged paragraph by paragraph, on words rather than the column they are wrapped at, and refused, naming each paragraph, while it holds prose no recorded entry produced. The block runs to the next release heading or the end of the file; a missing target, or one with no [Unreleased] heading, is refused with exit 1 and nothing written. A command renderer's stdout replaces the whole target, atomically, only after it exits 0 with a non-empty answer

      --keep-unrecorded
          Keep the block's unrecorded paragraphs whole above the projected entries, under an unrecorded marker, instead of refusing to write. Built-in renderer only

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc message --help =====
Append a structured cross-change announcement (never policy input)

Usage: arc message [OPTIONS] --type <MESSAGE_TYPE> --summary <SUMMARY> <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>
          Change the announcement is recorded against

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --type <MESSAGE_TYPE>
          Announcement class
          
          [possible values: status, discovery, note]

      --harness <HARNESS>

===== arc status --help =====
Machine-readable status report (the versioned arc-status/27 schema).

`debt_standing` is `declared` before the change integrates, `owed` after, and `discharged` once a review answers the debt; `debt_outstanding` is true only for `owed`. Forge observed and resolved head matches are separate; hosted checks apply only to their recorded PR identity and the patchset's approval anchor. Content and trailer rewrite successors require a fresh snapshot. Forge diagnostics expose conflicting or invalid observations; their projections remain unresolved and cannot establish forge readiness. Every verdict names its recorded origin, actor claims and admission provenance. Content coverage is separate from receiver gating eligibility. Readiness uses the integration shape resolved for the recorded target. Use check --shape to evaluate an explicit alternative. comparison_patchset names the closure's source patchset when recorded, otherwise the newest snapshot; head_matches_comparison_patchset compares its head. head_matches_latest_patchset always compares the newest snapshot. An unreadable selected change fails; unreadable neighboring changes are named with their errors on stderr and omitted from the dependency observation. An unreadable prerequisite cannot count as integrated Includes retained provisional deliveries, routing recipients, local merges and dispositions. Unfinished transitions include observed refs, event state, retained intent and recovery command. Recorded producer models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded. JSON production identities and review coverage retain raw values with model_recorded: false; valid models omit the flag. With a registry file, bindings and author facts appear as `review_context`; see `arc guide debt-clearance`. Gate rows carry recorded wait, preparation, command wall and CPU time and host observations; see `arc guide change-gates`.

Usage: arc status [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --json
          Accepted for compatibility; status output is always JSON

      --get <GET>
          Print one dotted object-key/array-index path

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --fields <FIELDS>
          Print a top-level JSON field subset

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --at <AT>
          Replay state as of this event ID ("what did the actor see?")

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>

===== arc events --help =====
Replay raw ledger events as NDJSON, optionally following new events

Records no events and writes nothing to the ledger, so a read-only ledger replays; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Local IDs are totally ordered by issuance across processes. ID cursors wait below unresolved provisional reservations. Imported events keep their origin IDs; follow emits unseen imports in each read. Follow waits on the workspace feed, with a fallback read every 30 seconds for writes without notices. If the feed cannot be followed, it polls with a backoff from 100 milliseconds to one second.

Usage: arc events [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --follow
          Continue emitting matching events appended after the replay

      --change <change_flag>
          Limit events to one exact change ID or unique prefix

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --tag <TAG>
          Limit events to the changes carrying all supplied tags (repeatable)

      --repository
          Read the repository's own events instead of a change's

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --type <EVENT_TYPE>
          Limit events to one raw kebab-case event_type value

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --since <SINCE>
          Start replay with IDs strictly greater than this cursor. Local IDs are totally ordered by issuance across processes; imports keep origin IDs. Follow emits all subsequently discovered events, including imports below the cursor. To recover older imports already present at restart, replay without --since and deduplicate by event ID. Replay withholds IDs at or above unresolved provisional reservations; a cursor crossing one refuses and names the boundary

      --exec <EXEC_COMMAND>
          Run `sh -c <cmd>` for every emitted event, with its NDJSON line on stdin and ARC_EVENT_ID, ARC_EVENT_TYPE, and ARC_CHANGE_ID set. A handler may exit without reading its line. A failing handler is a warning and never stops the stream

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc watch --help =====
Wait for a change, or a tagged series, to reach a ledger-derived condition

Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy.

Waits on the workspace feed, re-reading on a notice for a selected change, its repository, or unreadable or lost notices. Journal artifacts follow notices for their journal. A fallback read every 30 seconds covers writes without notices. If the feed cannot be followed, polling backs off from 100 milliseconds to one second. Stage and lease clocks wake at their deadlines; Git-derived conditions are checked each second using held ledger history.

Usage: arc watch [OPTIONS] --until <UNTIL> [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          A bare `.md` filename held only by another known journal is refused, naming its owning journal and project. Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --tag <TAG>
          Watch every change carrying all supplied tags (repeatable)

      --any
          With --tag: return when any one member reaches a condition

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --all
          With --tag: return when every member has reached a condition

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --until <UNTIL>
          Condition to wait for (repeatable, comma-separated): `snapshot`, `stalled`, `reviewed`, `approved`, `gates-green`, `ready`, `blocked`, `brief-recorded`, `integrated`, or `closed`.
          
          `stalled` is a stage clock, not an activity check: it holds once the change's live claim has sat in one stage longer than that stage's budget (`arc claim --stage-budget`), counted from its last `arc stage`, or from the claim while it is still at `launch`. Only `arc stage`, re-reporting the current stage included, or a snapshot under the claim restarts the clock; output, logs, and claim renewals do not. The reached line and the JSON name the stage, its age, and its budget. On a journal artifact, whose claim takes stages but no stage budgets, `stalled` means the claim's lease ran out.
          
          `reviewed` returns on any verdict against the patchset under review, whatever it concluded, and names the verdict event so the caller can read which. `approved` returns on the latest approving verdict, including a provisional approval and its reason. `gates-green` waits for every required gate to be green at the current head. `blocked` and `brief-recorded` name their events. `ready` is stricter and different: approved, gates green, no blockers — a review asking for changes never satisfies it, so waiting on `ready` for a dispatched review cannot tell a reviewer still working from one that answered.
          
          [possible values: snapshot, stalled, reviewed, approved, gates-green, ready, blocked, brief-recorded, integrated, closed]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --timeout <TIMEOUT>
          Fail with exit 2 after this many seconds. Each Git probe a check runs, and any other child it waits for without a timeout of its own, shares this deadline with its pipe cleanup: the child's process group is killed when the deadline passes, and unfinished pipe workers are detached. A child that dies or closes its input mid-check fails the check, and a check failing after the deadline reports the timeout

      --exec <EXEC_COMMAND>
          Run `sh -c <cmd>` once when a condition is reached, with the reached `arc-watch/1` document `--json` prints on stdin and ARC_EVENT_TYPE set to `watch-reached`. ARC_EVENT_ID and ARC_CHANGE_ID carry the document's values, empty where it names none. The hook may exit without reading its stdin; a hook that fails is a warning, and the watch still exits 0

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --json
          Emit the outcome as one `arc-watch/1` JSON object: `schema`, `event_type` (`watch-reached` or `watch-timeout`), and `condition`, the one reached or, on a timeout and under `--all`, every awaited one joined by commas. A reached change adds `change_id`, `event_id` when an event satisfied the condition, `provisional` on a provisional approval, and for `stalled` `stage`, `age_seconds`, and `budget_seconds`. `--all` lists one such member object per change under `changes`. A journal artifact names `file` and `claim_id`

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc export --help =====
Export one change as a deterministic, versioned JSON bundle.

History checksums hash compact JSON with recursively sorted object keys and a newline after each event, independent of stored field order. Exporter/project metadata is distinct from the preserved event origins. Includes a role-labelled Git object list derived from the exported events, including a delta's suffix and repository events. The summary counts objects by role; transports must deliver them before import. The commits a recorded rewrite replaced and retained for this change or in repository scope are listed with the role `retention-commit`. A commit a forge fact records as the provider's own is listed with the role `observed-commit` only where this repository holds it; an exporter that never fetched it does not oblige the receiver to. The commits and trees the repository events' received candidate evidence names are listed as `received-commit` and `received-tree` on the same terms. --custody-dir writes an optional arc-custody-bundle/1 sidecar: the retained content the exported forge observations and the repository events' received candidate evidence reference, which ledger custody holds, copied once per digest beside a manifest.json. Content absent or damaged here gets an explicit unavailable entry and the manifest and summary say incomplete; no archive claims complete custody it does not hold. The bundle binds the manifest's digest. The sidecar is finished beside its directory. --output, --objects-json and --custody-dir must name separate locations, none inside another, and the two files must not be directories; an overlap is refused before anything is written. The sidecar is published first, then the object list and the bundle; a failure removes what was published and restores what it replaced, so a previous file keeps its bytes, an empty directory stays empty and no bundle names a missing sidecar. Nonportable cost facts or bindings refuse transport; recording locators and unclassified cost reasons are never emitted. Recorded events are preserved. Records no events, initializes no replica and writes nothing to the ledger, so a read-only ledger exports; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy.

Usage: arc export [OPTIONS] --output <OUTPUT> <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>
          Change to export as a versioned bundle

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --output <OUTPUT>
          Output file ('-' for stdout)

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --since <SHA256>
          Export only the events after this history checksum, which the receiving store must already hold. Repository events travel in every bundle, even when the change suffix is empty. An import that changes this exact prefix requires a full export. Exports stop below the oldest unresolved provisional reservation

      --objects-json <FILE>
          Write an `arc-bundle-objects/1` JSON envelope with `schema` and `objects` fields for a transport

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --custody-dir <DIR>
          Write the custody sidecar into this new or empty directory

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --json
          Print an arc-export/1 summary with structured custody counts. Requires --output <FILE>; the summary goes to stdout

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc import --help =====
Import arc-bundle/6 or /5 into this repository's local store.

Imported events retain their origin IDs, including IDs below a previously observed cursor; subsequent local IDs follow the held floor. Received-byte digest, object availability, and publishing authorization are checked before publication; either refusal writes nothing. Unless --provenance-only is selected, every listed Git object must exist locally before any events or refs are written. Availability checks never fetch from a promisor remote. Bundles without an objects field retain advisory commit checks. Received source/tested/report heads that are held must be raw commits with their claimed trees; absent heads remain external claims. Tags are not peeled. These checks precede event, ref and custody publication. Held received originals are pinned under `refs/arc/retained/`. A candidate's optional original evidence-request digest binds its received document and declarant. Records without that intent remain readable and importable without establishing a registration retry. New rewrite events discard sender judgments and establish dependency-consistent approval carry against local objects (`arc guide rewrites-evidence`). Cyclic judgment states refuse with "rewrite judgments do not stabilize; nothing was imported" before publication. A rewrite event's retention refs are held under `refs/arc/retained/` for the imported change, and in repository scope, where the commit is here; a name that is no retention ref is refused, and events naming none import unchanged. Once the events are published, every commit the ledger cites that the bundle's object list delivered, that is here, and that no ref reaches is held under `refs/arc/retained/<change>/<subject>/<commit>` once per commit, leaving every name that already stands, and printed as `retained: <ref> <commit>`; --dry-run creates none (`arc guide change-bundles`). A projected falsification revision requires an active, uncontradicted rewrite admitted with rewrite authority. Legacy transport without an authorization receipt supplies no such identity proof. Full and delta histories replay with the same admitted proof before publication. Paired stores and receivers with enrollments require a transport-asserted publisher and exact received-byte SHA256. Operator enrollment authorizes each introduced origin and capability; actor strings remain claims. Admission and its private authorization receipt publish atomically. Receipt permissions bind full canonical content and scope. Enrollment is revalidated under the publication lock; unpublished failures leave the format stamp unchanged. --provenance-only permits missing objects; it never bypasses authorization. --custody-dir installs an arc-custody-bundle/1 sidecar into the ledger's custody. The bundle must bind the manifest's digest; the manifest, its change, its entries against the references of the bundled change and repository events, every path, length and sha256, and the absence of symlinks are all checked before any event, ref or file is written, and corrupt content or an unsafe path refuses the whole import. Content already held is not copied again, and installation is reported only after the events are published; a failure there says so and repeating the import finishes it. A ledger `custody/` or `custody/sha256` directory that is a symlink refuses the import before any event is published. Unavailable entries stay unavailable and the summary says incomplete. A bundle that binds a sidecar imported without one reports how many references stay unavailable, the manifest it binds and the flag that installs it. Import initializes no replica. A listed `observed-commit` held here is pinned under `refs/arc/retained/` for the imported change. Candidate events stamp store format 7, including evaluations, selections, and promotions without a registration in the bundle. Events without model provenance retain that absence. Repository map withdrawals apply even to maps already held; invalid withdrawal targets are refused before anything is written. So is a change history no command could have written: work after the change closed, an audit or post-integration disposition before it integrated, or a post-integration disposition on a finding that was resolved, accepted-risk, or obsolete when the change shipped, or a landing completion naming a member at a patchset or source head that member's ledger here does not hold. The bundled member and held partners follow the same combined repository history, including rewrites arriving with the completion; a signature-only successor needs no newer partner patchset. Nonportable cost facts or bindings refuse transport: recording locators and unclassified cost reasons are not admitted. A newly admitted candidate evidence attachment requires its registration's original requested-evidence digest and matching declarant. Already-held attachments without an intent stay readable as unbound claims; their presence never implies producer consent

Usage: arc import [OPTIONS] <INPUT>

Arguments:
  <INPUT>
          Input file ('-' for stdin)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --publisher <PUBLISHER>
          Publishing principal asserted by the authenticated receiving caller

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --received-sha256 <RECEIVED_SHA256>
          SHA256 of the exact received bytes, supplied by the receiving caller

      --dry-run
          Validate and report without writing files, Git objects, events, or refs

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --provenance-only
          Import events even when listed Git objects are missing, reporting each missing OID and role. Evidence requires those objects to arrive

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --custody-dir <DIR>
          Install this arc-custody-bundle/1 sidecar directory after judging it

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --json
          Print an arc-import/1 summary with diagnostics and custody counts. Custody phase is planned, installed or not-supplied. Installed counts in a planned summary describe the prospective effect

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc accept --help =====
Prepare a delivered change on the paired integration authority (lead only).

Admission precedes local checkout binding, exclusive scoped signing, snapshotting at the delivered base and bound brief, local gates, and baseline/final probes. Execution or observation errors remain distinct from failed gates and probe discrimination. Preparation records evidence and returns readiness; it records no judgment. A local review still owed normally exits 3. JSON includes structured custody counts from the latest import step, with phase planned, installed or not-supplied. Moved authoritative history requires explicit rebase, snapshot, and review. Stops at the first failure and reports refs, objects, events and pending rewrite recovery state, including effects preceding event publication. Unpublished bindings report administrative ownership and allocation proof; retry can finish a locally owned, empty, index-free checkout after guarded identity checks. Unrelated contents or changed ownership refuse recovery. Commands carry executable argv/cwd; internal bindings and signature checks are identified separately. Local event IDs follow admitted history even when the sender's clock is ahead. Retrying the same delivery reuses admission, checkout binding, signature recovery, and snapshot. Dry-run validates admission and local state without allocating, signing, executing checks, creating objects, or recording evidence; future outcomes are conditional. JSON emits one arc-accept/1 transcript. A delivery whose bundle binds an arc-custody-bundle/1 sidecar installs it with --custody-dir, judged as `arc import --custody-dir` judges it, in the import preview and before any effect. Without the flag the transcript says custody is unavailable, names the bound manifest digest and the flag. Populated checkouts use .arc/worktrees.toml preparation. Path-stable build storage remains outside checkout cleanup and is leased while used.

Usage: arc accept [OPTIONS] --branch <BRANCH> --publisher <PUBLISHER> --received-sha256 <RECEIVED_SHA256> <INPUT>

Arguments:
  <INPUT>
          Delivered arc-bundle/6 or /5 file ('-' for stdin)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --branch <BRANCH>
          Existing local branch matching the delivered change branch and latest patchset head, or its recorded signature-only successor. A rebased head requires a snapshot on the publishing replica before export; preserve the implementing actors with --contributors when the publisher only rebased, export again, and accept the new bundle

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --publisher <PUBLISHER>
          Authenticated receiving caller's enrolled publishing principal

      --received-sha256 <RECEIVED_SHA256>
          SHA256 of the exact received bytes

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --worktree <WORKTREE>
          Matching clean checkout, or absent path for an owned allocation

      --key <KEY>
          Signing key override; otherwise use Git's configured key

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --contributors <CONTRIBUTORS>
          Explicit contributor actors; defaults to the delivered declaration

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --solo
          Declare the acting subject as the sole contributor

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --dry-run
          Validate and report conditional steps without effects

      --json
          Emit one arc-accept/1 document, including retained effects and readiness

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

      --custody-dir <DIR>
          Install the arc-custody-bundle/1 sidecar the delivery's bundle binds

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')
