===== arc check --help =====
Integration preflight under the resolved target shape or --shape; exit code identifies the first blocker.

arc-check/4 reports recorded origins, admission provenance, content coverage, gating eligibility, and the selected authorizing verdict. A `none = "<reason>"` declaration in the invoking checkout's .arc/changelog.toml silences no-changelog-entry and prints its reason; JSON includes the optional changelog_none reason. This declaration uses the invoking checkout, like debt advisory thresholds, rather than the change's target branch. An unreadable declaration warns on stderr and declares nothing; readiness and the exit code are unchanged. Contribution-trailer advice never changes readiness or the exit code: a commit lacking an asserted line names arc rewrite trailers --from <rev> --append <line>, and a role line carrying a placeholder or a session id names arc rewrite trailers --from <rev> --edits <file>, to run before integration.

A change behind its target lists `merged-tree-unevaluated` beside `needs-rebase` under every shape, `ff-only` included, until the merge is evaluated; the exit code is the first listed, so `needs-rebase` (11) shadows `merged-tree-unevaluated` (14).

Unreadable neighboring changes are named with their errors on stderr; an unreadable selected change fails and an unreadable prerequisite cannot authorize integration

Human blocker reports name the guide topics owning their recovery rules. With a registry file, bindings and author facts appear as `review_context`; see `arc guide debt-clearance`.

Usage: arc check [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --provisional
          Evaluate explicit unsigned staging preparation and local provisional prerequisites. An authority offer in flight reports an integrate --provisional --dry-run command with an explicit recipient for the same readiness evaluation. The command retains the selected staging destination

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --into <INTO>
          Staging destination under arc/provisional/<local-replica>/; requires --provisional

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --shape <SHAPE>
          Integration shape to evaluate; defaults resolve from each selected destination's local branch ref, independently of same-named tags
          
          [possible values: ff-only, no-ff]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --tag <TAG>
          Check every change carrying all supplied tags

      --explain
          Print a full readiness checklist of every gate condition

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --json
          Emit all blockers and the resulting exit code as JSON

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

Exit status, with blockers in precedence order:
  0   ready to integrate
  17  integration-mode
  6   closed, branch-missing, target-unreadable
  20  landing-pending
  15  fork-branch
  13  iterating
  7   blocked-by-changes
  19  lands-with
  11  needs-rebase
  14  merged-tree-unevaluated
  2   blocking-findings
  3   no-valid-approval
  5   gates-not-green
  12  acceptance-probes-not-green
  4   hold-active
  1   an error, named on stderr
  2   also a usage error, refused before any change is read
A change with several blockers exits with the first one's code. With --tag, the command exits with the code of the first blocked change it lists, or 0 when every match is ready or closed.

===== arc squash --help =====
Rewrite a change as one commit on its base, with the same tree, and record it as a new patchset.

Evidence and verdicts on the earlier heads stay with those heads; the single commit is gated and reviewed like any other patchset. A failed commit or hook-modified tree restores the original head, index, and tracked files; untracked files are retained. Obstructing paths are moved to a reported recovery directory under <git-common-dir>/arc/squash-recovery/. Over another actor's live claim, a squash without --contributors or --solo refuses before the branch moves. The change stays locked from that check until the patchset is recorded, so a command acting on the change while the commit is made reports the lock busy

Usage: arc squash [OPTIONS] --message <MESSAGE> [CHANGE]

      --contributors <ACTOR[,ACTOR...]>

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --solo
          Record the invoking actor as the sole contributor; a placeholder actor or a harness:session actor with an empty session refuses

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`

===== arc snapshot --help =====
Record the current branch head as a patchset with explicit base and brief binding.

Accept preserves the delivered binding and contributor declaration through signature-only preparation; snapshots record evidence, never a judgment. A head whose rewrite invalidated approval requires a fresh patchset, even when the latest patchset's mapped head already matches the branch. Re-approval requires a verdict on that fresh patchset. The replay `arc rebase` performs itself carries a matching patchset's author coordinates, or completed bound-run attribution established at the original head before replay; the replaying session is not its implementer. That replay runs where the project's configuration cannot define a merge driver or filter, and arc reads its result from the ref Git updated, not from command output. Ordinary snapshots derive executor authorship from completed, bound runs when no --contributors, --solo or --on-behalf-of is supplied; the exact evidence and conservative fallback rule is in `arc guide debt-review`. Explicit attribution takes precedence. At unchanged content, an explicit --on-behalf-of (also supplied by ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF) differing from the latest effective author records a fresh patchset. An equal or omitted subject preserves that patchset and its attribution; the unchanged output names the effective subject. Earlier verdicts remain bound to their original patchsets.

Usage: arc snapshot [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --base <BASE>
          Override the recorded base revision

      --brief-version <BRIEF_VERSION>
          Brief version this patchset implements (defaults to latest)

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --verify
          Run verification after recording the patchset, reusing the newest green evidence at the same tree and running the rest

      --gate <GATE>
          Gate name from .arc/gates.toml (repeatable with --verify). Names must be unique and declared; invalid names refuse before any write

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --all
          Run every gate declared for the change profile

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --rerun
          Run the selected gates even where a pass would be reused (with --verify)

      --contributors <ACTOR[,ACTOR...]>
          Explicitly set the contributors for this patchset. Recording a patchset while another actor holds a live claim on the change requires this or --solo. Each is an actor: a harness:session form requires a nonempty session; a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one refuses

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --solo
          Record the invoking actor as the sole contributor; a placeholder actor or a harness:session actor with an empty session refuses

      --amend <PATCHSET>
          Amend one patchset's contributors before any verdict exists

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

      --journal-ref <FILE>
          Journal artifact this patchset was framed by (repeatable): a filename in this project's journal, or `<journal-dir>::<file>` for one in the journal at the absolute `<journal-dir>` another project's `arc journal dir` prints. The link records the reference as given. Its body digest is read from the owning journal when the patchset is recorded, and a name that resolves to no artifact is refused naming each other known journal that holds it; a path is refused naming the reference that resolves it. Given once or more, only the flagged artifacts are linked, `via: flag`. Omitted, the patchset links the artifact the change was opened from (`via: begin`) and the plan its brief names (`via: brief`), one link per file, a file named by both linked once as `begin`; a default that no longer resolves is left out with a warning on stderr. A rerun at an unchanged head with neither this nor --thread keeps existing links

      --thread <SCHEME:ID>
          External thread this work belongs to, as SCHEME:ID. Arc stores the identifiers and never fetches or resolves them

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc resolve --help =====
Record a finding disposition (supersedes current tips automatically)

A review finding takes its disposition while the change is open. On an integrated change, a review finding left open when the change shipped takes a post-integration disposition, recorded beside the finding state the change shipped with, which stays as it shipped; readers show both. A finding released by the receiver's eligible disposition graph at integration takes none. Recorded dispositions replay independently of receiver trust; eligibility is assessed in the receiver's projection. An audit finding takes its disposition after integration. A disposition is not a verdict and discharges no review debt.

===== arc review --help =====
Read review state, or record a verdict with an optional findings batch. Recorded history includes imported inputs; receiver eligibility requires admitted review-authority and operator trust for a foreign origin. Foreign verdicts cannot replace the receiver's local judgment.

Once a verdict is recorded, review says whether an approval gates. That is advice about a verdict that already stands: a failure to evaluate it is a warning naming the recorded verdict, and the review still succeeds. An unreadable tie-break election grants no exception; the verdict records and reports the lookup failure. An implicit verdict on the latest patchset is refused if a rewrite invalidated its review head; run `arc snapshot` or use `--snapshot`. Explicit `--patchset` judgments may name historical work; they retain that patchset's review head and may be stale for the branch head.

Authorship is read from the ledger, not from commit trailers: the patchset's recorded contributors, else its effective author, and its production record's implementer. Unless the dated tie-break election in `arc guide debt-review` applies, an approval under that implementer's model, at any effort, warns that it shows a review happened and not that it was independent of whoever wrote the work. Where `forbid_self_approval` applies, the gate rejects it as a self-approval, exactly as it rejects a reviewer matching a contributor, and says so when the verdict is recorded. Such a read never discharges review debt, regardless of self-approval policy: permission to approve does not establish independent coverage. `[policy] tiebreak_reviewers` on the committed target or operator layer waives only the model-match test on mixed-model work. The recorded model#effort and any observation must exactly match an entry in force at created_at; author actors, sessions, contributors and assumed identities remain barred. Each read retains its election or absence despite later policy edits. Its election binds to the recorded destination and supplies no exception for another `--into` target. Planning and orchestrating never refuse. The warning, the gate and debt discharge judge the verdict under the model it records and, where the reviewer's session store observed another, the observed one: either matching counts, and the warning or rejection names which model matched, with the recorded model beside an observed one.

Under assessed policy an approval gates only when it clears. A target ladder decides capability when present; otherwise the agent needs a recorded model with effort and a bound assessment saying it clears. Other approvals are findings reads. Integration requires declared debt over such a read and derives ineligible-review; all other bars remain. Verdicts retain observed publication authority.

A verified authority refusal on a provisional delivery remains applicable across authority transfer. Clearing it requires applicable explicit supersession; event-ID order and wall clock cannot make an earlier approval supersede an unobserved refusal. Scoped admission, trust and independence determine review eligibility. With a registry file, bindings and author facts appear as `review_context`; see `arc guide debt-clearance`. Under assessed policy with a target ladder, arc decides capability from rank, author floors and families. Assessments remain binding evidence; their capability opinion cannot override the ladder. The complete rule and coordinate cutoff are in `arc guide debt-clearance`.

Usage: arc review [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --verdict <VERDICT>
          The conclusion recorded
          
          [possible values: approved, changes-requested, comment-only]

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --start
          Record that this session began reviewing the change, on `--patchset` or the latest, before or after integration. A cost view counts the reviewer's session toward the change from this start to its last event on the change

      --json
          Emit the read view as a versioned JSON object

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --body <BODY>
          Inline body text

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Read body from file ('-' for stdin)

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --snapshot
          Snapshot the clean change worktree before recording the verdict; attribution follows `arc snapshot`, including completed bound runs. Explicit flags take precedence. The findings batch and the verdict are validated first, so a refused review records no patchset. The change stays locked from the snapshot until the verdict on that patchset is recorded, so a command acting on the change in between reports the lock busy. A head whose rewrite invalidated approval gets a fresh patchset; the verdict binds to that patchset, leaving older approvals stale

      --contributors <ACTOR[,ACTOR...]>
          Explicitly set the contributors for this patchset. Recording a patchset while another actor holds a live claim on the change requires this or --solo. Each is an actor: a harness:session form requires a nonempty session; a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one refuses

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --solo
          Record the invoking actor as the sole contributor; a placeholder actor or a harness:session actor with an empty session refuses

      --patchset <PATCHSET>
          Patchset under review, by id or by the revision it recorded. Defaults to the latest — which is what the verdict then claims, whatever the reviewer actually read

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

      --cause <CAUSE>
          Root cause of requested rework; repeat for a mixed round. Required with `--verdict changes-requested` and refused with any other verdict

          Possible values:
          - brief:                 The patchset faithfully exposed a missing, false, or ambiguous premise
          - executor:              The patchset violated a correct applicable brief
          - integration-staleness: Later target work invalidated a correct brief and implementation

      --forge-source <FORGE_SOURCE>
          Exact observed content used by this judgment and inline findings (repeatable OBS:GENERATION). Later forge edits expose drift and never invalidate this local verdict

      --findings-json <FINDINGS_JSON>
          Findings batch: a path to a JSON array, or '-' for stdin. Each element is an object with a required `severity` (critical, major, minor, or note) and `summary` (string), and optional `blocking` (bool, default false), `body` (string), and `anchor`. An anchor is an object with a required `path` and optional `side` (base or head, default head), `line_start`, `line_end`, and `context`. An unknown field of a finding or anchor that looks like a misspelling of one it omits (`blocker` for `blocking`, `title` for `summary`, `path` for `anchor`, `line` for `line_start`, `lines` for `line_start` and `line_end`, or one edit away) refuses the batch; any other is ignored with a warning, except a finding's `id`, ignored silently because IDs are assigned by arc. An approval cannot carry a blocking finding. Optional forge_sources is an array of exact OBS:GENERATION references; review-level sources are inherited

      --relation <RELATION>
          What this verdict does to the verdicts already standing on the change. `supersedes` replaces them; `corroborates` supports one without becoming a second authority, which is what discharging a provisional approval is. Ignored when no verdict stands yet

          Possible values:
          - corroborates: Supports the observed verdicts without replacing them
          - supersedes:   Replaces the observed verdict tips
          
          [default: supersedes]

      --provisional <REASON>
          Say this verdict is owed corroboration, and why. It gates like any other verdict — independence and staleness are unchanged — but the change carries a recorded obligation until somebody else supplies a second judgment, and `arc query --provisional` finds it until they do. Obligation selection follows approval's local-first verdict history; a trusted foreign claim cannot create one beside an authoritative local judgment. Use it when the reviewer's judgment has not been validated: an unproven model, a rushed pass, a reviewer outside their competence. arc never infers this; naming which reviewers are proven would be a routing opinion it does not hold

      --route-version <VERSION>
          The routing version that selected this reviewer. Recorded as a coordinate and nothing else: arc joins it against no roster and reads no quality from it. Omitted, the review is unrouted

      --assessment <FILE>
          Routing's assessment of this reviewer: an `agent-routing assess --json` file, validated, kept and bound to the reviewer's model and to the reviewed patchset's `Implemented-by` coordinates exactly as `arc audit --assessment` binds one; a binding that fails is recorded and said. With assessed ladder policy, arc decides findings eligibility and capability clearance; the file's opinion is retained and disagreements are named. Without a ladder, assessed clearance requires a bound /2 file saying clears: true. Every supplied file still has to bind the coordinate and authors

      --receipt <FILE>
          Record every verdict item of an `arc-review-receipt/1` file from `arc receipt template --verdict`, each on its own change, for the reviewer the receipt names (`arc receipt --help` has the shape). Each item is recorded as `--verdict` records one: the receipt's reviewer is the represented subject and the invoker the writer, under the same locks, independence, clearance and assessment binding. A receipt holding audit items, or both kinds, is refused. `--json` emits `arc-receipt-result/1`. Exits 1 when any item was refused; the others still record

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc receipt --help =====
Review receipts: what a delegated reader read and concluded, in one file arc records.

`arc-review-receipt/1` is a JSON object: `schema`, `issued_at` and `vocabulary` (kept from the template; `vocabulary` names the values a field accepts, keyed by its path, and a changed one is refused), `reviewer` (`harness`, its full native `session`, and `model` as `model#effort`), and `items`. Each item carries `key`, `kind` (`verdict` or `audit`) and `subject`, copied unchanged from the template, and what the reader fills in: `verdict` (approved, changes-requested, comment-only), `causes` (with changes-requested on a verdict item, refused elsewhere), optional RFC 3339 `judged_at`, `body`, `findings` (each with `severity` (`critical`, `major`, `minor` or `note`), `blocking` and `summary` required, `body` optional, and on a verdict item an optional `path` and `line` anchoring it to the head), `dispositions` (each naming a `finding` by finding id or finding event id, a `disposition` of `fixed`, `still-open` or `not-a-defect`, and optional `evidence`), and `assessment`, an embedded `agent-routing-assessment` object or a path relative to the receipt. An audit item also carries `extent`, what its reader covered: null when not established, `{"whole": true}`, or `{"paths": [<glob>...]}` with optional `"exclude": [<glob>...]`, either with an optional `"domain"` of `diff` (the default) or `tree`. Its globs resolve against the subject `arc audit --extent-paths` resolves them against (`arc guide debt-obligations`), and only the resolved entries are recorded. Unknown fields are refused, and so is an `extent` on a verdict item (`extent-not-accepted`).

`fixed` records `resolved`, `still-open` records `still-open`, and `not-a-defect` records `disputed`, which releases no blocking finding. A verdict item disposes the change's review findings, an audit item its audit findings; dispositions are part of the read's own event.

Recording, by `arc review --receipt` or `arc audit --receipt`, keeps the receipt's bytes in the ledger's custody under their sha256, which each read records with their length so an export's custody sidecar carries them, and refuses the whole receipt, writing nothing, for an unreadable file, a wrong schema, a missing `issued_at`, mixed kinds, or a reviewer whose session is not a full native id (claude, codex and pi ids are UUIDs, opencode ids start `ses_`; a prefix, a delegate handle or another harness is refused) or whose model names no effort. Each item is then recorded or refused on its own, under the change's locks: an item whose key an identical item already published for the same represented reviewer and model prints that event and writes nothing. Both the declared model and the binding model must match: the session store's observed model where present, otherwise the declared model. Different content, reviewer identity or either model is refused (`key-conflict`), naming both digests and the recorded and submitted reviewers, declared models and binding models; a subject that no longer matches (`subject-mismatch`: store, change, patchset, head, base, brief digest, or for an audit the closure, integrated revision, source patchset and outstanding debts), a head two patchsets record (`ambiguous-head`), a key that does not derive from the subject and `issued_at` (`invalid`), a verdict, finding or disposition recorded after the template (`stale-frontier`, naming them), and a contradiction (`contradiction`: approved with a blocking finding, changes-requested without causes, a disposition of a finding not on the subject or of one finding twice) and an extent whose glob matches nothing (`extent-unresolved`) are refused, never normalised. A recorded read that clears nothing is not refused; its bar is reported. Where the reviewer's session store holds the session, the model it observed is compared with the receipt's: a difference is recorded on each read and the assessment binds the observed model.

A receipt's basis uses an existing reviewer session reply naming the exact item key and verdict, else bounded `judged_at`, else recording time. Echo `<key>: <verdict>` on its own line or return the JSON item or receipt. `judged_at` outside `issued_at`..ingestion (inclusive) or malformed refuses the item (`judged-at`). Basis fields survive transport; the receiver judges its own ladder at that time. See `arc guide debt-clearance`. Receipt reads use the same target ladder, coordinate admission, binding, authority and coverage checks as direct reads; see `arc guide debt-clearance`.

Usage: arc receipt [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:
  template  Print an `arc-review-receipt/1` template with one item per change
  help      Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`

===== arc publication --help =====
Require publication gates at a local target branch's current head.

Use this guard from push and release tooling before publishing that revision. Scratch checkout preparation and build leases follow .arc/worktrees.toml; stable storage never substitutes for complete publication evidence. Fresh and cached receipts must meet current budgets; --skip-green never retries a budget refusal.

===== arc verify --help =====
Run a declared gate (or ad hoc command) and record the evidence.

Gate evidence only counts at the change's own head, so the command runs in the change's recorded worktree whichever checkout it was typed in; `--attest` records evidence arc did not run, and `--after-integration` keeps an integrated change's later check as a receipt that is never gate evidence. Acceptance executes local head gates even when imported evidence passed, and selects probes from the delivered patchset's bound brief. Baselines run in scratch checkouts. Declared timeouts also bound output-pipe cleanup after the shell exits. Arc kills the process group and does not wait for escaped descendants; unfinished readers supply no output tail, diagnostic artifact or environment identity. A gate's process group is killed when it ends, so nothing left in it outlives the run. SIGINT, SIGTERM or SIGHUP during a gate kills its group, records nothing from the run and ends arc with that signal. Executed gates capture combined stdout and stderr as private local artifacts: the first 16 MiB per run, retaining 16 completed captures. Events and status carry the producing store, relative path, retained and observed SHA-256 digests and sizes, and truncation or capture errors. Exports carry metadata only; expired files are unavailable diagnostics. Attested evidence carries no output capture. Configured path-stable build storage is leased through preparation, environment probes and gates. Probes never prepare or compile; unavailable storage supplies no environment identity. Storage reuse never supplies a passing gate result. A result whose evidence a busy ledger lock kept from being recorded prints as `not recorded:` and is not evidence; see `arc guide rules-claims`. A command exiting 75 within its timeout records `not-admitted`; see `arc guide change-gates`. Executed receipts separate `wait_ms`, `prepare_ms`, and command `run_ms`; `duration_ms` spans execution, including slot acquisition and in-run preparation. `prepare_ms` also includes preparation before environment probes; a batch charges shared preparation to its first executed command. `cpu_user_ms` and `cpu_system_ms` exclude the holder and are absent unless every descendant was accounted for. `load_average` and `core_count` describe the host at command start. These fields appear in `show --json` and `status`; attestations carry none. Budgets judge command run_ms under arc guide gates. An over-budget raw pass exits 1; reuse refuses it until --rerun. Untimed evidence is unmeasured. Declared timeout_seconds and effective execution_limit_ms are recorded apart. See `arc guide change-gates` for accounting boundaries.

Usage: arc verify [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --all
          Run every gate required for the selected event and change profile

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --parallel
          Run all declared gates concurrently and append evidence in name order

      --rerun
          Run each declared gate even where a pass would be reused.
          
          By default a declared gate run by --all, --gate or --against is not run again where a pass already answers for the content: at the head's tree, or with --against at the merged tree, under the same declaration and environment. Evidence counts for its tree whichever commit it ran on, as it does for readiness, and only the newest evidence readiness counts there is a candidate; a gate declaring `binds = "commit"` takes only evidence for the head's own commit. The reuse is recorded as `verification-reused` naming that tree and requires that evidence to carry the same content key. A gate reruns when its newest evidence failed, was not admitted, was bypassed, or has neither tree nor tested_tree, and when its environment probe yields no identity. --falsified-by always runs. Integration may reuse a clean passing source across a delta when a complete project selector excludes the gate and echoes the complete input object in its response's request field. Its provenance is recorded; unavailable, mismatched or uncertain selection runs the gate. Publication checks the complete tier on changed trees.

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --skip-green
          Accepted and ignored: reuse is the default. See --rerun

      --event <EVENT>
          Select integration or publication gates for --all or --against
          
          [possible values: integration, publication]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --gate <GATE>
          Gate name from .arc/gates.toml

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --command <COMMAND>
          Ad hoc command (recorded, but not a declared gate)

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --probe <PROBE>
          Named acceptance probe declared by a brief

      --brief-version <BRIEF_VERSION>
          Brief version containing --probe (defaults to latest)

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

      --probe-phase <PROBE_PHASE>
          Acceptance-probe evidence phase (defaults to final). Baseline commands run at the brief's base, in a detached scratch checkout under the configured worktrees directory when HEAD differs. Relative directories resolve against the invoking checkout's root; creation, cleanup and printed commands use the same absolute path. The scratch checkout is removed after the run. Failed creation cleans only this attempt's allocation and administrative entries whose gitdir names its path, preserving unrelated registrations. Cleanup confirms the path is absent from Git's worktree inventory before offering manual recreation commands. Failed cleanup reports the retained path and its removal command. Attested baseline evidence requires --tested-revision equal to the base
          
          [possible values: baseline, final]

      --attest
          Record externally observed evidence without running the command (e.g. a sandboxed executor or another host ran the gate)

      --after-integration
          Record the attestation as a check observed after the change integrated, retained beside its gate evidence and never among it.
          
          Requires --attest and an integrated change, and names one --gate or --command. The tested revision's tree must equal the integrated commit's tree; a receipt for other content is refused, so it never reads as verification of what shipped. The receipt records the producer, the execution host and runner, the command, the gate's declared timeout, the result, the tested revision and tree, and when it was recorded. It names the integrated commit as the integration recorded it, which views follow through rewrite maps, so withdrawing a map strands none. It changes no authorization basis, discharges no review debt, and satisfies no gate. An open change takes the ordinary attestation instead

      --result <RESULT>
          The attested result; required with --attest, rejected without it
          
          [possible values: pass, fail]

      --tested-revision <TESTED_REVISION>
          Revision actually tested; required with --attest

      --execution-host <EXECUTION_HOST>
          Host or environment that executed the attested command; required with --attest. Local runs record the hostname, or `unknown` when it cannot be read

      --runner <RUNNER>
          Stable identity of the external runner or job; required with --attest

      --environment <IDENTITY>
          Environment identity the attested evidence applies to, as the gate's declared environment probe yields it.
          
          Required with --attest for a gate whose declaration has an environment probe, and rejected for a declaration that has none: an identity is only meaningful against the probe that produces it. Omit it for a run arc observes itself; arc reads the environment from the declared probe

      --note <NOTE>
          Optional note recorded alongside the evidence

      --waive-dirty <REASON>
          Let evidence from a dirty worktree count, saying why.
          
          Dirt is fatal by default: a passing run whose tree no checkout reproduces is recorded and declines to satisfy the gate. The waiver binds the way the evidence binds — to this head alone — so the next commit ends it rather than leaving a standing exemption. It is visible to a reviewer, who is free to disagree with it. A baseline probe requiring a clean scratch checkout refuses this flag before recording; a baseline at HEAD can waive that HEAD.

      --falsified-by <EVENT_ID>
          Earlier failing evidence for this same check that this run answers.
          
          A gate that has only ever passed and a gate watched to fail and then fixed leave the same record. Naming the failure separates them. The event must be a failing verification of the same gate or command on this change; the reference stores the failure event's original revision even when a recorded rewrite projects it elsewhere. Replay accepts a rewritten reference only along an active recorded rewrite chain from that original revision. A withdrawn map supplies no proof: a reference only it carried is set aside and reported by `show`; restore the actual map with `arc history rewrite` to recover it. The candidate event is replayed before append; unreadable evidence is refused before recording or pinning its tree. Requires --predicted, and is advisory: it changes no gate result, readiness decision, or exit code.
          
          Only this declaration makes a gate `discriminating`. Separately, a pass that follows a failure of the same gate (or command, when unnamed) on this change records the newest such failure as `falsification_inferred`, whether or not this flag is given; that inference decides nothing. Acceptance-probe evidence neither records one nor serves as the failure one names.

      --predicted <REASON>
          Why the check was expected to fail, stated before it ran.
          
          A reason read off the failure afterwards restates the output; one stated beforehand is a claim that could have been wrong, which is what makes the pass that followed mean something. Requires --falsified-by.

      --against <BRANCH>
          Run every required gate against the merge with this branch, not against the change's own head.
          
          A change that is behind its target merges to content neither branch committed, and evidence at the head says nothing about it. This synthesizes that merge, checks it out on its own, runs the declared gates there, and records the result against the merged tree. The scratch checkout is removed after gates and preparation errors; source-mutating preparation keeps it for inspection. It is not created when no gate is left to run. The evidence is spent as soon as the target moves again, because that is a different merge. A gate already green at that merged tree records reuse rather than running a second time, unless --rerun is given.
          
          Gate declarations come from this destination's local branch ref, including when it differs from the change's recorded target. A same-named tag cannot select the destination. A textual conflict, including a modify/delete conflict, refuses before any gate runs or evidence is recorded; rebase first.

      --bypass <REASON>
          Refuse retired gate bypass. Make the gate fit its budget (`arc gates over`), or integrate with recorded debt

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc done --help =====
Finish implementation: snapshot, verify all gates, then print check state.

A profile with no declared gate records no evidence and prints that no gate is declared instead of a pass.

The caller's live claim moves to `verifying` before the snapshot, and only after every snapshot input is accepted: the attribution, --thread, and each --journal-ref. An input it refuses leaves the claim's stage and its clock as they were.

Usage: arc done [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --contributors <ACTOR[,ACTOR...]>
          Explicitly set the contributors for this patchset. Recording a patchset while another actor holds a live claim on the change requires this or --solo. Each is an actor: a harness:session form requires a nonempty session; a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one refuses

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --solo
          Record the invoking actor as the sole contributor; a placeholder actor or a harness:session actor with an empty session refuses

      --journal-ref <FILE>
          Journal artifact this patchset was framed by (repeatable): a filename in this project's journal, or `<journal-dir>::<file>` for one in the journal at the absolute `<journal-dir>` another project's `arc journal dir` prints. The link records the reference as given. Its body digest is read from the owning journal when the patchset is recorded, and a name that resolves to no artifact is refused naming each other known journal that holds it; a path is refused naming the reference that resolves it. Given once or more, only the flagged artifacts are linked, `via: flag`. Omitted, the patchset links the artifact the change was opened from (`via: begin`) and the plan its brief names (`via: brief`), one link per file, a file named by both linked once as `begin`; a default that no longer resolves is left out with a warning on stderr. A rerun at an unchanged head with neither this nor --thread keeps existing links

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --thread <SCHEME:ID>
          External thread this work belongs to, as SCHEME:ID. Arc stores the identifiers and never fetches or resolves them

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

===== arc rebase --help =====
Replay a change's branch onto its target, then snapshot the new head.

Where Git can, arc replays with `git replay`: it runs no hooks, leaves the worktree alone, and writes the new tip to a ref arc reads. The replay runs in a temporary repository with no project configuration, sharing only the object store, so a merge driver or filter the project configures cannot run. The commits it creates carry no signature, whatever the repository configures for commits it makes; `arc rewrite sign` signs a replayed range. Such a replay carries a matching patchset's author coordinates, or completed bound-run attribution established at the original head before replay under `arc guide debt-review`, model included; the replaying session is not its implementer, and the patchset names exactly the tip that replay wrote. A `post-rewrite` hook, a reflog entry, a merge driver, or an inherited `GIT_REFLOG_ACTION` cannot make ordinary work look replayed. A Git that cannot replay, a refusal, or a conflict falls back to `git rebase`, whose result is recorded as ordinary work; a stopped rebase leaves its partial resolution in progress. Before either replays, each commit of the replayed range that the ledger cites and none of the change's keep pins reaches is held under `refs/arc/retained/<change>/<subject>/<commit>`, and each ref created is printed as `retained: <ref> <commit>` (`arc guide rewrites-evidence`); a ref that cannot be created refuses the rebase before anything is replayed. `--contributors` and `--solo` decide the replayed patchset's contributor set; a declared member the replay carries over keeps its recorded coordinate, and one it does not carry is credited as declared. The snapshot links the artifact the change was opened from (`via: begin`) and the plan its brief names (`via: brief`), one link per file; a file named by both is linked once as `begin`. An unavailable artifact is omitted with a warning on stderr. Over another actor's live claim, a rebase without --contributors or --solo refuses before the branch moves. The change stays locked from that check until the replayed head is recorded, so a command acting on the change during the replay reports the lock busy. The recorded patchset carries when the rebase started (`rebase_started_at`), which `arc stats --round-trip` reports as the landing's rebase phase. The gates the replayed head owes are named after it is recorded; a failure to evaluate them is a warning, and the rebase still succeeds

Usage: arc rebase [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --verify
          Run every required gate at the replayed head

      --contributors <ACTOR[,ACTOR...]>
          Explicitly set the contributors for this patchset. Recording a patchset while another actor holds a live claim on the change requires this or --solo. Each is an actor: a harness:session form requires a nonempty session; a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one refuses

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --solo
          Record the invoking actor as the sole contributor; a placeholder actor or a harness:session actor with an empty session refuses

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc env --help =====
Print shell exports for a detected harness session, for `eval`: `eval "$(arc env)"`.

Detection reads the session variable a harness exports for itself — `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` or `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CODEX_THREAD_ID`, `OPENCODE_SESSION`, or `PI_SESSION_ID` — and then that harness's own session store for the model, and the effort where the store records one. A harness exports its session id into the processes it starts, so when several harnesses' variables are present it is the nearest ancestor that exported one that owns this process: a pi run inside a Claude Code tool shell reports pi, not the shell's claude. Where the ancestry names no single owner the ambiguity is reported and no harness, session, or model is set, rather than choosing by variable order; an ancestry that cannot be read, or that shows no exporter, says why beside the ambiguity. The store root is the harness's own override — `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CODEX_HOME`, `PI_CODING_AGENT_SESSION_DIR`, or `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` — before its default under `$HOME`. The store's answer for the exact canonical session id is reported with the exports: an id the store does not hold is uncorroborated; an ambiguous or unreadable lookup, one that resolves to a recording under another id, a recording file named for one session that records another, and an unread subagent lineage are unresolved, and the report names the reason, both ids included. A failed lookup is never reported as absence, nor as the absence of subagents. An unreadable Claude subagent directory or child ending leaves the model unresolved and reports the read failure.

Recordings and child metadata must be regular files; FIFOs are refused without waiting for writers. A plugin child's ending must name the parent's native session; a foreign or absent identity leaves lineage unresolved and reports the identity failure.

Every event naming a harness and session carries the store's verdict, with its reason unless corroborated, whether the session was detected or declared. Every established harness, session, and session link is exported; absent fields are explicitly unset. The resolved model is a comment and ARC_MODEL is always unset, so each write resolves its own model. Pi re-sets `PI_SESSION_FILE`, `PI_MODEL`, and `PI_REASONING_LEVEL` for every tool call, so those answer in preference to the recording while `PI_SESSION_ID` is the acting session, and a Claude subagent shares its parent's session id. An unfinished child recording leaves the invoking shell's owner ambiguous even if the parent's recording is newer, so no model is named, and no other model stands in for it; the reason names each unfinished child by the agent id and role its store records, three at most and then a count. Claude Code exposes no evidence that distinguishes a subagent's tool shell from its parent's. Completed children permit the parent's recorded model. For a corroborated Claude recording without a model selection, `arc env` and write-time model resolution re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second to allow the model-bearing record to reach disk. Other store answers return immediately. A missing recording and a recording still without a model after the wait each receive an explicit diagnostic. A recorded model that is a placeholder (unknown, <synthetic>, ...) or a session id names no model: the diagnostic names the value and no write records it, and a live Pi value of that kind leaves the recording to answer. A live Pi variable that is set but not valid Unicode is unreadable, not absent: no model is named, since the recording may predate the turn in flight, and the diagnostic names the variable. Lookup reasons omit tapes' debug-quoted native-query failure prefix before their one-line, 240-character bound. Launch configuration does not establish an observed acting model. Not every harness exports a session variable, and a harness that does may not in every mode. OpenCode v2 exports none and is recognized by `OPENCODE_TERMINAL` or its process ancestry, printing the harness export, unsetting the session and model, and leaving the session as a comment to set by hand.

With nothing to detect at all, or several harnesses and no owner the ancestry can name, it prints the export template as a comment, unsets every identity field, and exits non-zero, which is a report that identity must be set by hand rather than a failure. Every value it emits can be set directly: explicit identity always wins over a detected one.

The model comment includes the newest selection's timestamp and native id, and whether it is inside or before the recording's newest operator turn. An earlier observation may predate an effort change. ARC_MODEL stays unset so each write resolves its own model; declare one by hand with ARC_MODEL or --model only when it is known, since a write refuses a placeholder or a session id. Missing boundaries and incomplete head coverage are reported. A Codex selection outside the transcript tail is sought through at most sixteen 1 MiB metadata pages per invocation. A private checkpoint under the AI home's arc/cache/model-lookups resumes an unchanged recording; rerun env after a bounded lookup to continue. Changed recordings restart the search, and skipped records or source changes remain unresolved. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Prints the build commit and tracked-tree clean/dirty state as a comment; builds outside Git report unknown.

Usage: arc env [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc completions --help =====
Print a shell completion script to stdout

Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy.

Usage: arc completions [OPTIONS] <SHELL>

Arguments:
  <SHELL>
          Target shell
          
          [possible values: bash, elvish, fish, powershell, zsh]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`
