===== arc integrate --help =====
Explicit --provisional prepares one unsigned no-ff merge into an unchecked staging ref under refs/heads/arc/provisional/<local-replica>/<recorded-target>. Canonical policy, review and gate requirements apply. Publication and closure remain owed to the recipient, defaulting to the known authority holder. Recipient flags route an obligation and grant no authority. Provisional prerequisites satisfy local preparation only; authority publication requires canonical closures. Replacing provisional ancestry requires a content replay, a new patchset and fresh review or explicit debt. An unapplied retained reservation on an abandoned change is released by recovery only when the saved refs prove no transition occurred; the intent remains evidence. ID cursors wait until publication or this abandonment. Guarded integration of one change, or of a dependency-ordered queue named by several changes or by --tag.

Integration runs where the target is checked out. That checkout's tracked modifications refuse, staged or unstaged. Untracked or ignored paths integration does not write are left in place and named in the report; a path integration would write that is already there without being tracked is refused by name. A head the target already contains closes at the target revision that holds it, without a merge commit. When no checkout holds the target, integration takes over the change's own checkout if it still holds the change branch and leaves it on the target. Integration in a slot requires exclusive use before takeover or merge and retains it through the slot's release; a busy slot refuses before either checkout or target changes. A gate's declared environment probe runs against the content being integrated: in this checkout or the change's worktree when either holds it clean, otherwise in a checkout made for it and removed before the merge or the refusal. Probe-only checkouts skip build preparation. The integration event records when the command started on the change (`started_at`), which `arc stats --round-trip` times its integration phase from. The integrated revision and closure event are printed before retention cleanup. A retention cleanup failure, including a ref lock or a hook rejecting deletion, warns with the ref prefixes that may still hold pins and does not fail the recorded integration. Style and history constraints are validated under the target and change locks against each authorization policy reading, retaining the original flags and enforcing the destination's declared actor requirement. Contribution trailers are advisory only: a missing asserted line, or a role line carrying a placeholder or a session id, is advice to rewrite with arc rewrite trailers before integration. Review credit stays in the ledger. Approval clearance and debt derivation use destination plus operator policy; the invoking checkout's unmerged clearance setting does not apply. Destination declarations are pinned to the transition's observed revision; their actor requirement and agreement with authorization are required. Configuration disagreement refuses before merging. A queue's summary includes style and history refusals and all unattempted members. Known history, style, divergence and lifecycle constraints refuse before queue replay or gate preparation, including invoking-store and destination declared-actor requirements. Each member opens the landing store afresh, so earlier landings govern its append policy. Review refusals name the required local or external reviewer's next decision. The integrating role is this command's actor; `--on-behalf-of` records a ceremony subject beside it and never becomes the displayed integrator. Destination names select local branch refs even when a tag has the same name. Ready-to-send reuse requires matching patchset, destination branch and revision, contribution history and authorization. Its event and state retain those coordinates. Fast-forwards bind the ref update to the observed revision and update the checkout with read-tree; checkout failure attempts a guarded ref rollback. Both shapes isolate merge-shaping Git defaults. Transition failure, including checkout takeover, reports ref, HEAD, index and checkout state and preserves local and competing work. Fast-forwards changing initialized submodules refuse before Git mutation regardless of Git submodule ignore settings; use --shape no-ff or integrate manually with submodule updates. Closure publication failure reports whether the event is published, absent or unreadable; inspect publication and durability before retrying. An active claim warns as foreign unless the caller supplies a complete matching actor, harness, and session; absent or empty coordinates cannot match its owner. A change in a slot (arc guide slots) keeps its checkout under any cleanup setting: after recording, arc detaches the clean slot at the landed commit, deletes the merged branch with git branch -d, records slot-released and frees the lease; a slot in use or holding work keeps its lease and the output names the release command. With [slots] warm_after_integrate = true, a successful integration then starts one detached `arc slots warm --limit 1` when no slot's use lock is held, writing its output to warm.log in the pool root; it never blocks or fails the integration, and the output says what it started or why not.

Usage: arc integrate [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]...

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]...
          Changes to integrate. Several run as a queue, in dependency order, stopping at the first that needs a person. Omit only when selecting with --tag

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --provisional
          Prepare an unsigned merge in this replica's staging namespace; publication remains owed

      --for-replica <FOR_REPLICA>
          Route the obligation to a paired replica; grants no authority

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --for-forge <FOR_FORGE>
          Route the obligation to an opaque forge reference without transport

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --tag <TAG>
          Integrate every change carrying all supplied tags, in dependency order

      --into <INTO>
          Provisional mode selects an unchecked staging branch in the local replica namespace. Its declarations may add gates but cannot weaken canonical policy. Integrate into this branch; its gate, actor, contribution and authorization declarations govern every integration check

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --shape <SHAPE>
          Local integration shape: ff-only advances to the approved head and refuses divergence; no-ff creates a merge commit. Resolution: flag, operator defaults, target project defaults, user config, then ff-only. Project and operator allowed shapes intersect; squash history permits one commit above the member's destination, projected during queue dry runs
          
          [possible values: ff-only, no-ff]

      --message <MESSAGE>
          Merge commit message, requires resolved no-ff shape (defaults to "merge(<slug>): <title>")

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --cleanup
          Delete the merged branch and remove only a clean worktree inside the sandbox, excluding the integration checkout. Arc removes only the checkout instance it created, identified by an owner record in that checkout’s Git administrative directory. The registered checkout must still hold the change branch. An ignored directory outside .arc/ identified as a build artifact by a valid CACHEDIR.TAG, a built-in recognizer (`arc` lists them), or a configured integration.disposable rule's path and marker is eligible for removal only if a scan finds no nested Git repository and encounters no read error. This applies under either discard scope; a worktree containing another repository is never removed. The whole filesystem tree is walked independently of Git status, excluding only the checkout's own .git file at its root, without following symlinks. A recorded worktree path that is itself a symlink keeps the checkout. integration.discard defaults to artifacts; --discard ignored also discards other ignored files and directories outside .arc/. Tracked edits, untracked files and index entries marked skip-worktree or assume-unchanged keep it. Ignored .arc/ entries, including recognized artifacts, keep the checkout under either scope. An outbox holding only the spool's regular .gitignore with "*" and a trailing newline is disposable only when its Git administrative ownership record identifies the file and records successful promotion. Foreign metadata, unrecorded ownership and other outbox content keep the checkout. Kept checkouts retain checked-out branches. Cleanup failures warn after closure and do not fail integration or stop a queue. Overrides integration.cleanup; absent either flag, the resolved style default applies (built-in false)

      --discard <DISCARD>
          Select integration.discard: artifacts (built-in) discards recognized build artifacts; ignored uses the parent checkout's Git ignore rules outside .arc/. A worktree containing another repository is never removed under either scope. Cleanup walks the whole filesystem tree independently of Git status for .git entries and bare repositories (HEAD, objects/, refs/). Repository paths are named. The checkout's own .git file at its root is the only exclusion. The walk never follows symlinks; any read error keeps the checkout. Tracked edits, untracked files and index entries marked skip-worktree or assume-unchanged keep the checkout. Ignored .arc/ entries, including recognized artifacts, keep it under either scope; only owned, emptied outbox metadata is exempt. Ownership checks apply under either scope. Cleanup uses cleanup, discard and disposable values selected before the merge; a change's policy edits do not govern its own cleanup. Implies --cleanup; overrides configured discard scope for single changes and queues
          
          [possible values: artifacts, ignored]

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

      --no-cleanup
          Keep the merged branch and worktree, overriding integration.cleanup

      --dry-run
          Report what would happen without merging, closing, or writing. With `--debt`, the plan reads the change as it would once that debt is declared and says which debt it would declare; a declaration the real run would refuse is refused with the same message. Queues stop at known blockers before projection; future gates, replay and checkout guards are conditional. Style, history, approval, danger and provenance resolve against each member's projected destination. Exact-patchset review vetoes stop even an unresolved projection. After a divergent merge or replay, later destination ancestry, merged policy, style, history and authorization remain conditional. Prerequisites follow supersession successors through projected closures, rejecting missing successors and cycles

      --json
          With --dry-run, print the plan as `arc-integration-plan/1` JSON: the authorization basis the integration event would record, the target revision it was decided at, the approved head, and the tree the integration would ship, and its shape. The same decision governs the dry run and execution; a refusal prints the blocker on stderr and nothing on stdout. With `--debt`, the plan's `debt` object names the kind, reason, bound patchset and, for `ineligible-review`, the read, qualifiers and assessment basis, with `disposition` `would-declare` or `already-declared`; a debt not yet declared has no event in `authorization`. Provisional retries identify a completed preparation through reuse_event_id and reuse_commit, with merge_commit false. With provisional execution, emit `arc-provisional-transition/2` with observed refs, event publication, retained intent and recovery command. A later refusal includes any debt written or reused earlier in the invocation, including a failure to reread the ledger after declaration. A debt whose publication failed is reported too, with its `publication` observed as durable, visible-unconfirmed, absent or unknown. Applied intent recovery precedes eligibility checks for a fresh preparation. Unapplied retries enforce current readiness and the saved basis. Explicit destination or recipient conflicts refuse with the exact retry parameters. One change only

      --expect-basis <FILE>
          Compare the fresh decision with a plan an earlier `--dry-run --json` printed. The fresh readiness evaluation alone decides; the file never changes the decision. When the approved head, the target revision, the gate or policy declarations (a gate's `binds` among them), or the approval moved since, a refusal adds one line naming what moved. A permitted integration proceeds with a warning naming what moved. Provisional plans also compare recipient and prerequisite references. An unchanged basis adds nothing. One change only

      --debt <REASON>
          Integrate without an independent verdict, recording the review this change still owes. One change only: the reason binds to one patchset, so it has nothing to say about a queue. It stands in for a verdict nobody recorded — and for a self-approval policy would reject — in the same invocation. The waiver binds to the patchset's review head, following only signature-only and role-addition rewrites; any other message or content rewrite requires a fresh snapshot and declaration. The debt's production record names the ledger's planner, implementer, orchestrator and integrator. It also keeps the `Implemented-by` values of the commits in the range verbatim as a projection for comparison; a range naming none records an empty list. Trailers never decide a role. It never overrules a reviewer who read this patchset and asked for changes: that is a verdict, not a missing one. The obligation survives closure and `arc query --debt` finds it; discharge it with `arc audit`. A refused integration keeps the debt it declared, and a retry with the same reason reuses it, including a debt held in an import batch. Reuse confirms its backing publication. Divergence observed under the target and change locks refuses before declaration. External Git moving the target after that observation can retain debt. With `--dry-run` nothing is declared: the plan shows the debt. A provisional execution that refuses after the declaration ends its text output with `debt event <id> <written|reused> publication=<state>`; `--json` carries the same receipt

      --kind <DEBT_KIND>
          What kind of deficit the debt records. Omitted, arc derives it from the ledger; a declared kind wins, because arc cannot tell a merge resolution from a repair. `ineligible-review` records that a read happened by a reviewer routing does not count as independent of the author: arc never derives it, and it takes why from `--assessment` or `--qualifier`, and the read from `--read`. `replayed-unread` is the reverse: only `arc land` derives it, and declaring it is refused

          Possible values:
          - nothing-read:            No verdict on any patchset of the change
          - merge-resolution-unread: An approved patchset, then a merge or rebase resolution nobody read
          - repair-unread:           An approved patchset, then authored work nobody read
          - replayed-unread:         An approved patchset that `arc land` replayed without conflict onto a base it was not reviewed on: the edit was read, the context it landed in was not. Derived, never declared
          - contributor-only:        Verdicts on the shipped patchset, all of them from its contributors
          - ineligible-review:       A read happened, by a reviewer routing does not count as independent of the author; the record names the read and why
          - independent-review:      A read by somebody independent, which nobody supplied. Also what an obligation recorded before kinds existed means

      --read <EVENT>
          The verdict event of the read an `ineligible-review` debt names. Omitted, the latest verdict on the patchset the debt covers. Refused when the patchset has no verdict, or the event is not one of its verdicts

      --assessment <FILE>
          Why routing did not count that read: an `agent-routing assess --json` file (schema `agent-routing-assessment/1`) whose independence is `ineligible`. arc keeps its qualifiers, route version and independence, and never its tiers; it validates the shape and computes no family, tier or floor. The record reads as assessed. An eligible assessment is refused: there is no debt to record

      --qualifier <QUALIFIER>
          A qualifier named by hand, repeatable: `same-model`, `same-family`, `below-floor`, `floor-unknown`, `reviewer-unseated`. The record reads as declared, which nothing corroborates

          Possible values:
          - same-model:        The reviewer and an author are the same model, at any effort
          - same-family:       The reviewer is in an author's model family
          - below-floor:       The reviewer's review tier is below an author's floor
          - floor-unknown:     An author's floor is unestablished
          - reviewer-unseated: The reviewer holds no seated review tier, so no floor can place it
          - below-clearance:   The reviewer's review tier at its effort is below the clearance floor: its read may find defects but does not clear

      --route-version <VERSION>
          The routing version that assessed the read, with `--qualifier`. Recorded as a coordinate and nothing else

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

Arc exits 11 when --shape ff-only encounters a diverged target; run arc rebase <change> --verify and provide fresh review or declared debt. When --into selects a different destination, arc rebase cannot select it: run git rebase <destination> in the change checkout, then arc snapshot <change> --verify and obtain fresh review or debt. Arc exits 17 when a paired replica does not hold integration authority; the refusal names the holder or an offer in flight.

===== arc land --help =====
Land several changes on their target in one guarded transition

The members land in dependency order: each after every member it is `blocked_by`, ties in the order given; an earlier member satisfies a prerequisite. A member whose recorded base is the revision before it (the target for the first, the previous member's landed head after) lands as it is; when every member does, the landing is a stack. Any other member is replayed with `git replay` onto that revision, and the landing is composed: its head is pinned by `refs/arc/landing/<attempt>` and each member's original and landed commits are recorded in the attempt's intent alone, never as a rewrite. A replay that conflicts stops the attempt and names `arc rebase <member>`. A replayed member whose own approval read its exact patchset lands only with `--debt`, carrying `replayed-unread`. A member no approval covers carries the debt `integrate --debt` would declare, including an ineligible read's event, findings reason, qualifiers and assessment basis. Every open member of each must-land-together component is named. Each member keeps its own approval and is judged against the destination's policy at the revision the landing starts from. Planning and execution refuse an empty required-gate union (exit 5) before preparing an attempt, for either shape. The gates every member requires run once: a stack's planned gate union at its top head, using the selected destination even when the recorded target differs; a composed landing's at its composed head in a checkout the attempt owns (a leased slot when `[slots]` is declared, else a worktree of its own), with the attempt on their evidence; a gate two members declare differently refuses, and so, for a stack, does one the top head does not declare the same way. Under the commit guards everything pinned is read again and any difference refuses naming the field. The target and `refs/arc/landing-applied/<attempt>` move in one ref transaction, the target checkout follows, and one `landing-completed` event closes every member: each reads integrated via the landing, with its own range. Until then the members show `pending landing <attempt>` and refuse every other terminal transition. See `arc guide change-landing`

Usage: arc land [OPTIONS] [CHANGES]...

Arguments:
  [CHANGES]...
          The changes to land; prerequisites among them come first, and the order given breaks ties

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --target <TARGET>
          The branch to land on, for every member, as `integrate --into` names one; without it the members must share a recorded target

      --debt <REASON>
          Land members no approval covers, declaring for each the debt `arc integrate --debt` would, and replayed members whose approval read another base, declaring `replayed-unread` naming that approval; each debt is carried inside the completion

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --dry-run
          Print the plan without writing anything

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --json
          With --dry-run, print the plan as `arc-landing-plan/1` JSON

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --recover <ATTEMPT>
          Finish an interrupted attempt: publish its saved completion when its applied ref exists, abandon it when the target never moved, and otherwise report the movement and close nothing. A finished attempt frees the slot it leased, its checkout left detached. Running it again changes nothing

      --abandon <ATTEMPT>
          Abandon an attempt whose target arc did not move, freeing the slot it leased; needs --reason

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --reason <REASON>
          Why the attempt is abandoned

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --max-attempts <N>
          How many attempts the landing may make, its first included. When a gate fails and more remain, arc splits it between must-land-together components: a prefix and a suffix of their dependency order, each a new attempt naming its parent, the suffix only once the prefix landed, and a failed child split again. Finding every failing one of n components takes at most 2n-1
          
          [default: 1]

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

Exit status: 0 when the landing completed, recovered, or was abandoned as asked; a refusal before anything moved exits with its blocker's `arc check` code (3 an approval a replay would outrun without --debt, 7 prerequisite, 11 a replay that conflicts, 19 lands-with, 20 a pending landing, 5 no required gates or a gate failed); 1 for any other refusal, including a field that moved during the gate or an attempt recovery cannot finish; 17 when a paired replica does not hold integration authority.

===== arc debt --help =====
Record a review obligation this change carries but has not discharged

A waiver binds to the patchset's review head, following only signature-only and role-addition rewrites. Any other message or content rewrite requires a fresh snapshot and declaration before debt can waive review of that head. The production record names the ledger's planner, implementer, orchestrator and integrator. It also keeps the `Implemented-by` values of the commits in the range verbatim as a projection for comparison; a range naming none records an empty list. Trailers never decide a role.

With `--extent` or `--extent-paths` the debt names what the owed review must cover, resolved against the patchset it binds to, or once the change closed against what shipped: for a member landed by replay, the range it landed as. Only a read whose extent covers it discharges it; any other read is listed beside it as partial coverage (`arc guide debt-obligations`).

Usage: arc debt [OPTIONS] --reason <REASON> <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>
          Change that owes the review

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --reason <REASON>
          What review is owed, and why it could not run. The debt already in force, declared again unchanged, is reused rather than recorded twice. Extents compare at their signature-only or role-addition successors; confirmation syncs the unchanged backing publication. Imported obligations confirm the event in its backing import batch

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --kind <KIND>
          What kind of deficit this records. Omitted, arc derives it from the ledger; a declared kind wins, because arc cannot tell a merge resolution from a repair. `ineligible-review` records that a read happened by a reviewer routing does not count as independent of the author: arc never derives it, and it takes why from `--assessment` or `--qualifier`, and the read from `--read`. `replayed-unread` is the reverse: only `arc land` derives it, and declaring it is refused

          Possible values:
          - nothing-read:            No verdict on any patchset of the change
          - merge-resolution-unread: An approved patchset, then a merge or rebase resolution nobody read
          - repair-unread:           An approved patchset, then authored work nobody read
          - replayed-unread:         An approved patchset that `arc land` replayed without conflict onto a base it was not reviewed on: the edit was read, the context it landed in was not. Derived, never declared
          - contributor-only:        Verdicts on the shipped patchset, all of them from its contributors
          - ineligible-review:       A read happened, by a reviewer routing does not count as independent of the author; the record names the read and why
          - independent-review:      A read by somebody independent, which nobody supplied. Also what an obligation recorded before kinds existed means

      --read <EVENT>
          The verdict or audit event of the read an `ineligible-review` debt names: a verdict on the patchset the debt covers or, once the change integrated, an audit of what shipped. Omitted, the latest such verdict, else the latest audit. Refused when there is neither, or the event is not one of them

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --assessment <FILE>
          Why routing did not count that read: an `agent-routing assess --json` file (schema `agent-routing-assessment/1`) whose independence is `ineligible`. arc keeps its qualifiers, route version and independence, and never its tiers; it validates the shape and computes no family, tier or floor. The record reads as assessed. An eligible assessment is refused: there is no debt to record

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --qualifier <QUALIFIER>
          A qualifier named by hand, repeatable: `same-model`, `same-family`, `below-floor`, `floor-unknown`, `reviewer-unseated`. The record reads as declared, which nothing corroborates

          Possible values:
          - same-model:        The reviewer and an author are the same model, at any effort
          - same-family:       The reviewer is in an author's model family
          - below-floor:       The reviewer's review tier is below an author's floor
          - floor-unknown:     An author's floor is unestablished
          - reviewer-unseated: The reviewer holds no seated review tier, so no floor can place it
          - below-clearance:   The reviewer's review tier at its effort is below the clearance floor: its read may find defects but does not clear

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --route-version <VERSION>
          The routing version that assessed the read, with `--qualifier`. Recorded as a coordinate and nothing else

      --extent <whole>
          What the owed review must cover: the whole subject, which is the patchset the debt binds to (once the change closed, what shipped; for a member landed by replay, the range it landed as), in the domain `--extent-domain` names. With neither this nor `--extent-paths`, the obligation's extent is not established, and a read naming one discharges it only when whole

          Possible values:
          - whole: The whole subject: every entry of the domain

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --extent-paths <GLOB>...
          What the owed review must cover: the entries of the subject these path globs match (`**` spans directories, `*` stops at one, a trailing `/` names a subtree). Each glob resolves, before anything is written, to every matching path with its blob at the subject's revision, and in the diff domain to each deletion with its blob at the base. The glob is never stored. A glob matching nothing is refused

      --extent-exclude <GLOB>...
          Entries `--extent-paths` leaves out, as globs resolved the same way and recorded as the extent's exclusions. A glob matching nothing, or exclusions leaving no entry, is refused

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

      --extent-domain <DOMAIN>
          The extent's domain: `diff`, the change from its patchset's base to its head (the default), or `tree`, the candidate tree at that head. A read of one domain never covers an obligation of the other

          Possible values:
          - diff: The change's diff from its base to its head
          - tree: The candidate tree at the change's head

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc audit --help =====
Record a review performed after integration (never a late verdict)

An audit discharges a debt when it approves or requests changes and its auditor is independent of the work's ledger authorship: the patchset's declared contributors, else its effective author, and its production record's implementer. Planning and orchestrating never refuse; the audit records those roles beside the verdict. A `changes-requested` audit is the review the debt owed; its findings carry what remains. A `comment-only` audit judged nothing: it is listed as a read and the debt stays owed. An audit whose `--assessment` says `ineligible` discharges no debt of any kind, whatever actor records it. For either verdict, a debt of kind `ineligible-review` also needs routing's word that the auditor is eligible: `--assessment <file>` with `independence: eligible`. Without one, the audit is recorded and listed beside the obligation, which stays open. The assessment must also describe the auditor that records the audit: the audit's recorded model, and its effort where both name one, must match the reviewer's `identities` and `effort`. Otherwise the audit is recorded, says why, and is listed beside the obligation, which stays open. Every other kind, read without an assessment, is judged by that authorship rule alone.

Without an applicable ladder, under `[policy] review_clearance = "assessed"` an audit discharges a debt of any kind only when it clears: a person's audit by the authorship rule alone (an `ineligible-review` debt still needs an eligible, bound assessment); an agent's only when it records a model with an effort and its `--assessment` (`agent-routing-assessment/2`) says `clears: true` and is `bound`. Any other audit is a findings read: recorded with its verdict and findings, listed beside the obligation with kind `findings`, and naming the remedy `agent-routing assess --purpose clearance --json`. `independence` is not read there.

After writing, the audit prints `debt discharged` when it discharged the debt, `debt already discharged by <event>` when an earlier read had, and otherwise what the debt still owes and why this read leaves it owed.

A supplied assessment under assessed policy, and the assessment an ineligible-review debt otherwise requires, binds only when its authors answer for the model and effort of every `Implemented-by` line in the audited range: an author entry covers a line at its own effort, or at any effort when it names none. An assessment that omits one is recorded with binding `authors-uncovered`, names the omitted `model[#effort]` coordinates, and leaves the debt open. When the range's `Implemented-by` lines cannot be established (the production record kept none and the range does not read) the binding is `authors-unread` and the debt stays open too.

Unless the dated tie-break election in `arc guide debt-review` applies, an approving audit recorded under the model of the ledger's implementer of the audited range, at any effort, warns that it shows a review happened and not that it was independent of whoever wrote the work. Where the ledger records no implementer model, the audit says so and is judged by the actor, session and harness; `Implemented-by` trailers are reported as declared text only. A debt recorded before production summaries existed still reads the shipped patchset's ledger authorship, including a historical integrated closure that recorded only its integrated commit. Where `forbid_self_approval` is on it is refused, with the remedy a contributor match gets. Such a read never discharges review debt, regardless of self-approval policy: permission to approve does not establish independent coverage. `[policy] tiebreak_reviewers` is read from the destination's current committed policy layered with operator policy, never the candidate. Only mixed-model work qualifies; recorded model#effort and any observed coordinate must match an entry in force at created_at. Contributor, actor, authoring-session and assumed-identity bars remain. An unavailable election grants no exception; ordinary audit rules decide whether the read can record, and a recorded audit reports the lookup failure. The audit retains its election and destination, or absence, despite later date edits; capability and coverage requirements still apply. `--verdict changes-requested` is open to anyone. The warning, the refusal, debt discharge, and the planner and orchestrator roles the audit records its auditor holding compare the model the audit records and, where the auditor's session store observed another, the observed one: either matching counts, and the warning, refusal or discharge bar names which model matched, with the declared model beside an observed one.

With `--extent` or `--extent-paths` the audit records what it read, resolved against what the change shipped: its patchset, or for a member landed by replay the range the landing replayed it as. It discharges a debt only when that extent covers the debt's, and is otherwise listed beside the obligation as partial coverage, as is an audit naming no extent against a debt that names one (`arc guide debt-obligations`). Under assessed ladder policy, a clearing agent discharges debt without an assessment. A person auditing ineligible-review still needs an eligible bound assessment. Audits of a ladder-changing range use its pre-integration registry; other audits use the destination head. Read-time debt projection keeps the stricter checkout or target clearance policy. `arc guide debt-clearance` defines the complete rule and cutoff.

Usage: arc audit [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change whose integrated revision was reviewed

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --verdict <VERDICT>
          The auditor's verdict on the integrated revision
          
          [possible values: approved, changes-requested, comment-only]

      --body <BODY>
          Inline body text

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Read body from file ('-' for stdin)

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --findings-json <FINDINGS_JSON>
          Findings batch: a path to a JSON array, or '-' for stdin, in the shape `arc review --help` states for `--findings-json`, without an `anchor`: an audited revision has no patchset diff to anchor to

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --route-version <VERSION>
          The routing version that selected this auditor. Recorded as a coordinate and nothing else. Omitted, the audit is unrouted

      --assessment <FILE>
          Routing's assessment of this auditor: an `agent-routing assess --json` file (schema `agent-routing-assessment/1` or `/2`). arc keeps its independence, qualifiers and route version, a `/2` file's `findings` and `clears` beside them, never its tiers, and whether its reviewer `identities` (every model string routing accepts for the reviewer) and `effort` match the auditor's model: the one the auditor's session store observed wherever it has one, else the one this audit records. A declared model the store contradicts is recorded with the observed one beside it and binds nothing. Only the outcome is kept: `bound`, `model-mismatch`, `effort-mismatch`, `model-unrecorded` (a person, for example) or `identities-unstated`. A debt of kind `ineligible-review` is discharged only by an audit whose assessment says `eligible` and is `bound`; otherwise the audit is recorded and listed beside the obligation, which stays open. The auditor must also be independent of the ledger's authorship, as for any audit. An `ineligible` assessment leaves a debt of every kind owed; other kinds need no assessment

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --extent <whole>
          What the auditor read: the whole subject, which is what the change shipped (its patchset, or for a member landed by replay the range the landing replayed it as), in the domain `--extent-domain` names. With neither this nor `--extent-paths`, the audit's extent is not established, and it discharges no obligation that names one

          Possible values:
          - whole: The whole subject: every entry of the domain

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --extent-paths <GLOB>...
          What the auditor read: the entries of the subject these path globs match (`**` spans directories, `*` stops at one, a trailing `/` names a subtree). Each glob resolves, before anything is written, to every matching path with its blob at the subject's revision, and in the diff domain to each deletion with its blob at the base. The glob is never stored. A glob matching nothing is refused

      --extent-exclude <GLOB>...
          Entries `--extent-paths` leaves out, as globs resolved the same way and recorded as the extent's exclusions. A glob matching nothing, or exclusions leaving no entry, is refused

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

      --extent-domain <DOMAIN>
          The extent's domain: `diff`, the change from its patchset's base to its head (the default), or `tree`, the candidate tree at that head. A read of one domain never covers an obligation of the other

          Possible values:
          - diff: The change's diff from its base to its head
          - tree: The candidate tree at the change's head

      --receipt <FILE>
          Record every audit item of an `arc-review-receipt/1` file from `arc receipt template --audit`, each on its own integrated change, for the reviewer the receipt names (`arc receipt --help` has the shape). Each item is recorded as `--verdict` records one, and discharges exactly what that audit would; a read that discharges nothing is recorded and reports its bar. A receipt holding verdict items, or both kinds, is refused. Exits 1 when any item was refused; the others still record

      --json
          With --receipt, emit `arc-receipt-result/1`

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc close --help =====
Close a change without arc performing the merge

The change and closure event are printed before retention cleanup. A retention cleanup failure, including a ref lock or a hook rejecting deletion, warns with the ref prefixes that may still hold pins; the recorded closure succeeds. A change in a slot (arc guide slots) has a clean checkout detached at its HEAD, its branch kept, slot-released recorded and the lease freed; a slot in use, or holding uncommitted or untracked work, keeps its lease and the output says so.

Usage: arc close [OPTIONS] <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>
          Change to close

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --assert-integrated <ASSERT_INTEGRATED>
          Assert an integration arc did not perform, at this revision. Carries no authorization: arc did not guard this merge

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --patchset <PATCHSET>
          The patchset that was integrated (defaults to the latest)

      --into <INTO>
          The branch it was integrated into (defaults to the target branch)

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --target-before <TARGET_BEFORE>
          Assert where the target stood before, naming an ancestor of the asserted revision regardless of its parent count. If omitted, a merge head supplies its first parent; other heads record no base

      --correspondence <FILE>
          Assert that the patchset's commits landed as other commits (a cherry-pick, or a rebase arc did not perform), in place of the asserted revision containing the source head. FILE is `arc-correspondence/1` JSON: {"schema", "change" (the change id), "patchset", "source_head", "target_before", "revision", "commits": [{"source", "landed"}]}. The binding fields must name this change, the closed patchset and its head, --target-before (required) and the asserted revision. Every commit of the patchset, oldest first, maps to one commit in target-before..revision, one to one, each landed commit descending from the one before it, with equal stable Git patch ids. A merge or an empty commit on either side is refused. Each refusal names the commit and the rule. The closure stays asserted, carries no gate evidence and records the pairs

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --abandoned
          Close as abandoned: the work stopped and nothing was merged

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --superseded <SUPERSEDED>
          Superseded by another change

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --external-reference <EXTERNAL_REFERENCE>
          Opaque upstream reference for an external approval at this patchset

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc candidate --help =====
Register, show, list, judge, and retire candidates: alternative answers to a brief, recorded without opening a change

Usage: arc candidate [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:
  register  Register a candidate: a tree answering one version of a change's brief, with who produced it and where it came from
  show      Show one candidate: tree, contract, producers, parents, adoptions, episodes, judgements, retirement, whether its pin holds the tree, evaluations, selections naming it (promoted, superseded, or not promoted), promotion refs no `candidate-promoted` event records, and every registration sharing its tree
  list      List registrations as `show` renders them, received evidence included and labelled external, grouping every tree more than one registration names under `shared_trees`
  judge     Record a judgement of a candidate by its declarant: rejected, or superseded by another registration
  retire    Delete `refs/arc/candidate/<id>` and record `candidate-retired`, only when no root reaches the candidate
  verify    Evaluate a candidate: run the required gates of its brief's change, as that change's target declares them, against the candidate's tree
  select    Validate a named selection and, when every ground holds, record it and promote the chosen candidate into its brief's change
  promote   Promote a recorded selection, or finish one an interrupted run left
  help      Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Typed observation admission preserves coverage, acquisition time and generation references; legacy flag forms assert unretained facts with unknown acquisition time. Show resolved paths and style values with sources as arc-config/1 JSON

Style precedence: flag, operator defaults, HEAD project defaults, user, builtin. Style inspection parses user configuration and repository policy; malformed style or constraint tables and known keys refuse with file and key. ===== arc doctor --help =====
Check the append-only ledger for malformed or stale state (read-only)

Reports the build commit and tracked-tree clean/dirty state in text and arc-doctor/6 JSON; builds outside Git report unknown. An unreadable or malformed project or operator policy is the problem `policy-unreadable`, naming the file and error. Unfinished provisional transitions report their merge, refs, event state, retained intent and exact recovery command as problems. A `refs/arc/retained` ref whose object is gone is the problem `retained-object-missing`; a commit the ledger cites as a patchset head or a verdict's revision, that a forge fact records as the provider's own, or that a checkpoint names, that no ref reaches is the advice `unretained-commit`, with the `git update-ref` that holds it. A custody file whose bytes do not hash to its name is the advice `custody-content-mismatch`, and a temporary file an interrupted custody write left over an hour ago is the advice `custody-temp-orphaned`; neither is removed. A receipt a read cites whose bytes custody does not hold is the advice `receipt-unavailable`, one whose held bytes fail verification `receipt-corrupt`, and a receipt reference that records no byte length `receipt-length-unrecorded`; each names the read, its change, the receipt key and digest. An unreadable custody directory, or one that is a symlink, is the problem `custody-unreadable`. A failed reachability walk is the problem `retention-reachability-failed`; the report retains all findings collected before that failure. A change whose events fail to reduce is the problem `unreplayable-change`, naming the change with the reduction error. Admitted active rewrites prove falsification identity without advancing recorded revisions; dangling revision diagnostics still inspect the recorded objects, a checkpoint's revision and recorded change heads among them, each finding naming the checkpoint. A reference replay set aside because only a withdrawn rewrite map carried it is the advice `set-aside-reference`, naming the change, the event, both revisions and the recovery. A hold release that names no active hold is the advice `hold-release-names-no-hold`; a change already reported with a problem is not checked for one. Under `[policy] review_clearance = "assessed"`, a debt it leaves owed that a read would discharge without it is the advice `clearance-keeps-debt-owed`, naming the change, the debt, that read and the debt's findings reads.

Exits 0 when the ledger is clean or carries only advice, and 1 when it reports a problem. A ledger that cannot be read at all also exits 1, with the error on stderr and no report; a usage error exits 2. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy.

Usage: arc doctor [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --json
          Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --verbose
          Show every item behind grouped advice

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

===== arc workspace --help =====
Aggregate changes, inboxes, or backlog across every known project, and retire or restore a project's registration

Usage: arc workspace [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:
  gates      Gate declarations and costs from each registered project's own ledger
  list       Per-repo open-change rows across the data_root
  inbox      The inbox rollup for every registered project, including the count of open actionable filings untouched for more than 14 days
  pins       Pinned journal artifacts across projects: each project's standing references as `arc journal pins` lists them, grouped by project, with the operator journal's last when no scope narrows the view
  inventory  Every artifact across the selected stores, each with why it is where it is: present, terminal, archived, or superseded
  backlog    Ledger and journal backlog across every project, ranked by what is blocked on a decision rather than on work
  report     The backlog classified by named rules: each artifact's status, the section it belongs to, and the facts that deserve attention
  watch      Emit the workspace report as one JSON line, then again each time arc records something that changes it
  retire     Retire a registration whose project is gone, recording why, so the workspace views stop observing it
  restore    End a registration's retirement, recording why
  help       Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`

===== arc catchup --help =====
Orient a session: ledger queue, journal backlog, live lanes, and recent sessions across harnesses.

Recent activity without filed artifacts or a naming handoff/conclusion is marked unfiled. Filing counts include direct writes and published transition successors, with import and spool provenance. Stale filings name the `journal filed` query; plain reads record no activity. Project memories precede global memory titles with their ages in days; bodies are read on demand with journal show --global. An unavailable journal carries its own warning, including global_memory_error or project_journal_error in JSON; readable sources remain in the output. Each recording backend is queried once with an unrestricted candidate window. Backend scan bounds and failures appear in session_discovery_errors; readable rows remain ranked by recorded activity. Pending transitions carry a diagnostic and do not count as filings or naming handoffs/conclusions. Publication requires the supersedes link and a complete staged body match or the source's recorded retirement; a readable successor prefix is insufficient. Text warns when the running build differs from HEAD in an arc-ledger checkout: older for an ancestor, differs otherwise, with dirty builds identified. Other projects and unknown build revisions have no warning. Unreadable changes are named with their errors on stderr; readable changes remain in the output The worktree filesystem is measured at the configured worktree root; a configuration that cannot be loaded measures the current checkout and warns on stderr Provisional integration obligations have a separate section and do not count as review debt. Recorded producer models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded. JSON production identities and review coverage retain raw values with model_recorded: false; valid models omit the flag. JSON recent sessions retain raw models with model_recorded: false when the model is missing, a placeholder or a session id; valid models omit it.

Human ledger sections name guide topics for the blockers already observed.

Each intake configured as `[intake.<name>]` in the user config adds one line, `intake: <name>: <n> open items`, and the JSON field `intake` lists `{name, open}` per intake. Catchup only counts: it never reads, prints, or triages an item. A command that cannot start, exits non-zero, overruns 2 s, or prints no top-level integer `total` gives `intake: <name>: unavailable (<reason>)` and `{name, unavailable}`, and leaves the exit code alone. Nothing configured prints no intake line.

Usage: arc catchup [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sessions <SESSIONS>
          Number of recent sessions, selected after ranking every discovered candidate by its last recorded activity rather than file modification time. Backend scan bounds can hide sessions that would rank inside this limit
          
          [default: 5]

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --limit <LIMIT>
          Cap the changes listed per ledger bucket; the journal queue is always rendered in full, since finding it is the point
          
          [default: 10]

      --json
          Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc fr --help =====
File a feature request in the project journal: the discoverable alias for `arc journal note --kind feature-request`.

Read the queue back with `arc journal open` or `arc journal list --kind feature-request`

Usage: arc fr [OPTIONS] <TOPIC>

Arguments:
  <TOPIC>
          Kebab-case topic slug

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (written verbatim)

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --title <TITLE>
          Title for the `# <title>` heading; a body opening on prose is otherwise headed from the topic slug

      --scaffold <SCAFFOLD>
          Scaffold template around the body (.arc/templates/<name>.md or a built-in: sol-low, sol-high, reviewer, discussion). The body takes the place of the template's `{{body}}` line, or follows a template without one

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --no-scaffold
          Record the body alone, without the kind's default scaffold

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --source <SPEC>
          What this item was distilled from, as space-separated `key=value` pairs naming either a recorded session or an intake item. A session needs `harness=<name> session=<id> ts=<rfc3339>`, with `turn=<n>`, `schema=<name>`, and `coverage=<state>` recorded only when supplied. An intake item is exactly `intake=<name> item=<id>`: a name matching `[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,31}` and one token of at most 128 bytes. The two forms never mix. No transcript text or item body enters the journal

      --item-key <KEY>
          This caller's key for one item inside that source. A later write under the same key records nothing and reports the existing item's disposition instead. The source half of that identity is the harness and session for a recorded session, and the intake and item for an intake item, whichever session writes

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --spool
          Write to the repository-local outbox instead of the journal, for a caller that cannot reach the journal directory. `arc journal spool --promote` files it later, keeping this write's identity. Cleanup ownership is optional; an unrecorded owner keeps the checkout

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --planned-by <JSON>
          Planner identity for a plan artifact; repeat once per planner. Each value is a one-line JSON object whose only fields are `actor`, `harness`, `session`, and `model`, each a nonempty string or null. An absent or null field records nothing, and at least one field must be a string. Effort is not a field: it rides in `model` as `<model>#<effort>`. Values join the body's `planned-by:` headers; omitted, a plan records those headers, or else the invoking identity. A planner it records with a placeholder harness, session, actor or model, or a session id as its model, refuses: omit the field

      --no-planner
          Preserve unknown planner authorship when transporting a plan

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal --help =====
Cross-harness project and operator journals over plain Markdown.

Events replay in append order; timestamps record observation time. Project reads also resolve from their journal directory; project writes name the checkout they need. --global works from any directory.

Usage: arc journal [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:
  dir              Print the resolved journal directory (creates nothing)
  doctor           Check the journal for malformed or stale state (read-only)
  spool            List journal writes parked in the repository-local outbox, for a caller that could not reach the journal directory
  note             Write a timestamped artifact and append its journal line
  feature-request  File a proposal nobody is building yet
  todo             Record work waiting for a session
  handoff          Hand an unfinished thread to the next session
  plan             Record a plan before it becomes work
  conclusion       Record what a piece of work concluded
  decision         Record a settled decision, which is what resolves a discussion
  incident         Record something that went wrong in the running of work, not in the code, so a decision's revisit trigger can fire on evidence
  memory           Record a durable fact, surfaced by `catchup` every session
  later            Park a proposal that is real but not now
  review           Record a review performed outside the ledger
  log              Append a log-only journal line (no artifact file is created)
  source-attach    Record a further source of an existing artifact: another recorded session, or an intake item
  source           What one recorded session, or one intake item, already produced here: every item key filed against it, its disposition, the artifact when one exists, and the events behind it (journal-source/2)
  filed            Filed artifacts grouped by their writing session, with source references, changes that took them up, and consumption outcomes (arc-journal-filed/1)
  position         Add a position block to an artifact and emit a typed `position` event
  evidence         Append an evidence block to a live artifact of any kind and emit a typed `evidence` event [alias: append]
  verified         Record that an artifact was checked against the project's source at the current anchor revision
  pin              Pin an artifact as a standing reference and emit a typed `pinned` event
  unpin            Remove an artifact's pin and emit a typed `unpinned` event, returning an actionable artifact to the queues
  pins             List pinned artifacts, most recently active first: title, reason, the count of appended blocks and the newest one, and storage when the body is not hot, and the published copy (arc-journal-pins/1)
  render           Render an artifact as a standalone HTML page for reading away from the terminal: its title as corrected, its body, every appended block under its title, and a table of contents, which a narrow screen shows folded when it has more than eight entries
  published        Record where a rendered copy of an artifact lives and emit a typed `published` event; the latest one is in force
  question         Pose a question on a discussion that only a person can settle, and emit a typed `question` event
  checkpoint       Append a work checkpoint to an artifact this session has claimed: where the work stands, what comes next, and what a successor should read
  scaffolds        List the scaffolds a write can add to its body, and print one before using it
  questions        Every open question, across the journal
  answer           Settle an open question by choosing one of its options
  suggest          Suggest one of a question's options without settling it
  delivered        Record that a caller delivered an open question to somebody
  correct          Replace one field of one recorded entry without rewriting it
  retract          Withdraw a recorded entry while leaving it visible
  reattribute      Repair the recorded authorship of one artifact's creation event, in place
  events           Dump understood journal events as newline-delimited JSON in append order
  catchup          Newest-first artifacts plus the most recently appended journal tail (read-only) Incomplete successors carry availability `pending-transition` in arc-journal-catchup/9
  memories         List live shared project memories newest first Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  open             List actionable artifacts in primary, later, and feature-request tiers
  list             List live artifacts (hot journal dir) newest first, optionally filtered by kind (read-only)
  inventory        Project one observation of journal artifacts and their orthogonal lifecycle facts
  show             Print one artifact's raw Markdown body, or one entry inside it, to stdout (read-only)
  latest           Resolve the newest artifact filed under one topic and print it, so a workflow that appends continuations under a stable topic can read its own tail back without reproducing arc's ordering rules
  discussion       Derived summary of a discussion: stance tally, participants, replies, age, and resolution with a resolver-participation flag (read-only)
  rebind           Adopt an existing journal for this project, recording the move
  stamp            Print the current UTC timestamp in the journal house format (read-only) Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  lane             Manage advisory session work lanes
  consume          Mark an artifact consumed so it leaves the `open` queue
  transition       Change a live artifact's workflow kind without rewriting history: a typed successor is written, linked back with `supersedes`, and the source is retired as superseded
  promote          Promote a live project memory into the operator journal
  archive          Move artifacts to the cold sibling archive without deleting history
  unarchive        Restore one archived artifact to hot storage
  relocate         Move artifacts out of this journal into a directory arc does not read, leaving a tombstone
  redact           Replace passages in one artifact's body in place, hot or archived
  export           Export selected journal artifacts as a versioned bundle for another replica
  import           Import arc-journal-bundle/2 or /1 from another paired replica
  help             Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc gates measure --help =====
Measure declared gates; accept a concurrent batch after five successful rounds

Prints command wall and child CPU time, spread, and physical/logical host cores. Wall far above CPU indicates waiting inside the command; spread indicates load sensitivity. Execution and admission follow arc guide gates. Runs every gate unless --gate selects one. --concurrent follows arc guide gates-together and records local acceptance. Writes no ledger events or captured output files. Gate commands may write their own build outputs. Exits 1 if any run fails or is not measured.

Usage: arc gates measure [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --concurrent
          Measure the complete concurrent set five times and record host-local acceptance

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --runs <RUNS>
          Number of runs per gate; concurrent acceptance requires five
          
          [default: 5]

      --gate <G>
          Measure only this declared gate

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`

===== arc replica id --help =====

Usage: arc replica id [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>

===== arc replica import --help =====
Import a pairing record or authority exchange from another replica.

Imported IDs stay intact; local replica issuance shares the ledger's ID order across processes. Paired stores and enrolled receivers require --publisher and --received-sha256. Pairing grants membership, never publishing permission. Accepts arc-replica-bundle/3 and /2; absent model provenance stays absent

Usage: arc replica import [OPTIONS] <INPUT>

Arguments:

Options:

===== arc receipt template --help =====
Print an `arc-review-receipt/1` template with one item per change.

Each item's `subject` pins what the reader is to read and what it had seen. A verdict item names the store, change, latest patchset, base, head and the `sha256:` digest of the patchset's brief; an audit item the store, change, integration closure event, integrated revision, source patchset and outstanding debts. Both carry the frontier: the change's verdict tips and every audit and external verdict, its open findings, and the tips of every finding's dispositions. The item's `key` hashes its kind, subject and the template's `issued_at`, so it is stable for this template. Reviewer fields and the verdict are left null for the reader to fill, as is optional `judged_at`. The reader echoes `<key>: <verdict>` in its reply to establish judgment time; otherwise `judged_at`, bounded by issuance and ingestion, supplies it. `vocabulary` names the severities a finding accepts under `findings[].severity`; `arc receipt --help` has the shape

Usage: arc receipt template [OPTIONS] <--verdict <CHANGE>...|--audit <CHANGE>...>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --verdict <CHANGE>...
          Changes whose latest patchset a verdict item reads

      --audit <CHANGE>...
          Integrated changes whose integrated revision an audit item reads

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc external verdict --help =====

A rejection of the latest patchset closes the change as abandoned. An external approval never supersedes a local refusal

Options:

===== arc slots warm --help =====
Build stale free slots ahead of the changes that will lease them.

Ranks the free slots as begin does and leases the best one not built at the target under this identity: a cold slot, even one whose HEAD is already there, or one built at another revision. Holding the slot's use lock exclusively throughout, it detaches the checkout at the target and runs [slots] warm there through the preparation and environment gates use, at the lowest CPU and I/O priority. A success that leaves the checkout reusable records the slot's built revision and frees it; a failure keeps the lease and prints the `arc slots recover` command. Warms one slot at a time. Refuses without [slots] warm. Records no events

Usage: arc slots warm [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --target <TARGET>
          The commit or branch to build (default: the primary checkout's branch head)

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --limit <LIMIT>
          How many slots to warm, one after another
          
          [default: 1]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc slots recover --help =====
Resume an interrupted lease phase. A begin whose change exists records the lease on it; one that opened no change is undone, deleting the branch it made only while it holds no commits. Detaching and releasing phases, leases of closed changes and warms finish their release. Takes exclusive slot use before lease or phase bookkeeping. An open change requires an owned, clean checkout holding its branch at its recorded worktree path, even when its lease event already exists. Refuses a busy slot, dirty or unowned checkout, an obsolete token and a lease held by a pending landing attempt, which `arc land --recover` ends

Usage: arc slots recover [OPTIONS] --token <TOKEN> <SLOT>

Arguments:
  <SLOT>
          The slot: slot-NN, or its number

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --token <TOKEN>
          The lease's current token, as `arc slots status` shows it

      --harness <HARNESS>

          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`

===== arc fork begin --help =====
Create the fork worktree and journal its marker

The registered checkout must retain its allocated Git administrative directory after checkout hooks run. A changed identity refuses the marker and keeps all worktrees. Post-checkout receives Git's creation arguments (null old revision, HEAD, 1). Population or hook failure retains the branch and worktree and reports fork adoption and discard commands without recording a marker.

Usage: arc fork begin [OPTIONS] <SLUG>

Arguments:
  <SLUG>
          Kebab-case slug naming the fork (and the fork/<slug> branch)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --from <FROM>
          Branch to fork from; omitted, the current branch, or the repository's integration branch when standing on a fork

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]
