===== arc fork retire --help =====
Record the fork's disposition and remove its worktree

Usage: arc fork retire [OPTIONS] <SLUG> <OUTCOME>

Arguments:
  <SLUG>
          The fork slug

  <OUTCOME>
          The disposition: merged, dropped, kept — with a word of why

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --keep-worktree
          Keep the worktree on disk; the default removes it, the branch always stays

      --force
          Discard untracked work the removal refuses to destroy. The operator's decision, never arc's

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`

===== arc history withdraw --help =====
Withdraw a recorded history-rewritten map by event ID; other event types are refused.

Ref moves are not undone and retention refs are kept; output names recorded moves and retention refs, or says when the map has no ref-move information. Withdrawals travel with maps in bundles and are honoured by importing replicas. A falsification reference storing a rewritten failure revision, or a receipt after integration naming the integrated commit's successor, that only a withdrawn map carried is set aside: the change stays readable, `show` reports it under `Set aside` (`set_aside` in `--json`), `doctor` lists it as the advice `set-aside-reference`, and it counts for nothing. Re-record the actual map with history rewrite to restore it

===== arc history rewrite --help =====
Record a rewrite performed elsewhere, with its commit map.

Active maps must agree; a withdrawn map does not constrain its replacement. Re-recording the actual map restores what a withdrawal set aside: falsification references that store a rewritten failure revision, and receipts after integration naming the integrated commit's successor, without editing ledger events. Moves no refs, but holds each commit the ledger cites as a patchset head, a verdict's revision, or a checkpoint's revision or recorded change head that the map replaces and this repository still has under `refs/arc/retained/<change>/<subject>/<commit>`, named by the event's `retained` map; a cited commit already gone is named and cannot be held. An append error withdraws retention refs created by that attempt only when publication is confirmed absent; a published or uncertain event keeps them, and the error reports that state

Usage: arc history rewrite [OPTIONS] --map <MAP> --reason <REASON>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --map <MAP>
          Commit map (`<old> <new>` per line, as git filter-repo writes), or '-' for stdin. An old revision may abbreviate a cited SHA-1 or SHA-256 commit from seven characters up to its full width, in either letter case; the map is recorded in lowercase and retention holds the full cited commit

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --reason <REASON>
          Why the history was rewritten

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --tool <TOOL>
          What performed the rewrite

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>

===== arc rewrite sign --help =====
Recreate every commit from --from to the branch head so it is signed by one key, moving the refs and recording the map

Map validation and append policy precede ref movement. Every moved ref is named. A recording failure with no published event restores this attempt's ref moves and retains its intent for retry. Published or uncertain events retain the intent and applied refs for recovery.

A commit that any recorded verdict or audit read, that a patchset recorded as its head, or that a checkpoint names stays reachable: each one the map replaces is held by `refs/arc/retained/<change>/<subject>/<commit>` (the subject is the patchset, or the event that recorded the verdict or the checkpoint; a checkpoint's `<change>` is `repository`), created in the transaction that moves the refs, restored with them on a failed recording, and named by the event's `retained` map. The keep pin moves to the successor; a retention ref never moves. --dry-run lists them

Usage: arc rewrite sign [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --key <KEY>
          The key to sign with; Git's configured signing key by default

      --from <FROM>
          Oldest commit to recreate; the named commit itself is rewritten. Defaults to the oldest not signed by the key in target..head on a tracked change branch, or in the whole history outside a change. An explicit range includes merged ancestry beyond this commit's parents, including target history; refusal advice retains the default or explicit selection

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --include-shared
          Permit recreating commits reachable from the target or another local or remote-tracking branch. Permission never widens the default target..head range; use --from to name an inclusive shared range. A remote-tracking ref is its own work only when its fetch source, read from `remote.<name>.fetch`, is a branch with this branch's name; a ref no refspec writes stays protected, as do target refs. Every fetch source must name this branch, including for a configured upstream. Shared commits are refused without permission before any moves, including --dry-run

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --dry-run
          For a fresh rewrite, check map consistency and append policy, print the map, every ref it would move and every commit it would retain, then stop

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --no-sign
          Recreate the commits without signing them, for a repository with no signing key

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --retag
          Recreate each annotated tag whose target was rewritten on the commit that replaced it, carrying its message, tagger and date, and signing it with the key the commits are signed with; --no-sign leaves it unsigned

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc rewrite trailers --help =====
Edit the trailers of every commit message from --from to the branch head, recreating only the commits that change and what sits on them

--drop and --append apply to every commit in the range; --edits replaces or drops one exact trailer line of one named commit, which is how a placeholder line is corrected without touching the valid lines under the same key. A recreated commit keeps its tree, author, committer and both dates, and is signed with --key unless --no-sign.

Appending complete Planned-by, Implemented-by, Reviewed-by or Orchestrated-by entries, and nothing else, keeps earlier approvals and waivers; see `arc guide rewrites-evidence`. Any other message edit leaves them stale. Run `arc snapshot` followed by a verdict on the fresh patchset, or `arc review --snapshot`, to re-approve the rewritten head. A rewrite grants no approval. Map validation and append policy precede ref movement. Every moved ref is named. A recording failure with no published event restores this attempt's ref moves and retains its intent for retry. Published or uncertain events retain the intent and applied refs for recovery.

A commit that any recorded verdict or audit read, that a patchset recorded as its head, or that a checkpoint names stays reachable: each one the map replaces is held by `refs/arc/retained/<change>/<subject>/<commit>` (the subject is the patchset, or the event that recorded the verdict or the checkpoint; a checkpoint's `<change>` is `repository`), created in the transaction that moves the refs, restored with them on a failed recording, and named by the event's `retained` map. The keep pin moves to the successor; a retention ref never moves. --dry-run lists them

Usage: arc rewrite trailers [OPTIONS] --from <FROM>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --edits <FILE>
          Replace or drop one exact trailer line of one named commit, from FILE in `arc-trailer-edits/1`; repeat for more than one file. A file's lines are `<full commit sha>`, `replace` or `drop`, the exact old trailer line and the new trailer line (empty for a drop), separated by tabs; blank lines and `#` comments are skipped, and a comment naming another `arc-trailer-edits` version refuses. A field beginning with a double quote is a JSON string: \t encodes a tab, \" a quote and \\ a backslash; unquoted fields keep literal backslashes. Quoted fields contain no carriage return or newline; malformed quoted fields refuse with the file and line. An edit applies only to its commit, which has to be in the --from range, and only to a trailer line of that commit's trailer block whose trimmed text equals the old line; the old line occurs there exactly once. `replace` puts the new line in the old one's place and `drop` removes the old line. A new line on Planned-by, Implemented-by, Reviewed-by or Orchestrated-by has to be a fresh coordinate, and on Assisted-by so does its value without a trailing `(role)`. Models equal to the invoking session refuse, including non-UUID ids. Agent harness, model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. Any violation refuses before any commit is recreated, --dry-run included, naming the file and line. Combined with --drop and --append, edits apply first, then --drop, then --append; the edits read each commit's trailer block as the commit carries it

      --drop <KEY>
          Remove every trailer with this key, matched without case; repeat for more than one

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --append <LINE>
          Add this `Key: value` line to the trailer block where it is not already there; repeat for more than one. A Planned-by, Implemented-by, Reviewed-by or Orchestrated-by value, or an Assisted-by value without its trailing `(role)`, that is no fresh coordinate (malformed, carrying a placeholder or a session id, or a person without an email) refuses before any commit is recreated, --dry-run included. A model equal to the invoking session refuses, including a non-UUID id. Agent harness, model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --from <FROM>
          Oldest commit whose trailers are edited; the named commit itself is edited and rewritten when its message or ancestry changes. The range includes merged ancestry beyond this commit's parents, including target history. Refusals name protected target commits and affected refs. Required, since a trailer edit has no default range

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --include-shared
          Permit recreating commits reachable from the target or another local or remote-tracking branch. A remote-tracking ref is its own work only when its fetch source, read from `remote.<name>.fetch`, is a branch with this branch's name; a ref no refspec writes stays protected, as do target refs. Every fetch source must name this branch, including for a configured upstream. Without permission shared commits are refused before any ref moves, including --dry-run; unchanged commits are left alone. Permission never changes the inclusive range named by --from

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --key <KEY>
          The key to sign a recreated commit with; Git's configured signing key by default

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --dry-run
          For a fresh rewrite, check map consistency and append policy, print each edit as `<short sha> <action> <old line> -> <new line>`, the map, every ref it would move and every commit it would retain, then stop

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --no-sign
          Recreate the edited commits without signing them, for a repository with no signing key

      --retag
          Recreate each annotated tag whose target was rewritten on the commit that replaced it, carrying its message, tagger and date, and signing it with the key the commits are signed with; --no-sign leaves it unsigned

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc candidate register --help =====
Register a candidate: a tree answering one version of a change's brief, with who produced it and where it came from.

Records `candidate-registered` as a repository event and pins the tree at `refs/arc/candidate/<id>`. Registering opens no change and creates no patchset. Prints `candidate: <id>`. Two registrations of one tree are two candidates that share storage and nothing else.

Refused, naming the rule and writing nothing: `duplicate-candidate` for an id already registered; `no-producers` with no --producer; `unknown-tree` for a tree or commit the object store does not hold; `unknown-brief` for a reference that resolves to no recorded brief; `unknown-parent` or `unknown-adopted` for an id not registered; `parent-other-contract` for a parent answering another change or brief version; `adoption-drops-producer`, naming them, when the producers omit anyone along the adopted registration's parent chain; `unknown-episode` for a claim not recorded on the brief's change.

--evidence-file also records `candidate-evidence-received` right after the registration and prints `received evidence: <event> (external)`: an `arc-received-evidence/1` document of what an external execution reports, written once. It carries the private run handle, the packet or brief reference, the source, tested and report heads (each a full commit and tree), provenance entries, receipt references, commands with reported results, the environment, the head the results apply to, and `omissions`. A provenance entry is one role (producer, collector or reviewer) with the native producer and the model, each with its source coordinates, and the forge account, review sender, Git author and Git committer as fields of their own; none stands in for another, and two roles behind one account are two entries. A coordinate or identity is a value or is left out: a placeholder is refused anywhere in a provenance entry or the environment identity, and a session id as a model, effort or source coordinate. A receipt is a RetainedContentRef (provider, handle, sha256, byte_length, media_type, capture); it is a claim about bytes until custody holds them. `omissions` is required: provenance, receipts, commands and environment each appear in it exactly when empty.

Received evidence is the sender's claim and never an evaluation: it records no `candidate-verified` or `verification-recorded` event, and `select` reads none of it as a gate result. Candidate producers, patchset contributors and --on-behalf-of keep their roles. --custody-dir copies each receipt's file from `DIR/sha256/<digest>` into custody once every one has the recorded length and sha256. Also refused, writing nothing: `malformed-evidence` for a document that breaks its schema or contradicts itself (one commit with two trees, a result against its exit code, an empty section not named in `omissions`); `evidence-tree-mismatch` for a held commit whose tree differs; `evidence-head-type` for a held raw tag, blob or tree used as a head. Absent heads remain external claims. Import applies the same checks before publication to each evidence event the bundle adds, and the refusal names that event. Evidence already held is not re-judged: a head that arrives later with another tree shows as `diagnostics` in `candidate show` and as `held-evidence-mismatch` advice in `arc doctor`, and blocks no import. Held original commits have retention refs across rewrites and garbage collection. A --custody-dir file that fails verification refuses before publication. With --id and --evidence-file, a retry can finish the original request only when the active registration's fields, declarant and immutable requested_evidence_sha256 match. The digest covers the typed evidence document serialized as JSON with sorted object keys. A registration without evidence, including an older record without this optional intent, establishes no retry request (`evidence-not-requested`). Old records and their evidence remain readable and importable. Held evidence must match exactly; each fact is recorded at most once. An evidence publication failure names the durable registration event.

Usage: arc candidate register [OPTIONS] --tree <TREE> --brief <BRIEF>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --tree <TREE>
          The content: a tree, or a commit, which is recorded as its tree

      --brief <BRIEF>
          The contract answered, `<change>[@<brief-event>]`; the latest brief version when no event is named. Recorded with a `sha256:` digest of the brief body

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --producer <PRODUCERS>
          Who produced the content, repeated for each; at least one

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --parent <PARENTS>
          A registration this one continues, such as the candidate a repair repairs; it must answer the same brief version. Repeatable

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --adopts <ADOPTS>
          A registration whose content this one carries into its own contract; the producers must include every producer along its parent chain. Repeatable

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --episode <EPISODES>
          A claim id recorded on the brief's change that the work ran under. Repeatable

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --id <ID>
          The candidate id; generated from the change's slug when omitted

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --evidence-file <FILE>
          An `arc-received-evidence/1` document: what an external execution reports of the candidate, recorded as the sender's claim beside the registration

      --custody-dir <DIR>
          Adapter-materialized receipt files for --evidence-file, at `DIR/sha256/<digest>`, verified before anything is recorded and copied into custody

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc candidate show --help =====
Show one candidate: tree, contract, producers, parents, adoptions, episodes, judgements, retirement, whether its pin holds the tree, evaluations, selections naming it (promoted, superseded, or not promoted), promotion refs no `candidate-promoted` event records, and every registration sharing its tree.

Evidence received from an external execution is `received_evidence` with `origin: external`: each head as reported beside `resolved_commit`, the commit's resolution through recorded rewrites (itself, its successor, or null when dropped); `tested_tree_is_registered_tree`; provenance by role; each receipt with its custody availability (available, unavailable or corrupt); commands, environment, applicability and omissions; and `diagnostics`, the heads whose commit is held here with another tree or as another kind of object (code, role, commit, detail). It is null without evidence, and none of it is an evaluation. `--json` emits `arc-candidate/1` Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy.

          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>

===== arc candidate list --help =====
List registrations as `show` renders them, received evidence included and labelled external, grouping every tree more than one registration names under `shared_trees`.

`--json` emits `arc-candidate/1` Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy.

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>

===== arc candidate retire --help =====
Delete `refs/arc/candidate/<id>` and record `candidate-retired`, only when no root reaches the candidate.

Roots are selections and promotions, and a root reaches what its candidate's parents and adoptions carry; when one does, exit 1 naming it, writing nothing. The registration stands. Retiring a retired candidate says so and exits 0. arc never retires a candidate on its own Reusing its evaluation supplies no pin root: an otherwise-unrooted supplier may retire while its registration, evaluation and basis remain evidence. Its evaluation can still answer later selections under the reuse policy; the chosen registration pins the evaluated tree.

===== arc candidate verify --help =====
Evaluate a candidate: run the required gates of its brief's change, as that change's target declares them, against the candidate's tree.

The tree is checked out in a scratch worktree, removed afterwards, and each gate's environment probe runs there first. Each gate records `candidate-verified` as a repository event keyed by the tree, with the gate, its command, timeout, and environment probe as declared when it ran, the environment identity the probe yielded, and the result; `evaluation: <event>` names it for `select --evaluation`. A hostname that cannot be read is recorded as `unknown`. These are the only evaluations: evidence received from an external execution is recorded by `register --evidence-file` and is no evaluation. Diagnostic capture follows verify: private combined stdout and stderr, 16 MiB per file and 16 completed files, with digests, sizes and explicit truncation or capture errors in the event. Exports carry metadata only. A gate command that exits 75 records `not-admitted`, as a change gate does: nothing ran, so it is no passing evaluation and is reported as unrun rather than failed. Exits 0 when every gate run passes and 1 otherwise. Refused: `unknown-candidate`; `unknown-gate` for a --gate the change is not required to pass; `target-unreadable` when the target cannot be read

Usage: arc candidate verify [OPTIONS] <ID>

Arguments:
  <ID>
          Candidate id

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --gate <GATE>
          Run only this required gate

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc candidate select --help =====
Validate a named selection and, when every ground holds, record it and promote the chosen candidate into its brief's change.

arc never chooses: the proposal names the registration, the destination, the target head decided against, and the evaluations relied on. Every failing ground is reported, one line each led by its code, exit 1 and nothing written: `reuse-policy-undeclared` without `[candidates] evaluation_reuse`; `unknown-candidate`, and `candidate-retired` for a registration whose pin was released; `destination-other-contract` when the registration answers another change's brief; `destination-closed`; `target-moved` when --target is not the target head now; `environment-unobserved` for a gate whose probe yields no identity at the shipped tree. Each required gate needs a named, passing evaluation at the shipped tree (the chosen registration's), under the declaration in force and in the environment observed now, from the chosen registration or, under `matching-coordinates`, from any registration whose tree, declaration, and environment match; otherwise, per named evaluation, `unrecorded`, `other-registration` (under `never`), `other-tree`, `other-declaration`, `environment-unrecorded`, `environment-other`, or `failed`, and `no-evaluation` for a gate none is named for. A retired supplier's evaluation may still be reused: reuse roots neither the supplier nor its ancestry, and the chosen registration's pin holds the evaluated tree. Registration, evaluation and basis records survive supplier retirement. A retired chosen registration remains refused. Received evidence satisfies no ground: whatever an external execution reports of any tree, a required gate needs an evaluation at the shipped tree. Each `--must-read` of the registration's brief version needs a tool read (`arc context read`) on the registration or one along its parent chain, by an episode one of them cites, at the required version (an artifact's body digest; a file's blob, inferred or by the digest of its bytes over the range read), covering the required extent; otherwise `partial`, `unknown-coverage`, `only-declared`, `only-supplied` (the contract's own plan or opening artifact), or `not-read`. Reads through an adoption do not count.

A permitted selection records `candidate-selected` with its basis: the chosen registration, destination head, target, the evaluation and read meeting each requirement, the reuse policy, the contributors (the producers along the parent chain), the selector, and the rationale. A selection replacing an unpromoted one for the same destination names it as superseded. The promotion then runs as `arc candidate promote` describes; its refusal leaves the selection standing without one

Usage: arc candidate select [OPTIONS] --chosen <CHOSEN> --into <INTO> --target <TARGET> --rationale <RATIONALE>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --chosen <CHOSEN>
          The registration chosen

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --into <INTO>
          The destination change: the change whose brief it answers

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --target <TARGET>
          The destination's target head the choice was made against

      --evaluation <EVALUATIONS>
          A `candidate-verified` event relied on for a required gate (repeatable)

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --rationale <RATIONALE>
          Why this candidate: text, or `@<file>`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc candidate promote --help =====
Promote a recorded selection, or finish one an interrupted run left.

The promotion holds the destination's transition lock and the repository-events lock. It re-reads the destination head and target and refuses `basis-moved` when either differs from the selection's basis, recording nothing: a basis is never reused, so select again. It refuses a checkout of the destination branch with tracked modifications, or one holding untracked or ignored paths the update would write. It commits the shipped tree onto the destination head with the selector as committer, keeps it at `refs/arc/candidate-promotion/<candidate>/<selection>`, moves the branch from the head it read (a concurrent move stops the promotion there), updates the checkout, records a patchset with the selection's contributors and a candidate link, then `candidate-promoted`.

Given a promotion ref with no `candidate-promoted` event, it records the patchset and the promotion when the branch already points at the ref's commit, and otherwise deletes the ref and says so. A promoted selection is a no-op that says so; a superseded one is refused

Usage: arc candidate promote [OPTIONS] <SELECTION>

Arguments:
  <SELECTION>
          The `candidate-selected` event

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

===== arc context read --help =====
Record a read record: a tool's record that a call succeeded and returned bytes for a path and range, within one episode of work on a subject.

The read's version is the `sha256:` of the bytes the tool returned. Coverage is the range the tool recorded: `--lines`, `--whole`, or, with neither, `unknown`, which never counts as whole. A failed call is never a read. With `--at`, the returned bytes are compared with the path's blob at that revision over the same range (lines with their terminators; the whole blob for `whole` or `unknown`): when they are equal, the blob is recorded as an inferred locator labelled `content-matches-revision`; when they differ, as under uncommitted edits or a tool that decorates what it returns, or when the revision holds no such path, no blob is recorded and the read stands on its digest alone. A path directly inside a journal directory or its cold archive is also recorded as that journal artifact, with the artifact body's digest at recording, which is compared with the read's and never assumed equal: by file name in this project's journal, and as the qualified `<journal-dir>::<file>` in another project's.

`--from-tapes <file>` reads a tool record document instead: `tapes-events/14` (`tapes events <session> --json`) or `tapes-session/19` (the `.json` of a `tapes export` bundle, which carries the same events). Legacy `tapes-events/9` and `tapes-session/14` documents are also accepted. Every tool call with a `read` member becomes a read record: its `event_id` is the record id, `read.path` the path, `read.lines` a line range or `read.whole` whole coverage (neither is `unknown`), and `read.sha256` the digest. A call with no stable event id, no path, or no digest, one that failed (`read.succeeded` false, or its own or its paired result's `status` `error` or `failed`), and a record id already recorded on the subject are each skipped with one printed line naming the event and the reason. arc reads only the file it is handed; it opens no harness store and runs no program. `read.sha256` is the digest of the text the tool returned, recorded as tapes reports it and never normalized: a tool that returns decorated text, such as Claude Code's line-numbered `Read`, has a digest that equals no file's bytes, so `content-matches-revision` does not fire for it.

A change subject's reads are events on that change; a candidate's are repository events, so every bundle carries them. Refused, writing nothing: `unknown-subject`; `unknown-episode` for a claim not recorded on the subject's change (a candidate's brief change); `duplicate-record` for a record id already recorded on the subject; `malformed-digest`; `unknown-revision` for an `--at` naming no commit.

Usage: arc context read [OPTIONS] --subject <SUBJECT> --episode <EPISODE> <--record <RECORD>|--from-tapes <FILE>>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --subject <SUBJECT>
          The change or candidate id the read belongs to

      --episode <EPISODE>
          The claim id the read happened under

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --record <RECORD>
          The tool record's stable identifier

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --path <PATH>
          The path the tool recorded

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --digest <DIGEST>
          `sha256:` over the bytes the tool returned

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --lines <LINES>
          The line range the tool recorded, `<from>-<to>`, one-based and inclusive

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --whole
          The tool recorded the whole file

      --at <AT>
          Compare the returned bytes with the path's blob at this revision

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --from-tapes <FILE>
          A `tapes-events/14` or `tapes-session/19` document to take the reads from

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc context declare --help =====
Record a declaration: the declarant's claim that a subject cites, relies on, or considers a path (optionally at a revision) or a journal artifact.

--disclosure-file verifies outgoing/readback byte identity against custody; accessible Git/forge support and withheld private assertions stay labelled. Git support names a held commit by its full lowercase object ID: an abbreviation, uppercase spelling, held tag or blob, or absent object is refused, and each commit is pinned under `refs/arc/retained/` before the declaration is recorded. It records locally and never renders or publishes externally. A declaration is attributed to its declarant, never checked against what was read, and never becomes a read. `--citation` points it at a read record on the same subject; one that resolves to no recorded read is refused as `unknown-citation`, writing nothing.

Usage: arc context declare [OPTIONS] --subject <SUBJECT> <--cites|--relies-on|--considers> <--path <PATH>|--artifact <ARTIFACT>>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --subject <SUBJECT>
          The change or candidate id the declaration is about

      --cites
          The subject cites the target

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --relies-on
          The subject relies on the target

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --considers
          The subject considered the target

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --path <PATH>
          A path

      --at <AT>
          The revision the path is meant at

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --artifact <ARTIFACT>
          A journal artifact: a file name in this project's journal, or `<journal-dir>::<file>` in another project's

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --citation <CITATION>
          A tool record id recorded as a read on the same subject

      --disclosure-file <DISCLOSURE_FILE>
          arc-disclosure/1 selection, omissions, act provenance and exact outgoing/readback bytes. Records locally; never publishes. Author, reviewer, editor, discloser and role participants refuse a placeholder or a session id; absence is null

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

      --custody-dir <CUSTODY_DIR>
          Materialized sha256 files for disclosure outgoing bytes; hashes are checked before append

      --forge-origin <FORGE_ORIGIN>
          Explicit manual-preparation origin: CHANGE:OBSERVATION:GENERATION

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc context capture --help =====
Record what the provider reported for the retention of a read record's recording (`tapes capture <session> --json` is that report).

A capture report is a claim about retention, attributed to its declarant; the latest report for a record stands. `arc explain` reads a read whose recording has no standing `pinned` report as `at risk`. The report joins the ledger of the one subject holding the record; `--subject` names it when several do. Refused, writing nothing: `unknown-record` for a record not recorded as a read; `ambiguous-record` when several subjects hold it and none is named.

===== arc checkpoint record --help =====
Record a checkpoint: the revision and tree, the branch when there is one, every open change with its current patchset and head, every live, unconsumed journal todo of this project, and the name of every todo the journal has filed, open or consumed.

Names are unique in this repository; a second record under a name refuses and names the first. The event grants and requires no authority. Recording pins nothing; a rewrite arc performs or records holds the revision and recorded change heads it replaces, with or without --tag (`arc guide ending`).

Usage: arc checkpoint record [OPTIONS] <NAME>

Arguments:
  <NAME>
          The checkpoint's name: letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-`

          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --tag
          Also write the annotated tag `checkpoint/<name>` on the revision with `git tag -a`, signed when Git's configuration signs tags. An existing tag refuses before anything is recorded and is never adopted. One no checkpoint records is what an interruption between the tag and its event leaves: delete it with `git tag -d` and record again. When the event append fails, the tag is deleted again only if the event is known absent; an event published but not confirmed durable, or one whose publication cannot be told, keeps its tag and the error says which

      --note <NOTE>
          A note kept with the checkpoint and in the tag message

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`

===== arc checkpoint show --help =====
Show a checkpoint against the current state.

Changes open at the checkpoint: integrated (at which commit, and whether that commit is inside the checkpoint revision), closed otherwise, or still open with the head then and now. Landed since: every integrated change whose integrated commit the checkpoint revision does not reach and its target branch does, by Git ancestry, whether or not it was open at the checkpoint. Ancestry Git cannot answer, for a commit this repository does not hold, is reported as unknown with its reason and never classified: a missing checkpoint revision leaves landed since unknown, and a missing integrated commit lists its change as landing unknown. Todos open at the checkpoint: consumed since (outcome and time), or still open. Filed since: todos the checkpoint did not record as filed, which are not part of it. A checkpoint recorded without that list compares filing times instead, marked approximate (`filed_since_exact: false`), and can count a todo filed in its own second. `--json` emits `arc-checkpoint/1`. Records no events.

===== arc run dispatch --help =====
Record that a caller dispatched a run through a resolved route.

Exactly one subject is named among --change, --fork, --range, and --journal: rounds are numbered within it, so a run against nothing belongs to no sequence. Prints the round and `event: <id>`, the id `run bind` and `run end` take. A follow-up or resume is its own dispatch with its own binding

Usage: arc run dispatch [OPTIONS] --route <ROUTE> --worktree <WORKTREE>

Options:

          Commit range the run is against, as <base>..<head>, for work with no ledger change

          Journal artifact the run is against: a filename in this project's journal or <journal-dir>::<file> in another's, resolved as `begin --from-journal` resolves it. Records the reference as given and the digest of the body read now; after a kind transition the `supersedes` chain leads from either name to the other

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>

===== arc run bind --help =====
Record which backend session executed a dispatched run.

Written any time after the dispatch, its ending included; prints `event: <id>`. A dispatch has one binding: the same values again record nothing and print the binding already held, and different values refuse. An empty value, a placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...), whitespace, a harness carrying ':' and a session id given as the harness refuse. Imported distinct tuples remain a binding conflict; rebinding a conflicted dispatch refuses even when the supplied tuple matches one observation. Binding values carry no '/', '\\' or '~'; refusals never echo values A held binding with a locator refuses reads and retries with a fixed diagnostic; the ledger fact is preserved. With --parent-session the executor is a native child of that session, a Claude subagent or a Codex `thread_spawn` thread, and the pinned tapes reader must find exactly one recording naming both, or the binding refuses with a fixed reason. A delegated-agents mirror row refuses and names the agent-delegate run it mirrors

Usage: arc run bind [OPTIONS] --executor-harness <H> --executor-session <ID> <DISPATCH_EVENT_ID>

Arguments:
  <DISPATCH_EVENT_ID>
          RunDispatched event ID being bound

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --executor-harness <H>
          Harness that ran the executor session, e.g. claude, codex, pi

      --executor-session <ID>
          The executor's native session id in that harness

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --handle <HANDLE>
          The delegate's handle for the run

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --forked-from <SESSION>
          Session in the same harness whose history the executor began as a copy of

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --parent-session <SESSION>
          The session that spawned the executor as its native child, with --executor-session naming the child: a Claude subagent by the agent id the Agent tool's result names (its recording is `<parent>::<agent>`), a Codex child by its own thread id, whose recording names this session as `parent_thread_id`

      --executor-role <ROLE>
          The role the executor served in; absent reads as implementer
          
          [possible values: implementer, reviewer]

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')
