===== arc cost show --help =====
Report the work cost of a change, or of a journal artifact with its `supersedes` lineage and the changes begun from any name in it.

Journal membership uses resolved journal and artifact identity: bare, qualified and equivalent directory spellings select the same work. References retain their original spelling as provenance. References outside a readable artifact's lineage need no resolution. Unavailable membership qualifies selection and withholds only dependent work totals; readable session figures remain available. Unreadable ledger records and published imports are coverage gaps beside readable peers. A query refuses only when neither target metadata nor any relevant dispatch or session can be read

Each executor session bound to a selected run, and each participant, is measured through the pinned tapes reader over its whole recording, or, when the source is unavailable, from the facts `cost retain` kept, and the report says which. A fresh read that disagrees with facts retained at the same frontier shows the conflict. A session whose read failed and that no retained facts measure is unmeasured with `usage read failed`, or with `usage read failed transiently; a later read may succeed` where tapes found its store busy or unavailable through every attempt it makes. Direct totals count each undivided session whose work the item holds, and the item's share of each divided session with a span on it; inclusive totals for a change add the journal artifact it was begun from; the union counts each session once over the selection, a divided one by the selected items' shares. A run's figure is the segments that run alone covers. Every total names its quantity kind: the dollar figure a harness wrote is a harness-calculated estimate with an unknown pricing basis, a list-price estimate prices the disjoint categories by a `tapes-price-table/1` or `/2` table (`--prices`, else `TAPES_PRICES`, else `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/tapes/prices.toml`) and the row of the model that served them, an event on a branch besides the active one by the model it names, a model's efforts priced together, a part no model is named for under uncertain attribution by no row, a figure one model served whole where its read leaves attribution uncertain with the unattributed-request reason, and a figure added up from segments by each segment's models, each share by its own recorded cache-write TTLs and reasons, never retained and absent with a reason when no table, model or row prices it. A `/2` row's long-context, time-of-day and validity terms are priced request by request from a fresh read, under the billing its recording establishes; retained facts hold no requests, so a term they could pay leaves a known subtotal. A price in doubt is a known subtotal at the least it can cost, with the most under `bound`, or `known_bound` where other reasons qualify it, in every total holding it. Token counts are resource quantities by tapes' categories. A harness's native counters add only within that harness: a raw input sum across harnesses is refused and other native sums are withheld. Tapes' disjoint categories (uncached input, cache read, cache write by TTL, output, reasoning as a subset of output, and their total) add across harnesses with each harness's convention, within one accounting basis. An unbound or conflicting dispatch withholds its work totals, and a missing amount leaves a total absent beside the known subtotal, never zero. Measurement, assignment, and identity coverage are reported apart. Every quantity names its accounting basis. Incompatible harness or basis domains withhold the combined quantity and expose partitions. Completeness is per category and is tapes' typed answer for the figure measured, never a count of its prose notes. A counter or disjoint category tapes qualifies is a known subtotal, with one fixed reason per tapes reason: a missing counter or price, a written zero price, a stopped request, an unreadable record, a read window or counter reset, an unestablished scope, an unattributed request, a category the counters do not determine, or a record carrying usage that no reading counted. Records that carry no usage, such as a harness's attachment notes, qualify nothing. A disjoint category carries the reasons tapes gives it, and the total those of its four categories. Only the reader's `--latest` selection warnings remain prose, counted as such. A recording that keeps several conversation paths, such as a rewound Pi session, counts each consumption event once across every retained branch and reports the events, their per-counter coverage and their identity. A Claude session's recorded total is reconciled per counter with the requests its family identifies: the residual is the signed difference, never clamped, with tapes' status and reasons, and a recorded total below its requests qualifies that counter. A recorded zero price beside nonzero or unknown usage is unpriced, never a measured zero. Token arithmetic preserves integers and refuses overflow. A native child a binding names is an executor row measured from its own recording by its own requests, with `parent`, roles from `--executor-role`, and its agent from its recording's served model, else its routes. A Claude session whose recording names native children with consumption of their own is measured by its own requests, because its recorded total covers them; held only in the recorded-total basis it is unmeasured (`recorded total covers its native children`), never counted beside them. A spawn a binding identified groups no overlap unless a Claude parent's family reconciliation is not `conserved`: a request both recordings hold then withholds a total holding the parent and its bound child between the larger and their sum. A bound child whose recording is gone is unmeasured (`child recording missing`). Each parent row carries `family`: its bound child rows, every child its recording names with its recording present or missing, `bound` when any dispatch binds it, and an unbound child's figure, the `unassigned` figure outside every total, absent beside its known subtotal when a child in it has no figure, and for Claude the `reconciliation`: whether its own requests and each child's add up to the family's identified union (`conserved`, `not-conserved` with `differs`, `unestablished`), with the residual, the recorded total less that union, the parent's and in no total. A delegated-agents mirror row is never listed. A recording that starts before its session's first dispatch has pre-dispatch consumption: assignment is incomplete and work totals are withheld. An unknown opening boundary likewise withholds them; session measurements remain available. A session the ledger gives one span is measured whole. Spans come only from the session's own ledger records, never from proximity: an executor's span is each bound run, from its dispatch to its recorded `run end`, open to the read frontier while it has none; a participant's span on a work item runs from its first to its last ledger event there, both inclusive; a review start (`arc review --start`) or a claim its own session records opens it; an event recorded on its behalf carries the invoker's instant and opens none. A session with several spans is cut at every span boundary into disjoint segments, read in one pass, so no request counts twice. A segment one item alone covers is that item's share; a segment several items cover, one none covers, and a request with no recorded time are unattributed, with the reasons `overlap`, `before first item`, `between items`, `after last item` and `request time unrecorded`. The shares and the unattributed part add up to the whole in the segments' accounting basis, per counter; `conservation` is `conserved` only when every counter compares, names a counter that does not add up under `differs` (`not-conserved`) and one either side leaves unknown under `uncompared` (`unestablished`). A Codex session's segments are in the summed-requests basis. A recorded total no span divides stays whole in its own basis on the session row, and the unattributed part names `recorded total not splittable`. A session that cannot be divided (`span reading unsupported`, `unmeasured`, `ledger records are unreadable`, `retained spans do not match the ledger`, `recorded on its behalf`) stays outside every share; when it also acted on work a figure does not hold, its share there is unknown and the figure is withheld, naming it. A divided session joins every figure by its items' shares, even one holding all of its work, so its unattributed part is never charged to a selection. A divided session's consumption before its first span is unattributed and withholds no share. `--allocate even` divides each segment several items cover evenly among them, and `--allocate following` gives what remains between two items to the next, labelled `allocated` beside the conserving figure; allocation is never the default. A participant's segments no item covers are estimated by its working stretches at `--idle` (default 45m): requests up to its own event serve that event's item, those after a stretch's last event serve the same item, and a stretch with no event of it stays unattributed. Estimates never apply to an executor; each is labelled `estimated` with basis `working-stretch` and its idle gap under `sessions[].split.estimate`, and figures carry the figure with estimates beside the measured one under `work[].estimated` and `union.estimated`. Lineage relatives are plain session ids, read from the whole recording. Unresolved locators and lineage notes make identity unknown; reasons are fixed categories. Recorded lineage and binding fork origins group potentially overlapping sessions transitively; exact unions of those groups are withheld beside bounds from the largest member to their sum. An origin outside the figure adds no consumption.

A participant is a session no dispatch binds whose ledger events act on the selected work; a session a dispatch binds is an executor and never a participant. One rule gives every session its roles: its bindings' and its events' kinds'. A snapshot or a verdict is a reviewer's, an integration, a debt or a closure an integrator's, a dispatch an orchestrator's; a bound session's snapshot is its bound work and names none, while its own verdicts give it the reviewer role beside its binding's. An event recorded `--on-behalf-of` a `harness:<id>` subject naming a session is the subject's event too, giving it the kind's role while the invoker keeps its own; a subject `harness:<id>:<model>[#effort] (<label>)` names the session `<id>` and declares its model. The subject names a session when its harness's tapes reader locates the id (an OpenCode `ses_…`, a Pi id as its recording names it, a Claude or Codex UUID), when that store cannot answer, when the ledger retains facts for it, or when the id is shaped as its harness shapes a session id (a UUID, a Claude cloud session's `cse_…`, an OpenCode `ses_…`) though nothing on this machine locates it, which leaves a participant without a figure (`not located on this machine`); a person, a label, a `harness:model` or any other id nothing locates names none. It is divided by its span on each work item; one with several members, one of which only events recorded on its behalf name, is not divided (`recorded on its behalf`). Its membership is every change in this ledger with an event of any kind naming it, as its session or its subject, the rule `--footprint` selects by. Undivided, it counts whole where the item holds every member; divided, its share joins each item's total and a union takes the selected items' shares. It is listed in `participants` with its whole figure, roles, span count (its members), `counted` and `share`. A recording cannot show whether its session is still running, so the view asks `agent-status` once (`ARC_AGENT_STATUS` names another program), bounded to two seconds. A session it lists, in any state, is live: its figure is as of its read frontier and is incomplete. A claude, codex or pi session absent from a snapshot its registry owner answered has ended: a fresh read of it is complete. Any other answer is unknown and incomplete with a fixed reason: no program, a failure, no answer in time, unreadable output, a snapshot with no registry owner, or a harness agent-status does not report on. Facts retained after the session ended stay complete unless it is listed again; facts retained while it was live or unknown stay incomplete, since it may have gone on past their frontier. Each participant row shows its answer under `liveness`. An executor whose every bound dispatch has a recorded `run end` has ended; one with a dispatch that has none is running and incomplete as of its frontier, naming the dispatch; an agent-status listing makes it live either way, and when unreadable ledger records could hold the missing end, only a listing qualifies it. Its facts retained before its last run ended stay incomplete. Executor rows show `liveness` with their run ends. A session may also act in other repositories' ledgers, which this ledger cannot see, and an unreadable ledger record leaves the membership incomplete, so the participant is never counted as the work's alone. Acting events without a usable harness and session are counted as `unattributed_events` and make assignment incomplete. A participant that is not measured, such as one the store cannot resolve or a subject not located on this machine, is a session row without a figure: every total it would join is absent or withheld beside its known subtotal, never complete, and it is named outside assignment coverage with its event count and reason, making assignment incomplete. A bound executor whose read fails with nothing retained likewise leaves its totals without an amount. Another session with events on the selected work but no acting kind is named outside assignment coverage with its event count, never zero. Transitions and `begin --from-journal` add no consumption. Nonportable binding metadata qualifies the affected assignment with a fixed diagnostic. `--json` emits `arc-cost/1` Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy.

Several targets and the range selectors select their union, and the union counts each session once: `--tag` every change carrying the tags, `--footprint` every change and journal item one session was bound to or dispatched and every change with an event naming it, itself or as the subject (its membership), beside its whole figure, `--since`/`--until` every change with a ledger event or run in the window (the window selects work items; a session is divided by its spans, never by the window's edges), and `--all` every change. A range that selects nothing refuses.

Text output is a table with one row per session: agent (`harness:model#effort`, from the bound dispatches' routes, else the session's own event models and the models subjects declare for it, else the model its recording says served it, effort unnamed when it served several; the harness alone when none establishes one; a coordinate that carries the row's harness as a prefix names it once; one whose prefix names another harness establishes none and is shown beside the agent, `claude (route codex:gpt-6.1-sol#high)`), roles (impl, review, orch, integ), the shortest unique session id prefix of at least eight characters, runs, spans, the disjoint token total, the money columns, and a status (`complete`, `subtotal`, `split ×N`, `shared ×N`, `live`, `unmeasured` or `withheld`); a list price in doubt shows as `least–most`. With a price table the money columns are the list-price estimate, then the harness-written estimate; without one the harness-written estimate alone, and a footer line says how to install a table. The `total` row is the union figure with its status, with a row per accounting partition when it spans several, the `total with estimates` row when a participant's estimate touches it, and with `--allocate` the allocated total beside it; `unattributed shared` holds everything of each session outside it, its unattributed part and its shares of unselected items (`unselected item`), with a line naming each session's reasons; one line counts the qualifications `--detail` explains; a `children` line per parent of native children names its bound rows, the children bound to unselected work, and the unassigned figure outside the total, and a `family` line its reconciliation and each residual counter that does not reconcile. `--detail` prints the per-category report instead. `--by` groups counted rows with one subtotal per group, never adding incompatible accounting domains. `--json` adds the table's rows, total, allocated total, unattributed figure, qualification count, groups, families and price table under `table`, each session's segments, shares, run figures and unattributed parts under `sessions[].split`, allocated figures under `work[].allocated`, the price table under `prices`, and the footprint under `footprint`.

With `--commit <rev>` the target is a commit. Git resolves the revision, and the commit maps to the change that records it: a patchset head or the integrated commit decides first, and otherwise a commit inside a change's current patchset range belongs to that change; a change closed by a landing holds its landed range, and the commits a replay landed it from carry the role `replayed`. The view embeds the change's `arc-cost/1` document unchanged and adds the commit, its position ("2 of 3 commits") and its sibling commits, and states that the change's figures are shared by those commits and that none is per commit. Each role trailer on the commit lists the sessions recorded for that role. `Implemented-by` lists the sessions bound as implementer to the change's runs whose route model, effort and a prefix naming the executor's harness dropped, equals the trailer's model. `Reviewed-by` lists the sessions bound as reviewer on the same terms and the sessions, bound or not, whose events give them the reviewer role, and `Planned-by` and `Orchestrated-by` those whose events give them the orchestrator role, whose own model equals the trailer's: the models its own events, or a subject recorded on its behalf, declared in the role, else the one model its recording says served it (`model_source`), never the invoker's model on an event recorded on its behalf. A session in the role of the trailer's harness with no such model is listed unmatched (`unestablished`). A matching session's figure is its whole figure, shared exactly as the change view shares it, with the change's share beside it when the view divides the session, and its `status` is the word the change's table gives its row, beside its `measurement` status. A trailer no recorded session answers and no session is unmatched for reads `no session recorded`, never zero. A commit no change records answers `no change records this commit`, shows no figure, and succeeds. The lookup follows the aggregate read rule: each change whose state cannot be read is named with its error on stderr and the command succeeds. A lookup that skipped one never answers that no-match: it answers `no readable change records this commit` naming them, with `lookup.status` `unresolved`; a match found beside one is shown with `lookup.status` `may-not-be-unique` and a line saying so; `lookup.unreadable` lists them, and a lookup that read every change carries no `lookup`. A commit that several changes record is refused, naming them. With `--commit`, `--json` emits `arc-cost-commit/1`, which wraps the embedded `arc-cost/1`.

Usage: arc cost show [OPTIONS] [TARGETS]...

Arguments:
  [TARGETS]...
          Change IDs or unique prefixes, and journal artifacts: a filename in this project's journal or <journal-dir>::<file>. Several targets and range selectors select their union

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --tag <TAG>
          Every change carrying this tag, as `begin --tag` and `arc metadata --tag` record it; repeat to require several

      --footprint <HARNESS:ID>
          Every change and journal item the session `harness:id` was bound to or dispatched, and every change with an event of any kind naming it, itself or as the `--on-behalf-of` subject, with that session's whole figure beside them. A native child is `claude:<parent>::<agent>`, selecting the work its bindings name; a child key no binding records is refused

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --since <SINCE>
          Every change with a ledger event at or after this journal stamp (20260101T000000Z) or RFC 3339 timestamp. The window selects work items; a session is divided by its spans, never by its edges

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --until <UNTIL>
          Every change with a ledger event at or before this instant, as `--since` reads one

      --all
          Every change in this ledger

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --by <BY>
          Group table rows, with one subtotal per group: agent, role, model, harness, change or session. Repeat or separate with commas to group by several. `change` subtotals each selected change: a divided session joins each by its share of it, and a session several selected changes hold undivided forms one group naming them. A model is grouped under the id its recording gives, namespace and case kept
          
          [possible values: agent, role, model, harness, change, session]

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --allocate <METHOD>
          Allocate what no single item holds beside the conserving figures: `even` divides requests in overlapping spans evenly among the overlapping work items; `following` gives what remains between items, after a participant's estimated parts, to the next item. Each allocated figure is labelled `allocated` beside the conserving one, which leaves those requests unattributed
          
          [possible values: even, following]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --idle <DURATION>
          The idle gap that ends a participant's working stretch, such as `45m`, `15m` or `2h`; a participant's requests no item's span holds are estimated stretch by stretch. Retained facts estimated at another gap estimate nothing

      --prices <FILE>
          A `tapes-price-table/1` or `/2` file to price every figure with; else the file `TAPES_PRICES` names, else `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/tapes/prices.toml`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

      --detail
          Print every figure by category with its reasons and coverage, in place of the table

      --commit <REV>
          A commit, resolved with Git and then to the change that records it, in place of a target; a change whose state cannot be read keeps the answer from reading as a no-match or an unqualified match

      --json
          Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc cost evidence --help =====
Write the evidence `cost show` divides its sessions by to standard output, as a `tapes-attribution-evidence/1` file tapes reads, or into a directory of such files

The selection is `cost show`'s: change ids and journal artifacts, `--tag`, `--footprint`, `--since`/`--until` and `--all`, read the same way. Export `--all` for exact figures: a participant's division rests on every piece of work it took part in, not only the selected one. The first line is the header, whose producer is `arc/<version>`. A session `cost show` gives a figure writes the evidence that decides it, every record at tier `bound`. An executor the ledger gives one run writes one `binding` with no span, which tapes reads as the whole session; one with several runs writes a `binding` per run, its span from the dispatch to the recorded `run end`, or to `written_at` while the run has none, with the run's role. A participant divided by its own events writes one `event` per event, with the role its kind gives it; one acting on a single piece of work writes a `binding` with no span, with its role when it has one. A participant the ledger does not divide, because an item only an event recorded on its behalf names, or a ledger record that could not be read, writes nothing and is counted. A session `cost show` names without a figure writes nothing and is counted. Each record's work is the change id or journal artifact as `cost show` names it, as `label`; `commits` holds the commits the change made itself (full object names), and is left out for a change that has none and for a journal artifact; `repository` is the repository's `origin` remote URL without its user information, query or fragment, and is left out when there is none or it names a place on this machine. A change's commits are those `cost note` finds for it less the current-patchset commits of every change below it in a stack, and always include its own integration commit. A change is below another when it is open or integrated and its current patchset head, different from the other's, lies in the other's patchset range; a change closed without integration is below nothing, and changes with one head exclude nothing from each other. For a change closed by a landing, its current-patchset commits, head and range here are those of its landed range. Where a dispatch records both the session that dispatched it and the executor it bound, one `lineage` record per distinct pair is written, at the earliest such dispatch: tier `bound`, `at` the dispatch, `label` the binding's handle, and `subject` the change or journal artifact. A native child is linked by its recording and writes none. The `lineage` records come first, by time, parent and child, then each session's bindings and events, by session, time and work. Two exports of an unchanged ledger differ only in `written_at`, which also ends each span of a run still open. Evidence tapes would stop reading before its end is refused and nothing is written: standard output carries one file, of at most 10,000 records after the header, and a longer export is refused naming `--output-dir`. `--output-dir DIR` writes `evidence-0001.jsonl`, `evidence-0002.jsonl`, ... into DIR, which must be absent or an empty directory and is checked before the ledger is read, and leaves standard output empty. Name order is write order; each file is the header, with one `written_at` for every file, followed by at most 10,000 records, which keep the export's order and are never split. Evidence that would need more than the 1,000 files tapes reads from a directory, or that holds a line longer than 256 KiB, which no file holds whole, is refused either way. The files are written beside DIR and moved into place together, so a failure leaves DIR as it was. Standard error summarizes the sessions written, the records by kind, the commits that still appear under several labels, the sessions without a figure, the sessions the ledger does not divide, the runs still open, and with `--output-dir` the files written. Every session is measured as `cost show` measures it, so the read takes as long. Reads the ledger and the recordings and records no event; read the file with `tapes usage SESSION --evidence FILE`, and a directory with `tapes usage SESSION --evidence DIR`

Usage: arc cost evidence [OPTIONS] [TARGETS]...

Arguments:
  [TARGETS]...
          Change IDs or unique prefixes, and journal artifacts, as `cost show` takes them. Several targets and range selectors select their union

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --tag <TAG>
          Every change carrying this tag, as `begin --tag` and `arc metadata --tag` record it; repeat to require several

      --footprint <HARNESS:ID>
          Every change and journal item the session `harness:id` was bound to or dispatched, and every change with an event of any kind naming it, itself or as the `--on-behalf-of` subject. A native child is `claude:<parent>::<agent>`

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --since <SINCE>
          Every change with a ledger event at or after this journal stamp (20260101T000000Z) or RFC 3339 timestamp

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --until <UNTIL>
          Every change with a ledger event at or before this instant, as `--since` reads one

      --all
          Every change in this ledger

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --output-dir <DIR>
          Write the evidence into this new or empty directory as `evidence-0001.jsonl`, `evidence-0002.jsonl`, ..., each a whole file of at most 10,000 records, in place of standard output

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc cost retain --help =====
Retain the normalized facts a fresh read of each bound or participating session gives: the reader and calculation versions, the source frontier, digest and opening boundary, the counters by tapes' categories with their basis, coverage and category-specific gaps (each a fixed reason, one of them that records carrying usage were not read), the disjoint categories with their own gaps, the consumption events with their coverage, the Claude residual, and any harness-written dollar figure with its pricing coverage, plus lineage relations, read failures as fixed categories, the one model the recording says served the session when it names one, whether the recording leaves its attribution uncertain, each model's disjoint categories, its efforts added together and its cache writes by TTL, where the figure is priced share by share, the segments a session with several spans was read in (bounds, covering work items and runs, request count and facts, with the remainder and the whole in the segments' basis), and the liveness `agent-status` answered for the session then (`live`, `ended` or `unknown`, as `cost show` reads it).

Relatives are plain session ids; no recording locator or raw error text is retained. Binding fork origins remain in the run records. Unavailable journal membership, unreadable ledger peers and nonportable bindings are coverage gaps; readable session identities still permit independent retention. A query refuses only when no target metadata or relevant dispatch or session is readable. The source bytes stay in the harness store. A list-price estimate is never retained. Facts identical to ones already retained for a session record nothing and print the held event; a session whose source does not answer is reported and skipped. A fresh read whose segments match retained ones at the same frontier and disagrees with them shows the conflict

===== arc cost note --help =====
Write the commit view of `cost show --commit` as a Git note on each commit of a change, under `refs/notes/arc-cost`, replacing the note each holds

The target is a change, or a commit, which stands for the change that records it. A note is a cache of one read, never a source: the ledger and its retained facts stay the truth. It opens with the time it was read and the reader revision, and it keeps every figure with its quantity kind, accounting basis, status and reasons. A dollar figure is a harness-calculated estimate on the basis shown beside it, never a charge. It says the figures are shared by the change's commits, and it carries no commit subject or change title. Its text must pass the check retained cost facts pass for transport: a line with a path separator or a control character is refused, so no path, session link or raw error text reaches a note. Rerunning replaces each note with a newer read. `--all` refreshes every commit that already holds an arc-cost note, wherever a newer read changes its text beyond its opening time, and leaves the others as they are; a noted commit no change records keeps its note. A commit is located as `cost show --commit` locates it: a target no readable change records is refused naming the unreadable changes, a noted one is left as it is with them named, and a note from a match found beside an unreadable change says the match may not be unique. The notes ref is local: arc never configures it for push, never adds it to a refspec and never pushes it. Records no ledger events

Usage: arc cost note [OPTIONS] [TARGET]

Arguments:
  [TARGET]
          Change ID or unique prefix, or a commit that a change records

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --all
          Refresh every commit that already holds an arc-cost note

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

===== arc forge show --help =====
Reconstruct recorded forge content offline.

Observation selection freezes forge content; local rewrite resolution and custody availability are current reads. Includes typed deliveries, disclosure selections, exact source/byte availability and candidate/manual acceptance relationships. Local integration and provider lifecycle are independent; closed means closed-unmerged. Coalesced event aliases remain exact sources; conflicting reads are labelled. This command records nothing and never contacts a provider.

Usage: arc forge show [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change whose recorded forge content is reconstructed

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --provider <PROVIDER>
          Recorded provider identifier

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --host <HOST>
          Recorded forge host

      --repo <REPO>
          Recorded repository as OWNER/REPO

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --pr <PR>
          Recorded positive pull request number

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --history
          Include every observation through the selected read

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --observation <OBSERVATION>
          Exact observation event or read ID; exposes historical acquisition freshness. A provider lookup applies it to the changes that hold the read, shows the others without it, and refuses only when none holds it

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --body <BODY>
          Emit exact custody bytes to stdout, metadata to stderr. JSON uses base64; unavailable bytes are null, never an invented empty body

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --json
          Emit arc-forge-view/1

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc forge observe --help =====
Admit an adapter arc-forge-observation/1 file, including failed or truncated reads.

Records references only, never fetches bytes or changes readiness. Retries of identical read IDs return the receipt. Generations use stable provider/PR/item identity across retargeting; repository/ref and URL snapshots retain the values from each read. Admission refuses invalid input. Import judges a received read against the reads before it and a received projection against the read, link and declaration it was recorded with, never against the state merged history makes current. Replay coalesces identical replica reads, retains conflicts and invalid facts with diagnostics, and quarantines their projections; it never refuses a stored fact on validation alone. --custody-dir copies adapter-materialized bytes into the ledger's custody: for each reference the document names, the file at DIR/sha256/<digest> (digest in lowercase hex), if the adapter supplied one. Each file must be a regular file inside DIR with exactly the reference's length and sha256; a symlink leaving DIR, a non-regular file or a mismatch refuses before any event or file is written. Files the document does not reference are never read, and a reference with no file stays unavailable. Arc resolves no provider handle. The receipt reads each reference as available, unavailable (no bytes held) or corrupt (held bytes fail length or sha256), and says which generation or response it supports; absent bytes are never read as empty. A retry of an already recorded read with --custody-dir publishes bytes that are missing or damaged. Custody never follows a symlink: a ledger `custody/` or `custody/sha256` that is one refuses the write.

Usage: arc forge observe [OPTIONS] --file <FILE> <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>
          Change whose append-only ledger receives the read

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --file <FILE>
          Local arc-forge-observation/1 JSON document

      --custody-dir <DIR>
          Directory of adapter-materialized files at sha256/<digest>

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --json
          Emit arc-forge-receipt/1 metadata without response bodies

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc forge checks --help =====
Record the provider-observed hosted-check rollup at an exact PR head.

The rollup binds to the current link, which retains its observation's provider/native PR identity; matching numbers or URLs do not bind a replacement native PR. Import order never rebinds it; ambiguous legacy bindings remain unknown. Applicability compares the raw head to the patchset's approval anchor. Content and trailer rewrite successors require a fresh snapshot.

Usage: arc forge checks [OPTIONS] <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>
          Change whose hosted checks were read

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --observation <OBSERVATION>
          Usable recorded observation event or read ID; explicit flags must agree. Conflicting and invalid observations are refused as projection sources. Without it, values are unretained assertions with unknown read time

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --json
          Emit an arc-forge-receipt/1 document

      --pr-head <PR_HEAD>
          Exact commit the rollup was read at

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --state <STATE>
          The rollup the forge reported
          
          [possible values: not-configured, not-triggered, pending, failed, passed]

      --link <LINK>
          Current forge-link event the checks were read at

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --detail <DETAIL>
          Free-text detail for a legacy assertion. Typed reads retain raw prose through content references and refuse an unbound detail

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`

===== arc forge pr-state --help =====
Record the provider-observed PR lifecycle state.

Local integration is independent of provider closure: closed means closed-unmerged; merged requires an observed merge SHA. Neither grants a local verdict or approval. The head is the current link's observed head, never its local successor. Applicability requires its stable provider/native PR identity; matching display URLs, numbers or refs do not identify a replacement native PR. Unbound older facts need a prior link from their recording repository and an unambiguous link history to describe the current PR.

Usage: arc forge pr-state [OPTIONS] <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>
          Change whose pull request state was read

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --observation <OBSERVATION>
          Usable recorded observation event or read ID; explicit flags must agree. Conflicting and invalid observations are refused as projection sources. Without it, values are unretained assertions with unknown read time

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --json
          Emit an arc-forge-receipt/1 document

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --state <STATE>
          The lifecycle state the forge reported
          
          [possible values: open, draft, closed, merged]

      --merge-sha <MERGE_SHA>
          Provider-reported merge SHA; required when state is merged

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --link <LINK>
          The forge-link event this state was read at (defaults to the current link); the head is taken from that link's provider observation

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc hooks install --help =====
Install the arc hook scripts into this repository's hooks dir

Both hooks are advisory and always exit 0. `post-commit` says when the commit staled an approval or landed on a closed change's branch; `prepare-commit-msg` appends an `Arc-Change: <change-id>` trailer on an open change's branch when the message lacks one. The hooks directory honours `core.hooksPath`

Usage: arc hooks install [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --force
          Replace a foreign hook, saving it as <hook>.pre-arc

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`

          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>

          [env: ARC_ROLE]

===== arc workspace gates --help =====
Gate declarations and costs from each registered project's own ledger

Uses --under/--here/--global scope and the workspace collection manifest. Retired registrations are counted without observation; --retired lists them. Unreachable registrations and unreadable gates or ledgers are named, retaining readable rows and exiting 16 on partial collection. Lists budgets and declared totals. Records no events; append policy need not parse. JSON: arc-workspace-gates/1.

Usage: arc workspace gates [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --under <PATH>
          Report only projects beneath this canonical path

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --here
          Report only projects beneath the current directory

      --global
          Report every registered project (the default)

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --retired
          List retired registrations as well as counting them

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --json
          Emit arc-workspace-gates/1 JSON

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`

===== arc workspace list --help =====
Per-repo open-change rows across the data_root

Unreadable changes are named with their errors on stderr; readable changes remain in the output Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy.

Usage: arc workspace list [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --json
          Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`

===== arc workspace backlog --help =====
Ledger and journal backlog across every project, ranked by what is blocked on a decision rather than on work.

Debt rows carry their verbatim `reason` and optional `patchset_id`, and list only debt that is owed: one declared on a change that has not integrated, or already discharged, is not backlog. A partial collection keeps the projects it did read, names each failure, and exits 16. A retired registration is counted as `collection.retired`, never observed, failed, or unreachable Change observations name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Recorded producer models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded. JSON production identities and review coverage retain raw values with model_recorded: false; valid models omit the flag.

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

          Name every actionable artifact and pending transition successor. Pending successors supply no actionable counts

===== arc workspace report --help =====
The backlog classified by named rules: each artifact's status, the section it belongs to, and the facts that deserve attention. Review-owed rows carry the effective `kind`, declared `reason`, nullable `patchset_id`, `declared_at`, and `declared_by`. Versioned `arc-workspace-report/3`. With `--previous`, tallies carry their earlier values when both collections are complete, new rows are marked when their project was observed both times, and every artifact that left the backlog is listed with its recorded reason. The baseline must have the same schema and scope. Every failed component read, including the report's ledger read, enters the collection manifest and makes the command exit 16. A retired registration is counted as `collection.retired`, never observed or failed, so its missing anchor neither fails the collection nor raises attention. Attention facts, listed below by rule, never change the exit code. The report writes nothing. Change observations name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes

Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. A separate provisional integration section carries recipient, age, refusal and reconciliation evidence. Unfinished transitions have a separate section with refs, event publication and recovery commands. Debt implementers print as model not recorded when their model is absent, a placeholder or a session id. JSON retains the raw implementer beside model_recorded: false; valid models omit the flag.

Usage: arc workspace report [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --under <PATH>
          Report only projects whose canonical anchor is beneath this path

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --here
          Report only projects beneath the current directory

      --global
          Report every registered project, the default when no scope is set

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --previous <FILE>
          An earlier `arc workspace report --json` to compare against

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --retired
          List retired registrations in scope. Without it they are only counted, as `collection.retired`

      --json
          Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --library
          Include each project's complete hot and archived journal library in JSON, with corrected titles, absolute paths, recorded dates and fates. Rows carry `pinned: true` only while pinned in that project's journal

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

Attention rules:
  delivered-unconsumed      A delivered artifact: every promotion closed, one integrated, and nothing consumed it
  stale-question            A decision question open longer than 7 days
  stale-handoff             A handoff unresolved longer than 14 days
  stale-claim               A claim that lapsed and can be reclaimed
  stale-no-patchset         A change open longer than 7 days with no patchset
  shared-plan-slice         Two or more open changes whose in-force briefs share a plan and slice, or a body digest
  worktree-outlives-change  A closed change whose separate worktree is still on disk
  debt-grew                 Review owed in a project rose since the previous report
  collection-failed         A component read failed; the collection is partial
  unreachable-anchor        A registered project whose anchor is gone and whose registration is not retired
  unreachable-scratch       Registered journals whose temporary or scratch anchors are gone, folded into one fact; `arc workspace retire` retires each
  candidates-unjudged       A promoted candidate whose sibling registrations, answering the same brief version, nobody has judged

===== arc workspace watch --help =====
Emit the workspace report as one JSON line, then again each time arc records something that changes it

Each line is `arc-workspace-report/3` as `report --json` prints it, on one line. The watch follows the workspace feed (`arc guide workspace`): once the feed has been quiet for 250 ms, or 2 s after a notice, it reads again only the projects the notices name, and emits the report when anything but its observation times changed. Between notices it sleeps on one inotify watch and holds no Git process.

Only arc's own writes reach the feed. A commit made outside arc, a moved branch, or a vanished anchor shows at the next refresh, and ages count from the read that gave each project's row.

Each standard input line is one `arc-workspace-watch-request/1` JSON object carrying `"schema"` and one request: `"previous": FILE` compares every later report with that earlier report, which must have the same schema and scope; `"previous": null` stops comparing; `"refresh": true` forgets every held read and reads every project again. A request is answered at once. One that cannot be honored is named on stderr and changes nothing.

The watch ends, exiting 0, when its standard input closes or its output loses its reader. A failed read is named on stderr and the next notice or request reads again; component failures are in each report's collection manifest, never in the exit code. The watch writes nothing.

Usage: arc workspace watch [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --under <PATH>
          Report only projects whose canonical anchor is beneath this path

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --here
          Report only projects beneath the current directory

      --global
          Report every registered project, the default when no scope is set

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --previous <FILE>
          An earlier `arc workspace report --json` to compare against from the start

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --retired
          List retired registrations in scope. Without it they are only counted, as `collection.retired`

      --library
          Include each project's complete hot and archived journal library in JSON, with corrected titles, absolute paths, recorded dates and fates. Rows carry `pinned: true` only while pinned in that project's journal; pinning or unpinning emits a report

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc workspace retire --help =====
Retire a registration whose project is gone, recording why, so the workspace views stop observing it.

The registry is derived from journal directories, so a probe repository, deleted worktree, or scratch directory leaves a registration whose anchor no longer exists, and every workspace view counts it as a failed collection. Retiring it appends a `retired` binding with the reason to the journal's `bindings.jsonl`; the directory, its events, its cold archive, and any ledger stay where they are. `backlog`, `report`, and `inventory` then count it as `collection.retired` instead of a failure and list it under `--retired`; `list` and `inbox` skip it.

It first shows what the project still holds: unconsumed actionable artifacts (with their open questions), open claims, unfinished transitions or storage moves, and the open changes and owed debts of a ledger kept under `data_root` (a ledger inside the project's Git directory went with the anchor). It refuses while the anchor exists, a ledger holds open changes or owed debts, a transition or storage move is unfinished, or a claim is active. Unresolved artifacts refuse it unless `--archive-unresolved` moves each to the cold archive, shelved as `unresolved` with the reason as its note and its expired claims ended. The final check and the binding share the journal's events lock, so an artifact filed meanwhile refuses the retirement. A refusal writes nothing and exits 1.

History stays readable: with `ARC_JOURNAL_DIR` naming the journal directory, `arc journal show <file>` reads hot, then archived artifacts, and `arc journal events` streams the log, from any directory. `arc workspace restore` ends the retirement, and so does any journal write from a checkout that resolves to this journal, which re-registers it; archived artifacts stay archived until `arc journal unarchive`. Versioned `arc-workspace-retirement/1`.

Usage: arc workspace retire [OPTIONS] --reason <REASON> <PROJECT>

Arguments:
  <PROJECT>
          The registration: its journal directory, slug, or anchor

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --reason <REASON>
          Why it is retired, recorded with the retirement and as the note on every artifact it archives

      --archive-unresolved
          Shelve unresolved artifacts in the cold archive with the retirement instead of refusing

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --dry-run
          Show what the registration holds and what would happen; write nothing. Exits 1 when the retirement would refuse

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --json
          Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal doctor --help =====
Check the journal for malformed or stale state (read-only)

Exits 0 when the journal is clean or carries only advice, and 1 when it reports a problem. A journal that cannot be read at all also exits 1, with the error on stderr and no report; a usage error exits 2. Question state and corrections follow event-log append order. Transition checks include complete successor publication evidence and source retirement, with both hot and archived bodies considered. Position and evidence blocks are matched to their typed events for every artifact kind in both storage tiers; either missing half is a problem. Indented code and unrecorded fenced entry examples are prose; recorded position, evidence, question, and answer markers terminate an open fence, and a heading repeating an entry ID already met is a quotation. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy.

Usage: arc journal doctor [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal note --help =====
Write a timestamped artifact and append its journal line

Usage: arc journal note [OPTIONS] <TOPIC>

Arguments:
  <TOPIC>
          Kebab-case topic slug

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (written verbatim)

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --title <TITLE>
          Title for the `# <title>` heading; a body opening on prose is otherwise headed from the topic slug

      --scaffold <SCAFFOLD>
          Scaffold template around the body (.arc/templates/<name>.md or a built-in: sol-low, sol-high, reviewer, discussion). The body takes the place of the template's `{{body}}` line, or follows a template without one

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --no-scaffold
          Record the body alone, without the kind's default scaffold

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --source <SPEC>
          What this item was distilled from, as space-separated `key=value` pairs naming either a recorded session or an intake item. A session needs `harness=<name> session=<id> ts=<rfc3339>`, with `turn=<n>`, `schema=<name>`, and `coverage=<state>` recorded only when supplied. An intake item is exactly `intake=<name> item=<id>`: a name matching `[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,31}` and one token of at most 128 bytes. The two forms never mix. No transcript text or item body enters the journal

      --item-key <KEY>
          This caller's key for one item inside that source. A later write under the same key records nothing and reports the existing item's disposition instead. The source half of that identity is the harness and session for a recorded session, and the intake and item for an intake item, whichever session writes

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --spool
          Write to the repository-local outbox instead of the journal, for a caller that cannot reach the journal directory. `arc journal spool --promote` files it later, keeping this write's identity. Cleanup ownership is optional; an unrecorded owner keeps the checkout

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --planned-by <JSON>
          Planner identity for a plan artifact; repeat once per planner. Each value is a one-line JSON object whose only fields are `actor`, `harness`, `session`, and `model`, each a nonempty string or null. An absent or null field records nothing, and at least one field must be a string. Effort is not a field: it rides in `model` as `<model>#<effort>`. Values join the body's `planned-by:` headers; omitted, a plan records those headers, or else the invoking identity. A planner it records with a placeholder harness, session, actor or model, or a session id as its model, refuses: omit the field

      --no-planner
          Preserve unknown planner authorship when transporting a plan

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

      --kind <KIND>
          Artifact kind (closed set). Defaults to `note`; every other kind has its own subcommand above, which is the same write said plainly. `discussion` is the exception and has none: a discussion is argued and read far more often than created, so its verbs are `position`, `question`, `answer` and the `discussion` summary, and `--kind discussion` stays the way to open one. It also brings the `discussion` scaffold unless told otherwise, which puts the body under `## The question` and ends on `## Positions`, so every position appended follows that heading
          
          [default: note]
          [possible values: note, memory, plan, handoff, review, conclusion, todo, later, discussion, decision, incident, feature-request]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

      --derive
          Prepend a `## State` section read from the repository: branch, head, distance from the change's target, dirty or clean, the worktree, the change and its claim, open queue depth, and the installed build. Refused outside a Git repository

      --checkpoint <NAME>
          Name the checkpoint this handoff stops at: a line naming it and its revision is added to the body. Refused when this repository records no checkpoint by that name, and with `--global`

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

      --no-planner
          Preserve unknown planner authorship when transporting a plan

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal incident --help =====
Record something that went wrong in the running of work, not in the code, so a decision's revisit trigger can fire on evidence

Usage: arc journal incident [OPTIONS] <TOPIC>

Arguments:
  <TOPIC>
          Kebab-case topic slug

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (written verbatim)

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --title <TITLE>
          Title for the `# <title>` heading; a body opening on prose is otherwise headed from the topic slug

      --scaffold <SCAFFOLD>
          Scaffold template around the body (.arc/templates/<name>.md or a built-in: sol-low, sol-high, reviewer, discussion). The body takes the place of the template's `{{body}}` line, or follows a template without one

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --no-scaffold
          Record the body alone, without the kind's default scaffold

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --source <SPEC>
          What this item was distilled from, as space-separated `key=value` pairs naming either a recorded session or an intake item. A session needs `harness=<name> session=<id> ts=<rfc3339>`, with `turn=<n>`, `schema=<name>`, and `coverage=<state>` recorded only when supplied. An intake item is exactly `intake=<name> item=<id>`: a name matching `[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,31}` and one token of at most 128 bytes. The two forms never mix. No transcript text or item body enters the journal

      --item-key <KEY>
          This caller's key for one item inside that source. A later write under the same key records nothing and reports the existing item's disposition instead. The source half of that identity is the harness and session for a recorded session, and the intake and item for an intake item, whichever session writes

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --spool
          Write to the repository-local outbox instead of the journal, for a caller that cannot reach the journal directory. `arc journal spool --promote` files it later, keeping this write's identity. Cleanup ownership is optional; an unrecorded owner keeps the checkout

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --planned-by <JSON>
          Planner identity for a plan artifact; repeat once per planner. Each value is a one-line JSON object whose only fields are `actor`, `harness`, `session`, and `model`, each a nonempty string or null. An absent or null field records nothing, and at least one field must be a string. Effort is not a field: it rides in `model` as `<model>#<effort>`. Values join the body's `planned-by:` headers; omitted, a plan records those headers, or else the invoking identity. A planner it records with a placeholder harness, session, actor or model, or a session id as its model, refuses: omit the field

      --no-planner
          Preserve unknown planner authorship when transporting a plan

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal log --help =====
Append a log-only journal line (no artifact file is created). Prepared appends and promotions recover before writing; unprepared torn trailing lines refuse with a command preserving their bytes. With `--source --item-key --outcome no-action` the line is the standing answer that this source item needs no work, so a later scan of the same session is answered without an artifact entering any queue

Usage: arc journal log [OPTIONS] <TOPIC> <MESSAGE>

Arguments:
  <TOPIC>
          Kebab-case topic slug

  <MESSAGE>
          Free-text journal message

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --source <SPEC>
          What this item was distilled from, as space-separated `key=value` pairs naming either a recorded session or an intake item. A session needs `harness=<name> session=<id> ts=<rfc3339>`, with `turn=<n>`, `schema=<name>`, and `coverage=<state>` recorded only when supplied. An intake item is exactly `intake=<name> item=<id>`: a name matching `[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,31}` and one token of at most 128 bytes. The two forms never mix. No transcript text or item body enters the journal

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --item-key <KEY>
          This caller's key for one item inside that source. A later write under the same key records nothing and reports the existing item's disposition instead. The source half of that identity is the harness and session for a recorded session, and the intake and item for an intake item, whichever session writes

      --outcome <OUTCOME>
          Record this source item as needing no work at all. `no-action` is the only outcome a log line can carry: every other disposition belongs to an artifact
          
          [possible values: no-action]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --spool
          Write to the repository-local outbox instead of the journal, for a caller that cannot reach the journal directory. Cleanup ownership is optional; an unrecorded owner keeps the checkout
