===== arc journal source --help =====
What one recorded session, or one intake item, already produced here: every item key filed against it, its disposition, the artifact when one exists, and the events behind it (journal-source/2). This is what makes a repeated scan cheap — an item already filed, discharged, or judged to need no action answers for itself instead of being distilled again. Name a recording with `--harness` and `--session`, or an intake item with `--intake` and `--item`. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc journal source [OPTIONS] <--harness <HARNESS>|--intake <INTAKE>>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --harness <HARNESS>
          The recording's harness, as the source reference recorded it

      --session <SESSION>
          The native session id, as the source reference recorded it

      --intake <INTAKE>
          The intake's name, as `--source intake=<name>` recorded it

      --item <ITEM>
          The intake item's ID, as `--source item=<id>` recorded it

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --item-key <KEY>
          Report only this item key

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --json
          Emit structured JSON instead of text

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal filed --help =====
Filed artifacts grouped by their writing session, with source references, changes that took them up, and consumption outcomes (arc-journal-filed/1). Direct writes and published transition successors retain their original writers, including imported and spool-promoted artifacts. Successors name the artifact they supersede. A successor must carry its supersedes link and match the complete staged body or have its source's recorded retirement. Unverified intents are omitted; journal doctor reports pending transitions. Each item counts the evidence blocks appended to it. Open actionable items are stale after the threshold of recorded inactivity; active claims and evidence suppress staleness, and a pinned item is never stale. Plain reads record no activity. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Recorded models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded, qualified by the harness when it is established. JSON sessions retain raw models with model_recorded: false when no model is recorded; valid models omit the flag

Usage: arc journal filed [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --session <SESSION>
          Filter by an exact writing or source session id, only when explicitly typed

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --stale <STALE>
          Mark open actionable items stale beyond this many days
          
          [default: 14]

      --json
          Emit structured JSON instead of text

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal position --help =====
Add a position block to an artifact and emit a typed `position` event. `--stance` writes the `Position: for | against | amend` line above the body; without it, the body can provide that line itself. Use `arc journal position <file> --body-file - --question <id> --option <opt>` to argue under one branch of an open question instead of unconditionally. Target availability is checked before reading the body and again under the transition lock

Usage: arc journal position [OPTIONS] --body-file <BODY_FILE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>
          Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --ref <REFERENCE>
          Position or item this answers: a position ID, legacy timestamp, or item slug. Quote the claim answered on the line below the stance

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (the position argument, written verbatim below a tool-computed `### Position` heading)

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --stance <STANCE>
          Stance to write above the body and record on the typed event. Omit this when the body already opens with its own `Position:` line
          
          [possible values: for, against, amend]

      --question <QUESTION>
          Argue under one option of an open question, rather than unconditionally. Pass the question ID; `--option` names the branch

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --option <OPTION>
          The option this position argues under; requires `--question`

      --archived
          Explicitly append to an unresolved discussion in cold storage

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`

===== arc journal evidence --help =====
Append an evidence block to a live artifact of any kind and emit a typed `evidence` event. Use it to add a finding, an option, or a revised section to a todo, feature request, plan, or other live item instead of filing a successor. The block is append-only, states no stance, and never enters a stance tally: argue a discussion with `arc journal position`, and replace a recorded field with `arc journal correct`. A consumed artifact refuses; file a successor. `show` prints the block, `filed` counts it per item, and it is recorded activity, so it keeps an open item from going stale. Structural position headings in the body refuse; fenced and indented examples are prose. Target availability is checked before reading the body and again under the transition lock. Evidence cannot be appended in cold storage; use `journal unarchive` before appending. `append` names the same command for a living document that grows by sections

Usage: arc journal evidence [OPTIONS] --body-file <BODY_FILE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>
          Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (written verbatim below a tool-computed `### Evidence` heading)

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --title <TITLE>
          One line naming the block, written after the heading's writer and time and recorded on the event; a rendered page and its contents use it in place of "Evidence". `journal correct --target <evd-id> --field title` amends it

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal pin --help =====
Pin an artifact as a standing reference and emit a typed `pinned` event. A pin is a fact about an artifact of any kind, consumed or not, so a research note, a finished plan, or a discussion can each be one. `catchup` lists pins first; a pinned artifact never enters `open` or the queues read from it, is never stale in `filed`, is skipped by `archive --consumed`, and refuses `archive` and `relocate` until unpinned. The artifact must be in hot storage. Pinning again with the recorded reason writes nothing; another reason replaces it

Usage: arc journal pin [OPTIONS] <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>
          Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

          One line saying why the artifact stays visible

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`

===== arc journal render --help =====
Render an artifact as a standalone HTML page for reading away from the terminal: its title as corrected, its body, every appended block under its title, and a table of contents, which a narrow screen shows folded when it has more than eight entries. The page has no scripts and sends no referrer; raw HTML shows as code, links keep only http(s), mailto and in-page anchors, and images load only over https. An artifact address in the text links to that artifact's published copy, or to its block on this page. A home-directory path, a path under a directory arc knows is local, a private, shared or link-local address, or a private host name refuses, naming each; `--strip-private` replaces them in the page and `--keep-private` renders them as written. Reads the address forms `show` takes. Several addresses render in one run with `--json`, which reads each journal's event log once; an address that fails is reported in its entry and the rest still render, exiting 16. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc journal render [OPTIONS] <ADDRESS>...

Arguments:
  <ADDRESS>...
          Artifact address: a filename, `<project>::<file>`, `<journal-dir>::<file>`, or a topic for its newest live artifact. Several need `--json`

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --out <FILE>
          Write the output to this file instead of stdout. Rendering to the same path each time lets a publisher that maps paths to copies update the same one

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --strip-private
          Replace each private stretch with a placeholder naming its kind, in the page only, instead of refusing

      --keep-private
          Render private text as written, for a page that stays on its owner's network. The count still shows on stderr and in `--json`

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --fragment
          Only the page's markup (header, contents and text) for a host page that supplies the document, the stylesheet and a footer. Carries no render date, so the same journal gives the same bytes

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --json
          One `arc-journal-render/1` document: the stylesheet once, then per address its journal, file, title, kind, block count, the sha256 and length of the body as read, private stretches found, published URL and HTML, or the error that stopped it

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal question --help =====
Pose a question on a discussion that only a person can settle, and emit a typed `question` event. Placement is the design: `opening` is answered before any position is filed, so everyone argues from the same premise; `closing` is answered once the argument is in. There is no mid-argument placement — a question that blocks halfway makes the caller watch a run they delegated. Argue a closing question on both sides first, with `arc journal position <file> --body-file - --question <id> --option <opt>`. Pose one with `arc journal question <file> --placement opening|closing --option A --option B --body-file -`

Usage: arc journal question [OPTIONS] --placement <PLACEMENT> --option <OPTIONS> --body-file <BODY_FILE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>
          Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --placement <PLACEMENT>
          When it is answered: `opening` settles a premise before any position is filed; `closing` settles a choice the argument raised. There is deliberately no mid-argument placement
          
          [possible values: opening, closing]

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --option <OPTIONS>
          An answer the question offers; repeat for each. Two or more

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (what the question asks, written verbatim below a tool-computed `### Question` heading) Target availability is checked before reading and again under the transition lock

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --settle-by <SETTLE_BY>
          Who may settle it: `person` (the default, what the prose always meant), `anyone`, or `delegate` with --delegate <name>. A question marks what this session should not settle alone, not that no model may answer — an operator who delegated the call names the delegate
          
          [possible values: person, anyone, delegate]

      --delegate <DELEGATE>
          Settle-by delegate, as the name allowed to answer (use with --settle-by delegate)

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal checkpoint --help =====
Append a work checkpoint to an artifact this session has claimed: where the work stands, what comes next, and what a successor should read. Requires a live claim held by this identity. Later checkpoints in one claim are ordinary progress and replace its earlier ones; a correction names what it replaces with `--supersedes`, and the flag repeats so a takeover converges several continuations at once

Usage: arc journal checkpoint [OPTIONS] --body-file <BODY_FILE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>
          Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:

          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --next <NEXT>
          The action a successor should take next

      --gate <GATE>
          The command that says whether the work is good

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --blocker <BLOCKER>
          What the work waits on: `change:<id>`, or free text for anything outside arc

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --supersedes <SUPERSEDES>
          The checkpoint this one corrects; repeat to converge several in one takeover. Every target stays readable

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

===== arc journal questions --help =====
Every open question, across the journal. arc records what agents cannot settle alone; it does not ask. This is the view an agent reads to raise the question through its own harness prompt — file, id, placement, the options to offer, and which branches were already argued — and `answer` is where the reply comes back. `--json` carries the same, for building the prompt without parsing text. Indented code and unrecorded fenced question and answer examples are prose; recorded entry, question, and answer markers terminate an open fence, and a heading repeating an entry ID already met is a quotation. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Recorded models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded, qualified by the harness when it is established. JSON production identities retain raw models with model_recorded: false when missing, placeholders or session ids; valid models omit the flag

Usage: arc journal questions [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

          [env: ARC_SESSION]

===== arc journal answer --help =====
Settle an open question by choosing one of its options. Each answer append carries its own block digest and precise timestamp, so answers to a reopened question remain distinct during revision exchange. Branches that lost stay in the file: a branch argued and not taken is the only record that the alternative was explored rather than never considered. Use `arc journal answer <file> --question <id> --option <chosen> --body-file -`

Usage: arc journal answer [OPTIONS] --question <QUESTION> --body-file <BODY_FILE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>
          Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --question <QUESTION>
          The question being settled

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --option <OPTION>
          The option chosen; must be one the question offered. A typo is refused rather than silently recorded as an answer nobody offered

      --other <ANSWER>
          An answer none of the offered options expressed, in the answerer's own words. Recorded as settling the question and as evidence the option set was inadequate, which `arc journal questions` reports — a menu the answerer had to step outside of was framed wrong, and that is worth knowing before the next one is posed

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (why, written verbatim below a tool-computed `### Answer` heading) Target availability is checked before reading and again under the transition lock

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>

===== arc journal suggest --help =====
Suggest one of a question's options without settling it. A suggestion carries the suggesting identity and its reason, shows beside the question in every question view, and is input to a decision: it never closes the question and never counts toward the stance tally. Use `arc journal suggest <file> --question <id> --option <opt> --body-file -`

          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --option <OPTION>
          The option suggested; must be one the question offered

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>

===== arc journal delivered --help =====
Record that a caller delivered an open question to somebody. arc keeps the fact; it sends nothing, holds no messaging credentials, and infers no delivery from prose. Without it a question queue cannot separate prompting work still to do from an answer merely pending, so every question view reports `delivery: unknown|unasked|delivered|answered`. Deliveries accumulate — asking twice records twice and erases nothing — and a question waiting on one named delegate accepts only that delegate. Use `arc journal delivered <file> --question <id> --to person|anyone|delegate:<name> [--handle <opaque>]`

Usage: arc journal delivered [OPTIONS] --question <QUESTION> --to <RECIPIENT> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>
          Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --question <QUESTION>
          The question that was delivered

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --to <RECIPIENT>
          Who was asked: `person`, `anyone`, or `delegate:<name>`

      --handle <OPAQUE>
          Whatever opaque identifier the delivery system handed back — a thread id, a ticket, a message URL. Recorded verbatim so the delivery can be found again outside arc, and never resolved

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

===== arc journal correct --help =====
Replace one field of one recorded entry without rewriting it. The entry stays in the artifact as it was filed and a `### Correction` block records the replacement; derived views read the corrected value. `--target` names the entry: `artifact`, a position ID, an evidence ID, a question ID, or `answer:<question id>`. A consumed artifact accepts corrections — it is closed to new work, but a wrong record stays wrong

Usage: arc journal correct [OPTIONS] --target <TARGET> --field <FIELD> --value <VALUE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>
          Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --target <TARGET>
          The entry to correct: `artifact`, a position ID, an evidence ID, a question ID, or `answer:<question id>`

      --field <FIELD>
          The field to replace. Which fields exist depends on the target: `title` and, on a plan, `planners` on the artifact; `stance`, `option`, `ref`, `actor`, and `model` on a position; `option`, `actor`, and `model` on an answer; `actor` and `model` on a question; `title`, `actor`, and `model` on an evidence block. A corrected actor, model or planner is held to what a write records: a placeholder or a session id model refuses. A model is checked against the target entry's session and the invoking session; an answer target uses its latest settlement, and a correction of it applies to the settlement it was made against, not to one that replaced it. Headers and recorded planner coordinates undergo the same semantic validation before credit is granted. A planner correction that fails the same checks when read reads as `planner_status: malformed` in `show --json`
          
          [possible values: stance, option, actor, model, ref, title, planners]

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --value <VALUE>
          What the field should have said

      --note <NOTE>
          Why the recorded value was wrong

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal retract --help =====
Withdraw a recorded entry while leaving it visible. A retracted position leaves the stance tally and the branches argued under a question; a retracted answer reopens the question it settled, and answering it again settles it with the new answer, which a later retraction can withdraw in turn. Retracted evidence is marked withdrawn in `show --json`. The block stays in the artifact, because an argument withdrawn is a different record from an argument never made. A consumed artifact accepts retractions

Usage: arc journal retract [OPTIONS] --target <TARGET> --body-file <BODY_FILE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>
          Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --target <TARGET>
          The entry to withdraw: a position ID, an evidence ID, or `answer:<question id>`

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (why it is withdrawn, written verbatim below a tool-computed `### Retraction` heading) Target availability is checked before reading and again under the transition lock

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

===== arc journal reattribute --help =====
Repair the recorded authorship of one artifact's creation event, in place. A maintenance operation the operator selects: ordinary journal mutation stays append-only and no correction block is appended. Only the named fields and dependent model evidence of the one creation (`note`) event change; every other record keeps its bytes, and the previous log is kept as `events.jsonl.bak`. Repair and every event append share an event-write lock across the read and replacement. Other event kinds are refused because their identity carries contribution, decision, or continuation that provenance repair must not rewrite, and so is a creation event recording an on-behalf-of subject. `--dry-run` names the exact record and every other record carrying the replaced identity, and writes nothing. Legacy placeholder harnesses and sessions read as absent and do not identify sibling references during repair. A model replacement is a flag declaration compared with the retained observation; changing harness or session clears that observation because it belongs to the replaced session. A pending exchange refuses repair; finish it first with `arc journal import <bundle>`, or `arc journal --global import <bundle>` for the global journal

Usage: arc journal reattribute [OPTIONS] <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>
          Artifact filename this project's journal or its cold archive holds (a name, not a path). A file only another known journal holds, or its `<journal-dir>::<file>` reference, is refused naming that journal and its project, where the repair runs

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --event <EVENT>
          The event kind to repair; only the creation event (`note`) is supported and anything else is refused by name
          
          [default: note]

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --set-actor <ACTOR>
          Replacement actor; absent leaves the recorded actor alone. Every replacement is held to what a write records: a placeholder, or a session id as the model, refuses, under --dry-run too

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --set-harness <HARNESS>
          Replacement harness; absent leaves the recorded harness alone

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --set-session <SESSION>
          Replacement session; absent leaves the recorded session alone. The effective model must not equal this session. Unnamed model fields otherwise keep their historical values

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --set-model <MODEL>
          Replacement model; absent leaves the recorded model alone. A model equal to the effective recorded session refuses; a supplied --set-session selects that session for validation

      --dry-run
          Name the record and the other records carrying the replaced identity, then write nothing

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal show --help =====
Print one artifact's raw Markdown body, or one entry inside it, to stdout (read-only)

The argument is an address, never a path:

<file>                   an artifact in this project's journal <entry-id>               one entry, wherever this journal records it <project>::<address>     either, in another project's journal, by registered slug or label <journal-dir>::<address> either, in the journal at that absolute directory

An entry ID is unique within its journal, so it is the whole address of an entry. `<file>#<entry-id>` also reads it, and is refused unless that artifact records the entry.

Any other argument is a topic, which shows its newest live artifact and names it on stderr. An entry ID is `pos-`, `evd-`, `q-`, `cp-`, `cor-` or `ret-` followed by its ULID, and is never read as a topic. Each journal is read the same way: the hot journal first, then its cold archive, so an archived artifact reads as a live one does. `arc journal discussion <file.md>` reads an artifact's stances and open questions.

An entry prints the sections its ID heads, verbatim, from the heading to the next entry heading; a question's answers follow it. stderr names the artifact, the cold archive when it lives there, each corrected field in force and any retraction, so stdout is the text a reader quotes. A retracted entry still prints. A bare ID this journal does not record is refused naming each other known journal that does; an ID recorded on a different artifact than the one named is refused naming its artifact. A `<project>::` qualifier is a read convenience: recorded references name the journal directory. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. JSON artifact and entry recorded_by objects retain the raw model with model_recorded: false when missing, a placeholder or a session id; valid models omit the flag.

Usage: arc journal show [OPTIONS] <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>
          An artifact or entry address, or a topic

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --json
          Emit the versioned artifact metadata projection: the body, the planners credited with `planner_status` and its sources, and `planner_correction_event`, the amendment ID of the planner correction in force. Positions and evidence carry their ID, heading, body, recorded identity, corrected fields, and retraction status; unmatched blocks have null recorded identity and detached events have null heading and body. Indented code and unrecorded fenced entry examples are prose; recorded position, evidence, question, and answer markers terminate an open fence, and a heading repeating an entry ID already met is a quotation. Entry bodies keep their indentation, and a position's stance is the line the discussion tally reads. Corrections and retractions list typed amendment events in log order. A correction that fails validation reads `planner_status: malformed` and credits no planner.
          
          An entry address emits `arc-journal-entry/1` instead: `address` in its canonical `<entry-id>` spelling under the qualifier given, `journal` (hot directory), `file`, `storage` (`hot` or `cold`), `id`, `kind`, `text` (what stdout prints), `recorded_by`, `corrected` (field and value pairs in force), `retracted`, `retraction_note`, and `entry`: for a position or evidence, the object `arc-journal-artifact/3` lists for it; otherwise null.

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal latest --help =====
Resolve the newest artifact filed under one topic and print it, so a workflow that appends continuations under a stable topic can read its own tail back without reproducing arc's ordering rules. The topic must match exactly; a similarly prefixed topic never matches. The hot journal wins over the cold archive whenever both hold a match. Inside one timestamp second a transition's terminal successor is preferred, and otherwise the order the event log recorded the artifacts in decides; a numeric filename suffix is never an ordering of its own. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc journal latest [OPTIONS] <TOPIC>

===== arc journal discussion --help =====
Derived summary of a discussion: stance tally, participants, replies, age, and resolution with a resolver-participation flag (read-only). The tally counts `Position: for | against | amend` lines inside `### Position` blocks, and flags blocks that state no stance. Indented code and unrecorded fenced entry examples are prose; recorded position, evidence, question, and answer markers terminate an open fence, and a heading repeating an entry ID already met is a quotation. Reports each open question with the positions argued under every option, so a branch nobody explored is visible before the question is answered. Resolve a discussion with `journal consume --outcome done --decision <file>` Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Recorded models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded, qualified by the harness when it is established. JSON production identities retain raw models with model_recorded: false when missing, placeholders or session ids; valid models omit the flag

===== arc journal transition --help =====
Change a live artifact's workflow kind without rewriting history: a typed successor is written, linked back with `supersedes`, and the source is retired as superseded. The safe manual sequence, performed as one guarded operation rather than hand-composed by every caller. Promotion to a code change stays with `begin --from-journal`, which is implementation authorization and never a kind conversion. The complete body is staged durably before the relation is recorded and published exclusively. Reusing a matching preparation syncs its file and directory before recording the relation. Retry uses those staged bytes. Plan targets require valid inherited planner metadata or --no-planner. A legacy successor requires verification against the original body and title; a mismatch preserves the artifact and refuses retirement. A successor whose publication evidence is incomplete is a pending transition and is refused as a source; retry the transition that created it

Usage: arc journal transition [OPTIONS] --to <TO> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>
          Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --to <TO>
          The successor's kind
          
          [possible values: note, memory, plan, handoff, review, conclusion, todo, later, discussion, decision, incident, feature-request]

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Body source for the successor: a file path, or '-' for stdin. Omitted, the successor carries the source body verbatim under a heading naming what changed

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --title <TITLE>
          Title for the `# <title>` heading; a body opening on prose is otherwise headed from the topic slug

      --reason <REASON>
          Why the kind is changing, recorded in the transition event

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --dry-run
          Print the successor and the lifecycle effects without writing anything

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --acknowledge-claim <ACKNOWLEDGE_CLAIM>
          Acknowledge one open claim on the source artifact by ID (repeatable). A kind change retires the source, ending work in progress on it

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --planned-by <PLANNED_BY>
          Assert an additional material planner on a plan successor (repeatable): a one-line JSON object in the shape `arc journal plan --help` states for --planned-by. A planner the successor would record, asserted or inherited, that carries a placeholder or a session id model refuses, under --dry-run too; correct an inherited one with `arc journal correct <file> --target artifact --field planners`, or pass --no-planner

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --no-planner
          Preserve unknown authorship on a plan successor

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal promote --help =====
Promote a live project memory into the operator journal. Requires --global. Both journals record the relation; the project memory is consumed as superseded and its historical body is retained. Both journals must have settled exchanges. An interrupted promotion is completed before subsequent writes or by retrying the same command. Complete staged bodies publish exclusively; success requires body and event replay verification. Changed bodies or a different global journal refuse recovery. Open claims require release-claim. Cleanup keeps global recovery discoverable until the source intent is durably removed. A committed relation with a remaining source intent is verified in hot or archived storage and needs only metadata cleanup

Usage: arc journal promote [OPTIONS] <FILENAME>

===== arc journal relocate --help =====
Move artifacts out of this journal into a directory arc does not read, leaving a tombstone.

Each body is copied into the destination under its own filename, verified against its digest, and then removed from the hot directory, the cold archive, and any prepared transition copy, so `show`, `inventory --archived`, catchup, and every workspace view stop reaching it. A `relocated` event records the file, the destination path, the reason, and the body digest. The event log keeps what earlier events recorded about the artifact, such as its topic and title.

Readers treat the tombstone as the artifact's fate. `show` and writes name the destination, `filed` reports the fate `relocated`, and supersedes and successor links to the artifact are marked relocated. Its open claims end, its storage moves and transitions read as settled, and `journal doctor` reports a tombstone whose body is still present. Exports refuse to carry a relocated artifact, and imports hold back a bundled copy of one, with what references it, so a peer replica cannot restore it. A peer's own copy is relocated there.

The destination must lie outside arc's home and every known journal; it is created with mode 0700 when missing, and copies are written with mode 0600. Every named file is checked before anything moves. Refuses a destination that already holds the filename with a different body, and an artifact with an open claim, an unfinished transition or storage move, or unanswered questions, unless `--acknowledge-claim`, `--acknowledge-transition`, or `--drop-questions` accepts it, and a pinned artifact until `journal unpin`. Rerunning an interrupted relocation finishes it.

Usage: arc journal relocate [OPTIONS] --to <DIR> --reason <REASON> <FILES>...

Arguments:
  <FILES>...
          Artifact filenames inside the journal dir (names, not paths)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --to <DIR>
          Destination directory outside arc

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --reason <REASON>
          Why the artifacts leave the journal, recorded on each tombstone

      --acknowledge-claim <ID>
          End this open claim with the relocation; repeat for each

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --acknowledge-transition
          Relocate an artifact whose transition or storage move is unfinished, leaving it unfinished

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --drop-questions
          Relocate an artifact with unanswered questions, leaving them undecided

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --dry-run
          Check every artifact and print what would move; write nothing

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal redact --help =====
Replace passages in one artifact's body in place, hot or archived.

`--replace OLD --with NEW` pairs apply in order, each to every occurrence, and each must match; `--body-file` supplies the whole new body instead. The rewrite keeps every appended entry's heading line, in order, and a `supersedes:` first line unchanged. A `redacted` event records the reason, the body digests before and after, and the before and after digests of each changed section (the base text, or an appended entry by its identity). It never records the removed text, and refusals do not repeat it.

`journal export` carries the redacted body with its event, and `journal import` takes a recorded redaction's result as the section's current revision on both sides of a merge, so a bundle written before the redaction never restores what it removed. Refuses a relocated artifact, an unfinished storage move, and an unfinished transition whose prepared copy would keep the old text. Rerunning an interrupted redaction finishes it.

Usage: arc journal redact [OPTIONS] --reason <REASON> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>
          Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --replace <OLD>
          Text to replace; pair each with --with

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --with <NEW>
          Replacement for the --replace at the same position

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          The whole new body: a file path, or '-' for stdin

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --reason <REASON>
          Why the text is removed, recorded on the redaction

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --dry-run
          Check the rewrite and print what would change; write nothing

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal export --help =====
Export selected journal artifacts as a versioned bundle for another replica. --global exports the operator journal without Git pairing. The bundle carries each artifact body, every log record written about it as written, including records this build cannot read and records state replay sets aside, its body digest, the exporting replica, and every artifact the selection references by filename. A relocated artifact cannot travel, so naming or referencing one refuses the export. Exports arc-journal-bundle/2. Checksums retain event field order and opaque payload tokens, excluding formatting whitespace. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc journal export [OPTIONS] --output <OUTPUT> <FILES>...

Arguments:
  <FILES>...
          Artifact filenames inside the journal dir (names, not paths); the dependency closure of what they reference travels with them

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --output <OUTPUT>
          Output file ('-' for stdout)

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal import --help =====
Import arc-journal-bundle/2 or /1 from another paired replica. --global accepts only operator journal bundles, without Git pairing. Prefix revisions retain the longer body. Concurrent appended sections merge by event identity, ordered by timestamp then ID. Answers to a reopened question retain distinct append identities. Edits to the base or a shared block refuse the entire bundle, naming both digests and the first differing block, unless a recorded redaction relates the two revisions: the redacted one is kept on either side. Storage tiers must agree. A bundled copy of an artifact this journal relocated is held back with what references it. Checksums retain event field order and opaque payload tokens, excluding formatting whitespace. Artifacts carrying unsupported events are held back as a unit, reported with the event kind and schema; their dependent artifacts are held back too. Admitted artifacts retain their provenance. Receipts cover only admitted artifacts, so held-back artifacts are retried on every import. Deduplication includes understood physical log records rejected by state replay. Events replay at their receiving append positions. A completely admitted bundle changes nothing on a repeated import. Interrupted writes retain a prepared transaction; an import completes it before comparing revisions. Every journal write, including provenance repair and spool promotion, refuses a pending import and names its bundle digest and the remedy: `arc journal import <bundle>`, or `arc journal --global import <bundle>` when the global journal holds it. Reads stay allowed. Recovery refuses to overwrite unexpected body or event-log changes. --dry-run requires a completed exchange

Usage: arc journal import [OPTIONS] <INPUT>

Arguments:
  <INPUT>
          Input file ('-' for stdin)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --dry-run
          Validate and report without writing artifacts, events, or a receipt

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc replica provisional resolve --help =====
Resolve late delivery against this change's accepting integration.

Requires current authority. Forge obligations require asserted integration with the exact external reference.

Usage: arc replica provisional resolve [OPTIONS] --integration <INTEGRATION> --because <BECAUSE> <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>
          Change whose provisional delivery is being reconciled

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --integration <INTEGRATION>
          Accepting change-integrated or integration-asserted event ID

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --provisional <PROVISIONAL>
          Obligation event IDs; omitted, resolve every matching outstanding obligation

      --because <BECAUSE>
          Reason for explicit reconciliation; never silently disposes of a refusal

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

===== arc journal lane open --help =====
Open or replace an advisory work lane

A lane's owner is the declared harness and session together, so both must be given. Opening replaces only that owner's own previous lane; another harness presenting the same session string keeps its lane. Liveness follows the owner's latest recorded activity by timestamp, whatever order records reached the log in; a spooled write promoted late never lowers it. Ownership checks and lane changes share the journal transition lock.

Usage: arc journal lane open [OPTIONS] <TOPIC>

Arguments:
  <TOPIC>
          Kebab-case topic slug naming the lane

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --scope <SCOPE>
          Journal topics this lane covers, so `journal open` can annotate the items another session is already holding

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --ttl <TTL>
          How long the lane stays live without activity (e.g. 30m, 2h)
          
          [default: 2h]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --status <STATUS>
          What the lane is currently doing, shown beside it

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --status-file <STATUS_FILE>
          Read the lane status from a file ('-' for stdin)

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Represented subject; an empty declared model or effort refuses; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change selection; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Home-directory prefix; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal lane renew --help =====
Renew a lane owned by this harness and session. Recorded renewal requires both owner coordinates and compares them verbatim. Lane existence and ownership are checked before status input and again under the transition lock; opening and closure share that lock

Usage: arc journal lane renew [OPTIONS] <TOPIC>

Arguments:

          The lane to renew

Options:

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --status <STATUS>
          Replace what the lane is currently doing

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

===== arc journal lane close --help =====
Close a lane

A live lane closes only for the owning harness and session; a stale one closes for anyone with `--outcome expired`. Recorded closure requires both owner coordinates; foreign expiry is checked at the event timestamp. Ownership checks and lane changes share the journal transition lock.

Usage: arc journal lane close [OPTIONS] <TOPIC>

Arguments:
  <TOPIC>
          The lane to close

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity; see `arc guide identity-options`

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --outcome <OUTCOME>
          How the lane ended
          
          [default: done]
          [possible values: done, handoff, abandoned, expired]

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Acting harness; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS]

      --note <NOTE>
          Free-text detail recorded with the closure

      --session <SESSION>
          Native acting session; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private acting-session link; see `arc guide identity-options`
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declared model and effort; see `arc guide identity-options`
